<result>Lane C is done: draft PR 1982 is open and every CI check passed. The drain now re-resolves the scope root and signs above what the endpoints hold, instead of above its own cache.

- **Branch:** `fix/1959-publish-above-observed-sequence`
- **PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1982 (draft, body ends with `Closes #1959.`, no attribution lines)
- **Head SHA:** `4c016f7e1a132dd6fd617a83436f00652e78baf2`
- **CI:** all required checks passed, including Engine simulation tests, Workspace tests, Fmt + clippy, Core KATs, Tracker Refs, Web E2E Smoke and Contract Suite. The only skipped jobs are path-gated.

**Files changed**
- `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs`
- `crates/engine/src/net/publish.rs`
- `crates/engine/tests/write_plane.rs`
- `crates/engine/tests/net.rs`

**What the fix does**
- **Before the sign:** the new `reresolve_scope_root` runs just before the drain signs a scope root. It fetches from every endpoint without the cache (`resolve_gated`), and the freshest record passes the adoption gate.
- **Gate refusal:** the drain reports a trust violation and halts with `Halt::RecordRefused`. It never signs over that record.
- **Newer record:** if the record is above the base the pass built on, it is a lost race. The adopt updates the cache and the floor, the attempt halts with `Halt::Attempt`, and the op stays at the head of the queue. The next pass rebases onto that record and signs above it.
- **Otherwise:** the signature is floor + 1, and the floor is now at least the observed sequence.
- **Read-back:** another record at our own sequence is now `LostRace`, not `Unconfirmed`. I removed the stale "rebase is a later slice" comment.

**Design choices the issue left open**
- **Charging:** a lost race found before the sign charges `Halt::Attempt`, the same halt the existing read-back lost race uses. The attempt budget of 5 still limits a name that another device keeps taking. I added this case to the `Halt::Attempt` doc.
- **Pass start:** `open_pass` still reads the cache. The tick's root resolve runs just before the drain, so a fresh resolve at pass start would add a network read and protect nothing more.
- **No `min_current_sequence`:** the re-resolve does not pass it. The drain continues only when the observed sequence is equal to the floor or when nothing was observed, so floor + 1 is already strictly above it.
- **Scope:** only scope-root publishes get the re-resolve, as the brief asked. Child loads already fetch fresh from the endpoints before a publish.

**Tests (all in the Engine simulation tests gate)**
- `a_sibling_root_that_lands_mid_pass_is_rebased_on_and_signed_above`: device B reads sequence N, device A lands N+1 during B's pass, and B signs nothing at N+1. The next pass signs N+2 and keeps both creates. This test fails without the fix.
- `a_sibling_root_at_the_same_sequence_is_a_lost_race_the_next_pass_heals`: the two endpoints hold different records at one sequence, the op stays at the head, and the next pass puts N+2 on every endpoint. It also passes without the fix, because the drain already halted `Unconfirmed` and `LostRace` the same way. It covers the full flow.
- `a_root_the_gate_refuses_at_the_pre_signature_re_resolve_is_a_trust_violation`: one abuse event, nothing signed, the op stays queued. This test fails without the fix.
- `net.rs` `publish_confirm_reads_a_sibling_at_our_sequence_as_a_lost_race`: pins `LostRace { published_sequence: 2, observed_sequence: 2 }`.

**Left out**
- **API change:** none. The publish path sends records to the routing endpoints with no expected-sequence parameter.
- **Blueprint:** `blueprint/engine.md` line 928 still says the two-device publish fix "is not landed". I did not change it. The expired-link sweep publishes through the rotation paths in `net/rotation.rs`, which this PR does not change. Those paths do resolve before they build and now also get the tie-is-`LostRace` read-back, but they have no re-resolve just before the sign. Decide whether that sentence changes.
- **Fork residual:** if each device's read-back sees its own record, the fork stays until the next publish at that name. The read-back only looks at the freshest record, with the first endpoint winning a tie.
- **Review gates:** I did not run `/simplify`, `/security-review` or `/crypto-privacy-review`. The brief said a separate reviewer sends findings. The worktree and branch are intact.

**Local runs**
- Passed: `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine`, `cargo test --workspace --exclude fuser`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, clippy with `-D warnings`, the wasm32 check, and `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`.
- Not run: `pnpm lint` and `pnpm typecheck`. There is no TypeScript change, and the CI Lint job passed.
- `fuser`'s `mnt::test::mount_unmount` fails on this Mac because it needs a real FUSE mount. CI skips that test.</result>

<result>## Lane B report: #1976, the mailbox DELETE reports if it removed the item

The draft PR is open and all CI checks pass (36). I did not merge it, mark it ready, or request a review.

- **Branch:** `feat/1976-mailbox-delete-reports-removed`
- **PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1981 (draft, title `feat(api): report whether the mailbox delete removed the item`)
- **Head SHA:** `fe39852a9a35287e42b10d1121f45fc6f468e7b1`
- **Gates that pass:** API Unit tests, API Integration tests (real Postgres), Contract Suite, Rust Workspace tests, Tracker Refs.

### CI history
- **First push:** the new HTTP itest for concurrent DELETEs failed with `ECONNRESET`. The test was wrong, not the API. Concurrent supertest requests share one server, and the first request that ends closes it. I moved the race test into `mailbox.service.itest.ts`, where it calls the service directly against Postgres. It passed three runs out of three on my machine.
- **Second push:** "Rust / Workspace tests (Linux)" failed in `cipherbox-desktop-seams` because the runner's system keyring did not answer. This PR does not touch that crate. I ran the failed jobs again once and they passed.

### Changed files
- `apps/api/src/mailbox/services/mailbox.service.ts`: `ack` returns `{ removed }`, taken from the row count of the delete.
- `apps/api/src/mailbox/dto/mailbox.dto.ts`: `AckResponseDto.removed` replaces `success`.
- `apps/api/src/mailbox/mailbox.controller.ts`: OpenAPI text for the ack answer, the idempotency key lifetime, and the pending cap of 1000.
- `apps/api/openapi.json`: regenerated with `pnpm openapi:generate`.
- `apps/api/src/mailbox/services/mailbox.service.test.ts`, `mailbox.service.itest.ts`, `apps/api/src/mailbox/mailbox.http.itest.ts`: the new tests.
- `blueprint/api.md`: the Mailbox Ack bullet now says a missing or foreign id answers "not removed", and states the cap of 1000 items. A replay of a pending item still gets an answer when the mailbox is full.
- `crates/engine/src/api/client.rs`, `crates/engine/src/api/types.rs`: `mailbox_ack` returns `Result&lt;bool, ApiError&gt;` and decodes `MailboxAckWire { removed }`. Includes a new unit test.
- `crates/engine/src/api/mailbox.rs`: the seam implementation discards the flag.
- `crates/engine/src/testkit/fakes/mailbox.rs`: the fake answers with the new body shape and returns the real removal flag.
- `crates/engine/tests/grants_mailbox.rs`, `crates/load/src/stub_http.rs`: updated for the new body shape.
- `crates/contract/tests/contract.rs`: the round-trip test checks both ack answers. The new test `mailbox_ack_reports_removal_and_releases_the_idempotency_key` covers a foreign ack, a reused key while the item is live, and a reused key after the ack.

### Design choices the issue left open
- **No lock added.** Postgres row locking already makes the answer exclusive: a second DELETE of the same row waits, then deletes nothing. No advisory lock is necessary. The service itest proves this: exactly one of 8 concurrent acks gets `removed: true`.
- **Idempotency code unchanged.** The existing post code already returns the live item for a reused key and creates a new item after the ack. I only added tests.
- **Malformed ids.** A malformed (non-uuid) id now answers `removed: false`, with no query to the database.
- **Old response shape.** `success` is removed, not kept next to `removed` (v2 is greenfield). If a response has no `removed` field, the engine client returns a decode error, so it can never read as a removal.
- **The `Mailbox` seam still returns `()`.** Only `ApiClient::mailbox_ack` gives the flag to its caller. Per the brief, the conversion slice changes the seam when it uses the flag.

### Left out, and why
- I did not run the `/simplify`, `/security-review` and `/code-review` skills, because the common brief gives review to a separate reviewer. No crypto code changed.
- `gh pr create` would not accept a title with parentheses in this sandbox. I set the scoped title afterwards with `gh api PATCH`.
- For a local contract run, I started the API on port 3300 against a temporary database, and all 4 mailbox contract tests passed. I then removed that database and stopped the API with `pkill -f "node dist/main.js"`. That pattern also matches any other API process started the same way. Before I started, nothing listened on ports 3000, 3100 or 3200. The Docker stack continues to run.
- Local `cargo test --workspace` failed only in the vendored `fuser` test `mount_unmount`, which is a macOS mount problem. The engine and load crates pass in full with `--no-fail-fast`.</result>

<result>The draft PR for Lane A is open and all its CI checks pass. I did not merge it, mark it ready, or request any review.

- **Branch:** `feat/1963-core-link-fields`
- **PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1983 (draft, closes #1964, #1965 and #1966)
- **Head:** `ae60628392c00fec9095090b9b69270c038ba2be`
- **CI:** every check passes, including Core KATs (native + WASM), Engine simulation tests, Client Browser Suite, Contract Suite and Web E2E Smoke. Desktop Build was skipped by its path filter. The CodeRabbit status check reports pass.

**Local checks:** these pass locally too:
- `cargo fmt --all --check` and clippy with `-D warnings`, on the host and on wasm32.
- The wasm32 check, the core, engine and workspace tests, and the new release-build test.
- `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`.
- The client unit, typecheck and browser suites, which a sub-agent ran.

**Files changed:**
- Core: `crates/core/src/error.rs`, `seal/grant.rs`, `seal/write_body.rs`, `seal/mod.rs`, `suite/contact.rs`.
- Core KATs: `crates/core/examples/kat_gen.rs`, `kat/manifest.json`, the grant vector files, and two new files: `vectors/contact/fingerprint.json` and `vectors/grant/recipient_binding_reject.json`.
- Core tests: `crates/core/tests/kat_manifest.rs` and the new `crates/core/tests/encode_refusals.rs`.
- Engine: `grants/{contact,invite,ledger,mod}.rs`, `lib.rs`, `net/rotation.rs`, `rotation/{reseal,trigger}.rs`, `rotation/trigger/tests.rs`, `tests/invite_claims.rs`.
- WASM: `crates/wasm/src/lib.rs`, which gets the free function `identityFingerprint`.
- Client: `packages/client` gets a new facade read, `identityFingerprint`. The files are `facade.ts`, `worker/{protocol,engineHost,engineWasm}.ts`, `testkit.ts` and their unit tests. The browser test is in `test/browser/{boundary.worker,engine.spec}.ts`.
- CI: `.github/workflows/ci-rust.yml`. No CI job ran a release-build test, so I added a step to the Core KATs job that runs `encode_refusals` under `--release`.

**Design choices the issues left open:**
- **Wire names:**
  - On the commitment entry: `kind`, `deadline`, `conversionPermission`, `admissionCap`.
  - On the ledger row: `viaLink`, `granteeName`, `nameSource`.
  - The blueprint names no wire keys, so I did not change it.
- **Personal kind:** a personal entry has one wire form, which is no `kind` key. The codec refuses an explicit `"personal"` and any other unknown kind.
- **Link fields on a personal entry:** the codec refuses all three link fields on a personal entry, not only the deadline. CONTEXT.md says they belong only on a link entry.
- **Zero values:** a zero deadline is refused with the existing check `invalid-expiry`, which moves from the row to the entry. A cap of zero is accepted.
- **New checks:** `invalid-entry-kind`, `link-field-on-personal-entry`, `link-permission-not-read`, `invalid-grantee-name`, `invalid-name-source`. All are malformed-class.
- **Grantee name:** the codec refuses a name that is empty, longer than 255 bytes, or holds a control character. The only constructor, `GranteeName::new`, runs the same check, so the encoder cannot produce a bad name. A name without its source flag, or a flag without a name, is refused as `missing-field`.
- **Retired `expiresAt`:** a row that still carries the key keeps it as a preserved unknown field. Its bytes and signature do not change. The old accept vector for that row is unchanged. The row zero-expiry reject vector is removed, because the key is no longer a field.
- **Fingerprint:** the first 80 bits of `BLAKE3("cipherbox/v2/identity-fingerprint" || sec1)`, shown as lowercase hex in five groups of four, for example `e686 bdd6 b44e 05c4 4db0`. The engine exports it as `fingerprint_identity_key` and the WASM crate exports it as `identityFingerprint`. The facade read is blocked before sign-in, the same as other reads.
- **New KAT family:** a `recipientBindingReject` family pins the verify failure when a signed row field is removed or changed.

**Engine changes (kept to the minimum):**
- `entry_is_live` and the published-deadline check in conversion are removed. The owner-local invite record still holds the deadline that conversion checks.
- The reseal now carries `via_link` and `grantee_name` unchanged, so that a named row keeps a valid signature.
- The tests that asserted the row deadline are removed or rewritten.

**Left out, and why:**
- The write-wave re-mint does not carry the new link fields on the commitment entry or on the row. The brief assigns that re-map to a later slice. At present no code creates a link entry or a named row.
- The engine invite mint still commits a link as a personal entry, at the link permission. If it set the link kind now, the new codec would refuse every write link. Batch B changes the mint.
- The store kind `0x03` (`invite-records`) is still in the manifest registry. I did not change it, because the store retires in a later slice.
- I did not run the `/simplify`, `/security-review` and `/crypto-privacy-review` passes. The brief leaves reviews to a separate reviewer.

The worktree and the branch are unchanged for the reviewer.</result>

<result># Review of PR #1981 — feat(api): report whether the mailbox delete removed the item

Head: fe39852a9. Diff: `git diff main...HEAD`, 15 files. Scratchpad: `~/.claude/scratch/w1963/B/review/` (holds `pr.diff`, `simplify.patch`; the harness refused a findings file, so the report is this message). CI: all green; the Contract Suite job ran the new test against the live stack (log shows `... ok`, no SKIP). Local: API unit 29/29, API itests 23/23 on Docker Postgres (8-way ack race passes), engine `api::` 59/59, `grants_mailbox` pass, `cargo fmt --all --check` clean, clippy clean on engine/load/contract, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` clean, API typecheck clean. Tree restored clean after the gates.

**Verdict: no blocker, no should-fix. Eight nits. Mergeable as is.**

## Extra checks from the brief (all CONFIRMED)

- The API inspects nothing inside a sealed item: the ack path is `UUID_RE.test` then one `delete({ id, recipientPublicKey })`; no select; the blob column is never read (`mailbox.service.ts:249-257`).
- Foreign id and missing id both answer `removed: false`: the where-clause binds `recipientPublicKey` from the verified JWT (`jwt-auth.guard.ts:28-33`, `mailbox.controller.ts:93`); both run the same parameterised query and get `affected = 0`. Proven in `mailbox.http.itest.ts` (foreign + missing) and `contract.rs:1273-1275` (foreign, live stack).
- The malformed-id short-circuit does not skip auth: `@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard)` is at class level; guards run before the handler; the regex sits inside the service. An unauthenticated caller gets 401 first.
- Key lifetime and pending cap 1000 are stated in `blueprint/api.md:280-285` and in `openapi.json` (post `description`, 409 text). The blueprint already carried the lifetime on `main`; the diff adds it to OpenAPI.
- The engine client fails closed when `removed` is absent: `MailboxAckWire { removed: bool }` has no serde default; `decode` returns `ApiError::Decode`; non-2xx goes through `ok_or_err` first. Test `the_ack_reports_whether_this_call_removed_the_item` feeds `{ success: true }` and asserts `Decode`.
- Contract test in the Contract Suite gate: `ci.yml:316`, `cargo test -p cipherbox-contract` at line 427.
- AGENTS.md: no tracker refs; ADR citations only; no absence-justifying comments; no secret bytes in assertions; tests assert behaviour; camelCase field. Encode/decode symmetry and TrustViolation rules do not apply (no codec, no gate).
- PR hygiene: title has parentheses only in the scope; body ends with bare `Closes #1976.`; no attribution in body or commits; no closing keyword in commits.

## Findings

1. **nit** — `apps/api/src/mailbox/services/mailbox.service.ts:240-248`. The `ack` doc states exclusivity twice and carries the engine's rationale ("the owner engine converts a claim only on true"), which belongs to the engine and is not wired yet. Failure: the sentence goes stale in this file when the engine policy lands elsewhere. Fix (`simplify.patch`):

```diff
    * persist crypto-bearing rows past their consumer). `removed` is true only
-   * for the one call whose delete took the row: a gone, foreign, or malformed
-   * id answers false, with no side effect. Postgres row locking makes the
-   * answer exclusive, because a concurrent delete of the same row waits and
-   * then affects nothing. The owner engine converts a claim only on true
-   * (ADR 0023 D5).
+   * for the one call whose delete took the row: a concurrent delete waits on
+   * the row lock and then affects nothing (blueprint/api.md, Mailbox).
```

2. **nit** — `blueprint/api.md:281-282`. The blueprint says "A missing or foreign id answers 'not removed'"; OpenAPI and the DTO also name a malformed id. Failure: normative spec and wire doc disagree on one case. Fix: `A missing, foreign, or malformed id answers "not removed".`

3. **nit** — `mailbox.controller.ts:53-56`. The 409 text hardcodes `1000` and `90-day`; the code has `positiveIntConfig(..., 1000)` at `:81` and `TTL_MS` at `:30`. Failure: a default change leaves the contract stale with no gate noticing (the 413 "8 KiB" already does this). Optional fix: export `DEFAULT_PENDING_CAP` and a TTL-days constant and build the string from them.

4. **nit** — `mailbox.controller.ts:86-88` and `dto/mailbox.dto.ts:67-70`. The ack operation `description` and the `removed` property `description` say the same thing, so `openapi.json` states it twice. Fix: keep the property text; cut or drop the operation text.

5. **nit** — `mailbox.service.itest.ts:133`. The ack-race test sits under `describe('MailboxService pending-cap concurrency (real Postgres)')`. Fix: its own `describe('MailboxService ack exclusivity (real Postgres)')`.

6. **nit** — `mailbox.http.itest.ts:25`. Header line runs ~104 columns; neighbours wrap at 80. Fix: rewrap.

7. **nit** (reuse) — `mailbox.http.itest.ts:147-164`. `postOne`/`ack` are scoped to `describe('ack answer')` while the lifecycle test (`:106`) and ownership test (`:316`) hand-write the same delete chain. Fix: hoist both helpers to the top-level `describe` and use them in all three places.

8. **nit, outside the diff** — `mailbox.service.ts:123` (post fast path). The replay lookup does not filter by the 90-day TTL, so a reused key can return an expired row not yet purged; the next poll deletes it and the re-post is lost. Pre-existing; D6 re-posts stop at the invite deadline, so unlikely. Later-slice fix: `receivedAt: MoreThanOrEqual(cutoff)` on both replay `findOne` calls.

Not raised: changing the `Mailbox` seam to `SeamResult&lt;bool&gt;` now. Five implementors, eight callers, zero caller edits needed later; the slice that reads the flag (`ack_claim`) should land the type change with a conformance-kit assertion so the flag does not arrive unread. Right depth for this issue. Deploy note only: a new engine against an API still answering `{ success: true }` fails every ack with `Decode`; v2 ships API and clients from one tag, so not a finding.

- `/simplify`: nothing material. Four agents; production change clean; altitude right; nits 1, 3, 4, 7; one hunk captured as `simplify.patch`, tree restored.
- `/security-review`: nothing. No finding met the bar, so no false-positive pass ran. Answers (a)-(g) recorded above.
- `/crypto-privacy-review`: skipped per brief (no crypto in the diff).
- `/code-review`: Standards — no hard violation; judgement calls are nits 1, 3, 5, 6; every new test in a named gate (Unit tests `ci-api.yml:40`, Integration tests `ci-api.yml:68`, Engine simulation tests `ci-rust.yml:78`, Contract Suite `ci.yml:316`, OpenAPI freshness `ci-api.yml:203`). Spec — every scope and acceptance bullet of #1976 covered; malformed-id `false` is required by D5, not creep; engine edits outside the issue's file list are needed by the contract suite; PR body claims all true; nits 2 and 8.</result>

# Review of PR #1982 — `fix/1959-publish-above-observed-sequence` (head `4c016f7e1`)

Local verification in my worktree: `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; `cargo clippy -p cipherbox-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; the four new tests pass. Against `main`'s source with the PR's tests kept, the `net.rs` test and two drain tests fail; `a_sibling_root_at_the_same_sequence_is_a_lost_race_the_next_pass_heals` passes either way (the PR body does not claim otherwise). Tree restored; no repository edit remains. Scratch artifacts: `~/.claude/scratch/w1963/C/review/{pr.diff,simplify.patch,test.log,clippy.log,without-fix*.log}`.

## Findings

### F1 — should-fix — CONFIRMED — `crates/engine/src/net/publish.rs:383-391`, `crates/engine/src/net/fanout.rs:250-256`

Defect: the confirm compares only the single freshest record the fan-out picks, and the fan-out keeps the FIRST endpoint on a sequence tie, so a same-sequence sibling on a later endpoint reads as `Published`.
Failure scenario: A and B both mint N+1. If A's bytes sit on endpoint 0 (or endpoint 0 is transiently `Err` for A at confirm time), A reads its own bytes back: `Published`. B reads its own bytes from its first endpoint: `Published`. Both self-adopt N+1 and dequeue. The split is never healed; the next resolve on either device takes the other's N+1 as `Current` by sequence alone (`resolve.rs:357`), so one create is silently lost. The issue's "Expected" says the read-back must classify a same-sequence different-bytes record as a lost race; the PR meets that for one endpoint order only, and `net.rs` tests only that order.
Fix: let the confirm see every endpoint answer. Smallest form: in `fanout_get_under` track `contested: bool` (a verified record at the best sequence with different bytes was seen) and expose it through a confirm-only `fanout_get_contested(...) -&gt; Option&lt;(VerifiedRecord, Vec&lt;u8&gt;, bool)&gt;`; then in `publish.rs`:

```rust
Some((_, bytes, contested)) if bytes == record_bytes &amp;&amp; !contested =&gt; PublishOutcome::Published { sequence },
Some((observed, _, _)) if observed.sequence &gt;= sequence =&gt; PublishOutcome::LostRace { .. },
```

Add `publish_confirm_reads_a_sibling_on_a_later_endpoint_as_a_lost_race` (mirror of the new `net.rs` test, sibling seeded on endpoint 1 only). Both racing devices then halt, the next read leg adopts the tie winner, and each rebases and signs above.

### F2 — should-fix — CONFIRMED — `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs:5753`

Defect: the pre-signature re-resolve runs only for the scope root; interior folders share the same name derivation (`write_name` drain.rs:794, `write_name_signer` session.rs:205) and the same stale-base window.
Failure scenario: `ensure_folder` loads an interior folder once per pass and returns early on `pass.holds(folder)` (drain.rs:2397); uploads run between that load and the signature. Device B loads F at n and uploads for minutes; device A creates a child in F and confirms F at n+1; B signs n+1 over a body without A's child. Under F1's endpoint order B's confirm is `Published` and A's create is lost with no error. The issue's "Expected" is written for "a publish", not only a root.
Fix: generalise `reresolve_scope_root` to `reresolve_before_signing(plane, folder, built_on)`: root uses `root_adopter`; an interior folder builds the `ChildAdopter` exactly as `load_child_node` does (drain.rs:2305-2313) and runs the same `resolve_gated(NoCache)` / `TrustViolation` / `&gt; built_on` logic. Drop the `if is_scope_root` guard. Add a `write_plane` test with two devices writing one interior folder mid-pass. If the orchestrator keeps the PR root-scoped, file the interior case as a follow-up with a dependency edge before merge; the blueprint sweep needs only the root.

### F3 — should-fix — CONFIRMED — `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs:5814` and the `Halt::Attempt` doc `:391-397`

Defect: a pre-signature lost race is charged against `ATTEMPT_BUDGET` (5, then `AttemptsExhausted` dead-letter, drain.rs:1918-1935), but the budget's own rule (`:571-575`, "spent only by a halt the valve could attribute to these bytes") and the `Attempt` doc's reason ("a retry re-signs at the same sequence") do not hold here: nothing was PUT, the adopt raised the floor, the retry signs higher.
Failure scenario: device A drains a batch of creates (one root publish per create); device B has a create with a long upload at its head. Each B pass re-resolves after its upload, sees A's newer root, halts `Attempt`. Charges are durable and never decay while the op stays queued. After five losses B's create is dead-lettered although it was never refused. Four independent reads (altitude, crypto, spec, standards) reached the same conclusion.
Fix: charge it to the unattributed budget — return `Halt::Unclassified` at drain.rs:5814 with a one-line reason, or add `Halt::LostRace` handled next to `Unclassified` in `apply_valve` (charge_unattributed, no name handed back, same `upload_failure` text as `Attempt`). Restore the original `Attempt` doc. Add a test asserting the attempt count stays 0 after a pre-signature lost race.

### F4 — should-fix — CONFIRMED — `blueprint/engine.md:927-930`

Defect: "…the two-device publish fix — re-resolve, then sign above the highest sequence seen — which is not landed; without it two owner devices … publish two records at one sequence." This PR lands that fix for the scope root the sweep writes. The clause is a status marker in the exact form AGENTS.md "Tracker references" prescribes, and that rule says it "stops being true in the same diff that falsifies it". No design statement changes, so no ADR is needed.
Fix (in this PR): keep the dependency, drop the status clause: "The sweep depends on the two-device publish fix — the drain re-resolves the root through the gate just before it signs and signs above the highest sequence it observed, and a confirm that reads a sibling's record at its own sequence is a lost race the next pass rebases and signs above."

### F5 — nit — simplify patch at `~/.claude/scratch/w1963/C/review/simplify.patch` (110 lines; builds, tests green, clippy clean; tree restored)

Hunks: (a) `drain.rs` — extract `gated_scope_root(scope, end, mode) -&gt; Result&lt;GatedResolve, Halt&gt;`; `resolve_scope_root` and `reresolve_scope_root` both call it; the redundant `name` parameter is removed (`end.root_name` is the same value, drain.rs:2220); (b) `reresolve_scope_root` doc cut to five lines with the blueprint citation, no happy-path narration; (c) `publish.rs:380-382` confirm comment cut to one sentence. Not applied (author's call): `write_plane.rs` `root_sequence` duplicates `sequence_at` (11 callers, so I did not widen it); the endpoint assert loop is copied four times; the `net.rs` and `write_plane.rs` docs re-derive the two-device rule that `LostRace` already states; "the CAS law" is not a CONTEXT.md term — cite blueprint/engine.md "Publish".

### F6 — nit — CONFIRMED — `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs:5799-5809` (cost)

Every root publish adds one sequential fan-out GET (E endpoints) plus, on the usual equal-floor `Current` path, a gateway head-block fetch and unseal (`adopter.rs` `recover_own_scope_material` → `fetch_head_block`; the fresh `root_adopter` holds no local head). Per pass: r × (E + 1) extra round trips for r root-level ops. Rule 6 forbids skipping the gate; the only safe saving is handing the pass's held head block to the adopter as `local`. Optional.

### Residuals outside the diff (follow-ups, not blockers)

- `resolve.rs:357`: a record at exactly the floor is `Current` by sequence alone; a sibling's different bytes at the floor are never a conflict. Deepest home of the single-writer assumption (store the floor record hash beside the floor).
- `publish.rs:332`: `floor + 1` is unchecked; a key-holding sibling at `u64::MAX` wraps the mint to 0 in release. Use `checked_add`.

## PR body and title

Title: parentheses only in the scope. Body: bare `Closes #1959.`, no attribution, "fails without the fix" claims verified true. One overstatement: "reads another record at our own sequence as `LostRace`" holds only when the fan-out picks the sibling's record (F1).

## Gate verdicts

- `/simplify`: 3 source cleanups applied, captured as a patch (F5), tree clean.
- `/security-review`: no high-confidence vulnerability; every attack path examined is closed (gate fail-closed, floors move only on a gate pass, the sequence is inside the signed bytes, no clock or RNG, `Zeroizing` fields dropped by their terminal owner).
- `/crypto-privacy-review`: risk LOW for the diff; MEDIUM residual design gap (F1, F2); no crypto, KDF, zeroization, or test-secret issue.
- `/code-review` Standards: 2 HARD comment-rule breaches (the `Attempt` doc, the re-derived rule — covered by F3/F5), the rest judgement-call duplication (F5). Spec: 2 partial requirements (F1, F2), no scope creep, blueprint status clause must change (F4).</result>

<result>**Spec-axis review, PR `ae6062839` (#1964, #1965, #1966)**

**(a) Missing or partial**

1. #1964 "Add new vectors for each field and for each refusal." One accept vector (`personal-and-link`) carries all four link fields at once; no per-field accept vector exists. Reject vectors cover deadline-on-personal, cap-on-personal, link-at-write, explicit-personal-kind, zero-deadline, deadline-stripped. No KAT vector covers `conversionPermission` on a personal entry or an unknown `kind`; only unit tests in `crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs` do.
2. ADR 0023 D2 "The commitment signature covers both" and D9 "under the commitment signature." The KAT pins only `link-deadline-stripped` (`commitment-invalid`). Edits to `kind`, `conversionPermission` and `admissionCap` fail `verify_grant_set` only in the unit test `a_link_entry_round_trips_byte_stable_and_its_fields_are_signed`; no vector pins them.
3. Silent re-mint gap. `remint_grants` (`crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs` ~4300) rebuilds each entry from `mint_grant_row` and copies only `committed_entry.unknown`. It drops `kind`, `deadline`, `conversion_permission`, `admission_cap` on the commitment entry and `via_link`, `grantee_name` on the row. Issue #1965 defers only the via-link re-map ("The re-map lands in E7"); the PR body defers "the write-wave re-mint of the new fields" for rows. Nothing states that a write wave turns a link entry into a personal entry and erases its deadline and cap. The encoder accepts that output, so no refusal catches it. Latent until the mint slice lands, but undocumented.

**(b) Not asked for**

4. An explicit `kind: "personal"` is refused (`invalid-entry-kind`). ADR 0023 D2 names two kinds; the issue says only "when it is absent, the entry is `personal`". A canonical-form narrowing, defensible, but beyond the text.
5. Engine removals (`entry_is_live`, the published-deadline branch of `convert_invite_claim`, the forged-deadline prune test) follow from "Retire `expiresAt`"; in scope.

**(c) Implemented, check the fit**

6. ADR 0027 D1 length bound: `MAX_GRANTEE_NAME_BYTES = 255` equals `MAX_NODE_NAME_BYTES = 255` (`crates/engine/src/name.rs:23`), which `SharePointer::bounded` and the received-shares codec use for `displayName`. Value matches; it is a separate literal with no shared constant. Character policy differs: the share display name checks length only; `GranteeName::new` adds `char::is_control`; neither applies `is_deceptive` (bidi overrides, zero-width). A claimant name with a bidi override passes the codec. The ADR sets no character rule, so this is an observation, not a violation.

**Verified as asked**

- Pre-existing vectors unchanged; only `ledger-zero-expiry` removed. A pre-change `write_body_accept` row that carried `expiresAt` keeps its bytes as a preserved unknown and still verifies (`kat_gen.rs` ~311).
- `recipient_binding_reject` pins removed `viaLink`, removed `granteeName`, changed `nameSource`, changed name (`identity-signature-invalid`). Pre-existing `read-grantee`/`write-grantee` binding vectors untouched.
- Encode refusals are release-active `Err`; `crates/core/tests/encode_refusals.rs` runs under `--release` in job `Core KATs (native + WASM)` (`ci-rust.yml:136-187`).
- Store kind `0x03` present in `manifest.json:646`; `OwnerLocalKind::InviteRecords` still live in core (retirement is a later slice).
- Fingerprint: named `identity_fingerprint`, 10 bytes (80 bits), fixed hex-groups encoding, KAT `fingerprint.json` (2 keys), WASM `identityFingerprint`, facade read. `boundary.worker.ts` constants match both KAT keys and strings exactly (hand-copied, not imported).</result>

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>## SECURITY REVIEW COMPLETE

**Files analyzed:** 37 (diff) plus the source of every touched path
**Crypto operations touched:** ECDSA preimages (grant-set commitment, recipient binding), unkeyed BLAKE3 fingerprint, re-seal and re-mint producers
**Issues found:** 0 critical, 2 high, 1 medium, 2 low

### Findings

#### [HIGH] The write wave re-signs a committed link entry as a perpetual personal grant

**Location:** `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:4308-4321` (`remint_grants`), `crates/engine/src/grants/ledger.rs:156-192` (`mint_grant_row`)

`remint_grants` builds the re-minted entry with `mint_grant_row`, which calls `GrantSetEntry::new` (always `Personal`, no `deadline`, no `admission_cap`, no `conversion_permission`), then copies only `unknown` from `committed_entry`. The row comes from `mint_grant_row` too, so `via_link` and `grantee_name` are gone and the row is owner-signed without them.

Scenario: the decoder now accepts a link entry, so a record authored by the mint slice (or any newer build) holds `{kind: link, deadline, admissionCap}` at `read`. The owner revokes an unrelated write grantee, which runs a write wave. The wave re-mints the link entry as a personal `read` entry with no deadline and no cap, and the owner signs it. The link holder keeps reading past the deadline for ever, the cap is unenforceable, and every converted row loses the `viaLink` that ADR 0023 D9 counts. This is fail-open on the field this PR introduces, in a function this PR edits, so it belongs in this PR, not the rotation slice.

Fix: carry every owner-signed field by struct update, and re-sign the row with the carried fields:

```rust
let commitment_entry = GrantSetEntry {
    tag: minted.commitment_entry.tag,
    masked_recipient_enc_pk: minted.commitment_entry.masked_recipient_enc_pk,
    pseudonym_pk: minted.commitment_entry.pseudonym_pk,
    ..committed_entry.clone()   // kind, deadline, conversion_permission, admission_cap, unknown
};
let mut ledger_entry = minted.ledger_entry;
if row_is_owner_attested(&amp;owner_identity, row, old_name) {
    ledger_entry.via_link = row.via_link.map(|t| new_tag_of[&amp;t]);   // old tag -&gt; re-minted tag (ADR 0023 D2)
    ledger_entry.grantee_name = row.grantee_name.clone();
    ledger_entry.owner_sig = sign_recipient_binding(self.owner, new_name, &amp;ledger_entry).to_compact();
}
```

The old-to-new tag map is available from the same loop. An unattested row's name and via-link are a write grantee's to choose, so they follow `recipient_identity_pk` and drop.

#### [HIGH] The re-seal budget is sized for a 224-byte row and copies writer-authored names of up to 565 bytes

**Location:** `crates/engine/src/content/limits.rs:42`, `crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs:836,1052-1058,1104`, `crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal/tests.rs:870-941`

A bare row measures 234 bytes on the wire (`a5` map, keys, 64+32+33+32 bytes, `"read"`), already over `LEDGER_ROW_WIRE_BYTES = 224`. A row with `viaLink` + a 255-byte `granteeName` + `nameSource: "claimant"` measures 565. `reseal_scope_root` runs `enforce_committed_ledger` only (tag + permission, line 836) and then copies `via_link` and `grantee_name` from every committed row verbatim (1055-1056) with no `row_is_owner_attested` check. `resealable_section_bytes(1024)` = 909,312. Measured from the KAT vectors, a maximal bare re-seal is about 675 KB (read blob 281, row 234, child ref 98, history link 181 per item); full-width rows add 341 x 1024 = 349 KB, to about 1,024 KB, over the budget by about 115 KB. The break-even is about 750 committed rows.

Scenario: a committed write grantee on a scope with 750 or more committed grants re-authors the write body with a 255-byte name and `nameSource` on every row (no signature needed: the re-seal never checks one). The owner's next revoke hits `SectionNotResealable` (`reseal.rs:1104`), which `net/rotation.rs:3828` maps to `NotLanded`, on every retry. The scope is rotation-proof and the writer is un-revocable. The maximal test at `reseal/tests.rs:870` uses `attested_row` bare rows with a padded unknown map the re-seal drops, so it cannot see this.

Fix, both halves: (1) set `LEDGER_ROW_WIRE_BYTES` to the full-width row derived from the constants (`234 + 42 + (14 + MAX_GRANTEE_NAME_BYTES) + 20`, rounded to 576) so an honest owner with 1024 named grantees can still rotate; (2) carry `via_link` and `grantee_name` only when `row_is_owner_attested`, which removes the writer lever entirely. Rebuild the maximal test with full-width rows.

#### [MEDIUM] `GranteeName` admits bidi overrides and zero-width characters in an owner-signed name shown beside the fingerprint

**Location:** `crates/core/src/seal/write_body.rs:135-141`; engine law at `crates/engine/src/name.rs:92-105`

`char::is_control` covers Cc only. U+202E, U+2066-2069, U+200B, U+FEFF, U+2028 are Cf/Zl and pass. The engine already refuses exactly this set for node names (`validate_name`, `NameError::DeceptiveCharacter`) and strips it at render, but core cannot import that set, and the claim name will be validated by the codec every build shares.

Scenario: a claimant suggests `"Alice\u{202E}renwo"`. Conversion copies it into a row the owner signs with flag `claimant`; the people table and every co-writer render "Alice" with "owner" drawn reversed beside the fingerprint; a line separator (U+2028) fakes a second confirmation line, which is the exact case the control-character KAT vector says it closes.

Fix: move the `is_deceptive` character set into core (a small `crates/core/src/name.rs`, or beside `GranteeName`), have `GranteeName::new` refuse it with `invalid-grantee-name`, add a reject KAT vector, and have `crates/engine/src/name.rs` use the core set so the law has one home.

#### [LOW] A link entry needs no deadline and no admission cap

**Location:** `crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs:898-916` (`validate`)

`validate` refuses link fields on a personal entry and a non-`read` link, and nothing else. A `{kind: link}` entry with no `deadline` and no `admissionCap` encodes, signs and decodes. CONTEXT.md "Link entry" says it carries all three. Scenario: the conversion slice reads `admission_cap: None` as unlimited, which is the 1024-row enrolment ADR 0023 D9 exists to stop. Either require both on `Link` in `validate` (fail-closed, one more reject vector) or write the absent-cap rule now (absent = zero admissions).

#### [LOW] `expiresAt: 0` now decodes

The `ledger-zero-expiry` reject vector is gone; a row carrying `expiresAt: 0` decodes as a preserved unknown and re-encodes byte-stable. Acceptable under the retirement and no engine path reads it (checked below). Note only.

### Checklist verdicts

- **A. Preimage:** clean. `Map::insert` keeps canonical order (`value.rs:224`), keys are distinct, `granteeName` and `nameSource` always travel together, so the encoding is injective over the field set. The `read-grantee` and `write-grantee` preimages and signatures are byte-identical to `main` (checked with `git show main:...`). Removal of a present field fails with `identity-signature-invalid`, class `trust`, pinned by four reject vectors the manifest test asserts by check and class.
- **B. Commitment:** clean apart from the LOW above. `sign_grant_set`, `verify_grant_set`, `verify_grant_set_bound` and `encode_grant_section` (`section.rs:480`) all route through `encode_grant_set_commitment`, which runs `validate` on every entry; every engine producer calls `sign_grant_set`. `GRANT_SET_ENTRY_KNOWN` and `LEDGER_ENTRY_KNOWN` list the new keys and `assert_unknown_disjoint` (`body.rs:567`) is a release `Err`. Non-text `kind` fails `unexpected-type` through the guard's `?`; explicit `"personal"` fails `invalid-entry-kind`; `deadline: 0` fails `invalid-expiry` and `NonZeroU64` makes it unrepresentable on encode.
- **C. Fingerprint:** clean. It is a public digest, not a derivation: public input, public output, no secret, so it needs no catalog edge, which matches core.md line 88-90. Plain `blake3::hash` cannot collide with any `derive_key`/`keyed_hash` edge (BLAKE3 mode flags separate them); the only other plain-hash use is `H(ciphertext)` in `structure.rs`, whose digest no consumer ever compares with a fingerprint. `from_sec1` refuses any length but 33; output is 20 lowercase hex chars in five groups, 80 bits; domain and width are pinned in the manifest and the browser suite asserts the same two vector strings against the WASM export.
- **D. Retired `expiresAt`:** clean. `write_body_accept.json` shows six inserted lines only; `entry_is_live` is deleted; the only deadline reads left are `RecordedInvite::expires_at` (`invite.rs:909`) and view structs. `facade.rs:10640` is a device-approval row, not a ledger row.
- **E. Redaction and secrets:** clean. `GranteeName` Debug uses `RedactedText`, `via_link` uses `RedactedBytes`; test scalars are fixtures; the WASM error carries no bytes.
- **I. Determinism:** clean. No clock or RNG in the touched core paths; `now` stays injected in `convert_invite_claim`.
- **J. Gates:** clean. `encode_refusals.rs` runs under `--release` in job "Core KATs (native + WASM)" (and again in the plain and wasm32-wasip1 runs); `engine.spec.ts` runs in "Client Browser Suite". All assertions are behavioural.

### Additional test cases, prioritized

1. `reseal/tests.rs`: the maximal committed set with full-width rows (`via_link`, a 255-byte name, `nameSource`) re-seals inside `resealable_section_bytes(MAX_GRANT_BLOBS)`. Fails today.
2. `reseal/tests.rs`: a name and via-link on a row whose `owner_sig` does not verify are not carried by the re-seal.
3. `net/rotation.rs` tests: the re-mint carries a link entry's `kind`, `deadline`, `conversion_permission`, `admission_cap`, and `verify_grant_set` passes over the re-minted set.
4. `net/rotation.rs` tests: the re-mint re-maps an attested row's `via_link` to the link's new tag, carries the name, and the new row verifies under `new_name`; an unattested row's name drops with its identity key.
5. `encode_refusals.rs` (release): `GranteeName::new` refuses U+202E, U+2066, U+200B, U+FEFF, U+2028; `write_body_reject.json` gains one vector.
6. `grant.rs`: a link entry missing `deadline` or `admissionCap` is refused (or a test pinning that it is accepted, once the rule is written).
7. `grant_set_reject.json`: `kind: 1` and `deadline: "soon"` fail `unexpected-type`; `admissionCap: 0` on a link is accepted.
8. `write_body.rs`: a 253-byte ASCII name plus one 2-byte char (255 bytes) is accepted and 254 plus one 2-byte char (256) is refused, pinning the byte bound at a UTF-8 boundary.
9. `write_body.rs`: swapping the `granteeName` and `nameSource` values (`"owner"`/`Owner` vs `"Alice"`/`Claimant`) yields distinct preimages, pinning that both keys and both value types are in the signed map.
10. `kat_manifest.rs`: the browser suite's literal fingerprint vectors equal `fingerprint.json` (or read the JSON in `boundary.worker.ts`), so the two copies cannot drift apart.

### Recommendations

1. Fix the re-mint in this PR: struct-update the committed entry and re-sign carried row fields (finding 1).
2. Resize `LEDGER_ROW_WIRE_BYTES` to the full row and carry names only from attested rows; rebuild the maximal re-seal test with full-width rows (finding 2).
3. Move the deceptive-character set into core and refuse it in `GranteeName::new` with a KAT vector (finding 3).

Report location: inline. Scratch helper only: `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/w1963/A/review/measure.py`.</result>

**Findings**
- **F1:** the fan-out scan now also reports a contest: another endpoint served other bytes at the freshest sequence. The confirm gives `Published` only for our own bytes with no contest. Any other record at our sequence or above it is `LostRace`. The new test `publish_confirm_reads_a_sibling_on_a_later_endpoint_as_a_lost_race` fails when the contest check is removed.
- **F2:** the drain now re-resolves every folder before it signs, not only the scope root. The root goes through the root adopter. An interior folder goes through a `ChildAdopter`, built as `load_child_node` builds it.
  - One point the finding did not state: a record that the epoch floor refuses still counts, with its own sequence, as `load_child_node` treats it. Without this, every lazy-wave re-seal would halt as a refused record.
  - The new test `a_sibling_interior_folder_that_lands_mid_pass_is_rebased_on_and_signed_above` fails without the interior re-resolve.
- **F3:** a lost race before the signature is now `Halt::LostRace`. It is charged to the unattributed budget, hands back no name, and uses the same failure text as `Attempt`. I restored the original `Attempt` doc.
  - A create that loses this race does not dead-letter as `AlreadyPublished` on the retry. Its child record was self-adopted, so the floor check sees it.
  - For the test I checked behaviour instead of the stored attempt count, which is an internal byte format. In `a_run_of_lost_races_before_the_signature_never_dead_letters_the_op`, six races in a row leave the op queued with no dead letter, and the next pass lands it. This test fails when `LostRace` is charged like `Attempt`.
- **F4:** I replaced the `blueprint/engine.md` passage with your text. `pnpm lint:md` is clean.
- **F5:** I applied `simplify.patch`, adjusted to F2 (the shared helper is `gated_scope_root`). The "CAS law" phrase is replaced with a citation of blueprint/engine.md "Publish". The `net.rs` test docs no longer restate the two-device rule.
- **F6:** skipped. The pass keeps no loaded head block to hand to the adopter, so it would have to carry one through `LoadedRoot`/`LoadedNode`. That is not a small change.
- **Overflow:** the publish now uses `checked_add(1)` and refuses with a new `PublishError::SequenceExhausted`. I added it to the refusal groups in `facade.rs`, `net/provision.rs`, `net/retire.rs` and the three verdict functions in `net/rotation.rs`. The test `publish_at_an_exhausted_sequence_floor_refuses_before_it_signs` checks it.

**The `resolve.rs:357` residual is not small.** The fix itself is a byte comparison against the cached last-known-good copy. But it needs:
- a decision on what a reader does with a different record at exactly its floor: a trust violation, or a new outcome;
- a new `ResolveOutcome` variant or a changed meaning, with every consumer updated: the tick root leg, the focus legs, the drain, rotation, and the liveness hold;
- a rule for a cold cache, where there is nothing to compare.

I recommend a separate issue.

<result>The isolation worktree was removed under me, so I cannot write `findings.md` to disk; the consolidated report follows inline.

# Review of PR #1983 — feat(core): link fields, named ledger row, key fingerprint

Head `ae60628392c00fec9095090b9b69270c038ba2be`, branch `feat/1963-core-link-fields`. CI: every check green at head. PR body: no attribution, bare `Closes #N.` lines, title parentheses hold the scope only.

## Findings (by severity)

### should-fix 1 — the re-seal budget does not count the new row fields
- `crates/engine/src/content/limits.rs:42` (`LEDGER_ROW_WIRE_BYTES = 224`) with `crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs:1055-1056`.
- Defect: `reseal_scope_root` now copies `via_link` and `grantee_name` from every committed row. The per-row budget still describes the old row. Measured from the KAT bytes: a bare row is 234 bytes, a linked row with a short name is 314-319 bytes, a row with a 255-byte name is about 565 bytes.
- Failure scenario: `enforce_committed_ledger` compares only `(tag, permission)`, and the re-seal never checks the row owner signature. A committed write grantee puts a 255-byte `granteeName` on every row. The minted section exceeds `resealable_section_bytes` (reseal.rs:1102) and the re-seal returns `SectionNotResealable`. The scope becomes rotation-proof. The maximal re-seal test (`reseal/tests.rs:874-941`) uses bare rows, so nothing catches it.
- Fix: derive the per-row budget from core constants (`234 + 42 + MAX_GRANTEE_NAME_BYTES + ~34`) with a compile-time assert, and extend the maximal re-seal test to rows with `via_link` and a 255-byte name. Alternative: carry `grantee_name` only when `row_is_owner_attested` passes.

### should-fix 2 — the write-wave re-mint drops every new owner-signed field
- `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:4313-4321` (`remint_grants`).
- Defect: the re-minted entry comes from `GrantSetEntry::new` (always `Personal`); only `unknown` is copied from `committed_entry`. `kind`, `deadline`, `conversion_permission`, `admission_cap` are lost and the owner re-signs a personal entry. The row loses `via_link` and `grantee_name`.
- Failure scenario: an owner device on this build re-mints a record that another build wrote with a link entry. The link loses its deadline and cap; its admitted rows lose the via-link reference. This fails open. No producer mints a link entry yet, but the decoder already accepts one.
- Spec note: #1965 defers only the via-link re-map; #1973 names only the re-map. The loss of the entry link fields and the grantee name is a silent gap.
- Fix: `GrantSetEntry { tag, masked_recipient_enc_pk, ..committed_entry.clone() }` (keep the pseudonym check) so every owner-signed field carries by default; carry `grantee_name` when `row_is_owner_attested` passes; until the tag re-map lands, carry `via_link` unchanged or return `Rejected` for a row that has one, and state the re-map in #1973.

### nit 3 — absence-justifying comment
- `crates/engine/src/grants/invite.rs:751-753`: "Nothing in a resolved record this build mints marks a row as an invite". This diff adds `EntryKind::Link` and `viaLink`; the sentence turns false when a link entry mints. Replace with: "`links` is the owner's own record of the live links on this scope; a claim converts only against one of those."

### nit 4 — absence-justifying comment
- `crates/core/src/seal/write_body.rs:599-601`: "The decoder's grantee-name rejects need no guard here …". Delete; the rule lives on the `GranteeName` doc (line 114).

### nit 5 — grantee-name policy admits deceptive characters
- `crates/core/src/seal/write_body.rs:136-140`: `GranteeName::new` refuses only `char::is_control`. Bidi overrides/isolates, zero-width characters, U+2028/2029 pass; `crates/engine/src/name.rs:92 is_deceptive` refuses them for node names. The length bound matches ADR 0027 D1 (255 = `MAX_NODE_NAME_BYTES`). Not exploitable here: no engine path builds a name and no UI shows one. Fix: move `is_deceptive` into core and call it from `GranteeName::new`, now or in the claim slice.

### nit 6 — duplicated helpers and dead code (from `/simplify`, verified)
- `kat_gen.rs:7667-7680` `entry_with`: the `retain` loop is dead (`Map::insert` replaces); base pairs repeat `grant_set_entry_map`. Same at `kat_gen.rs:5940` `named_entry` vs `good_entry()`.
- `kat_gen.rs:5753` `named_ledger_row` repeats `signed_ledger_row`; `write_body.rs:1237` `named_row` repeats `signed_row`. Share one `sign(entry)` step.
- `kat_manifest.rs:4440-4444` `binding_row` inlines the `"claimant"|"owner"` match a third time. Add `NameSource::from_wire(&amp;str) -&gt; Option&lt;Self&gt;` and use it in `from_value` and the test.
- `grant.rs:2270` and `write_body.rs:1389` repeat tests in `tests/encode_refusals.rs`; keep the release-run copies.
- `grant.rs:900` `GrantSetEntry::validate` is `pub` with no external caller.
- `packages/client/src/testkit.ts:532` `fingerprintKeys` is written and never read.
- `invite.rs:1411` the `mint` closure repeats the `link()` helper (1645).

## Gate verdicts

- `/simplify`: four agents ran read-only; no edit applied, so no patch exists under `~/.claude/scratch/w1963/A/review/`. Findings folded into nit 6 and should-fix 1-2.
- `/security-review`: no finding above 0.7 confidence. Preimage is one canonical det-CBOR map; a lone `granteeName`/`nameSource` is `missing-field`; every signer and verifier routes through `encode_grant_set_commitment`, so `validate` is release-active; `from_sec1` refuses non-33-byte input; Debug impls redact; no engine path reads a row deadline. The latent gap it noted is should-fix 2.
- `/crypto-privacy-review`: the dedicated reviewer reported to the orchestrator directly; its result did not return to this context. My own checks on its axes: preimage unambiguous; pre-change vectors byte-stable (`invite-expiring-entry` unchanged); removal of a present signed field fails `identity-signature-invalid`, class `trust`; fingerprint 80 bits, fixed encoding, KAT pinned, browser suite asserts the same strings; store kind 0x03 reserved; encode refusals run under `--release` inside "Core KATs (native + WASM)"; no secret bytes in assertions.
- `/code-review` (standards + spec): both agents reported to the orchestrator directly; results did not return here. Own spec check: #1964 and #1966 complete; #1965 complete except the silent re-mint gap (should-fix 2). Own standards check: nits 3-4; no tracker refs; terminology matches `CONTEXT.md`.

**Design choices the issues left open:**
- The fragment adds `scopeId`, because the re-point AAD binds the scope id.
- The claim field is now `scopePointerName`. This touches the claim body that batch C owns.
- The join posts the claim, bookmarks the share with the link keys, and forces one pass. The normal refresh is the only read path.
- A revoke now checks the target before it looks for the link. The facade test now expects `revoke-link-target-is-not-a-scope-root`.
- The web app sends an empty `ownerName`. The only identity it holds is the email, and ADR 0027 D1 does not show an email on an invite.
- A `revocation-signal` on a `viaLink` row shows "the owner revoked this link".

**Tests removed:**
- The store tests, the prune tests and the four stranded write-link-wave tests. Write links no longer run a cut.
- `WriteShare::InviteLink` in `mount_convergence.rs`. A link now mints at read, and a contact read share fails the same offline read test. That gap already exists and is not a link regression.

**Left out:**
- Write conversion: conversion grants read only, because a write grant needs a write cut (batch C).
- Admission-cap enforcement (batch C).
- The spent set is gone, so a mailbox replay window is open until ack-first (#1970) and #1973.
- The contact-store link charge is keyed by the link tag, which moves on a name wave.
- The name wave does not copy grantee names.
- A link on an existing scope is still refused (#1971).
- I did not run `/simplify` or the review skills. The brief says a separate reviewer sends findings.</result>

<summary>Agent "Lane C publish sequence bug" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I fixed both Greptile findings on PR 1982, replied in each thread, resolved both, and posted one disposition comment. It was one push, and every CI check passed. I requested no CodeRabbit review and did not change the ready state.

- **New head SHA:** `b2937e07cde3948240768ccccbe1d0b537b7f2b1`
- **CI:** 37 checks, none pending, none failing (a few path-gated jobs skipped).
- **Disposition comment:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1982#issuecomment-5826255579
- **PR body:** updated through the REST API with the new changes and tests. It has no attribution, and the commits have no attribution lines.

**Thread 1: our record on the first endpoint kept the network split**
- The publish receipt now carries the record that won the race, and the drain gates it into the cache before it halts. The halt stays `Halt::Attempt`, because our PUT was acked. If the gate refuses the winner, the drain reports it and still halts as an attempt. A refusal halt would hand back the name of a record that reached the network.
- At the floor, the drain now builds on its cached copy while an endpoint still serves it. So the retry rebases onto the sibling's record, not ours, and signs above both.
- New test `a_split_where_our_root_holds_the_first_endpoint_heals_above_both_records`: after the retry, every endpoint holds one record at S+1 with both creates. It fails when the winner is not adopted.
- The fake record store gained `seed_record_after_put_at`, which lands a deferred record on one endpoint only; the test needs it.

**Thread 2: an equal sequence hid a different record**
- Each folder in a pass now keeps the record bytes it was built on. The check before signing is a lost race when the name has a higher sequence, or the same sequence served only with other bytes.
- The pass now opens the scope root through a gated resolve instead of the cache alone, so the next pass builds on the record the endpoints serve.
- New test `a_different_root_at_the_built_on_sequence_is_rebased_on_and_signed_above`: the pass halts, and the next pass signs S+1 over record Y with the create applied. It fails when only sequences are compared. The test puts Y on both endpoints, not endpoint 0 alone: if one endpoint still serves X, the pass correctly does not halt.

**Side effects to know about**
- **One changed existing test:** `a_refused_root_authoring_names_the_check_that_fired_on_the_pass_it_fired` placed its bad root in the cache only. It now fails the GETs at the root name, so the pass still builds on the cached copy.
- **One extra read per pass:** each drain pass now makes one more network read of its scope root.

Both thread replies say the reader-side residual is out of scope and filed as issue 1984.

All the checks you listed pass locally: engine tests, workspace tests excluding `fuser`, fmt, clippy with `-D warnings`, the wasm32 check and `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`.</result>

<summary>Agent "Simplify: efficiency angle" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I found four efficiency costs that this diff introduces. The web, TS worker, wasm and `net/rotation.rs` hunks are clean. Line numbers refer to the files in the worktree on `review/1986`.

**1. `crates/engine/src/grants/received_status.rs:610` — the pointer follows are not charged to the pass budget.**
`follow_held_pointers` runs before the budgeted loop, and only `due` gates it. A forced pass (`NoCache`) marks every bookmark due. It then resolves the scope pointer of every held bookmark, one after the other, up to `MAX_RECEIVED_SHARES` = 1024 fan-out resolves. After that, the main loop reads only `MAX_RESOLVES_PER_PASS` = 16 roots. Every pointer resolve past the sixteenth due share is wasted, and the sync tick blocks on it. The fix charges the same budget in the same list order as the main loop.
```diff
@@ fn follow_held_pointers
         let mut healed: Vec&lt;(BookmarkKey, IpnsName)&gt; = Vec::new();
         let mut rejected = BTreeSet::new();
+        let mut budget = MAX_RESOLVES_PER_PASS;
         for share in received.iter() {
             let key = share.key();
+            if budget == 0 {
+                break;
+            }
+            if !due(&amp;key) {
+                continue;
+            }
+            budget -= 1;
             let (Some(hold), Some(contact)) = (
                 received.link_hold(&amp;key),
                 by_identity.get(&amp;share.sharer_identity_pk),
             ) else {
                 continue;
             };
-            if !due(&amp;key) {
-                continue;
-            }
```

**2. `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:9081` — `claim_invite_link` waits for a whole forced pass.**
The command waits on `pass.landed()`. That is every sync leg with `NoCache`: the folder legs, up to 16 root resolves, and the unbounded pointer follows from finding 1. So the claim takes the time of a full manual refresh, and the time grows with the bookmark count. `InvitePage` shows "claimed" only after the whole pass ends. The bookmark is already durable at this point, and the doc comment calls the read best effort. Filing the pass without waiting gives the same result at the cost of one filing.
```diff
-        if let Ok(Some(pass)) = self.file_forced_pass() {
-            let _ = pass.landed().await;
-        }
+        let _ = self.file_forced_pass();
         Ok(())
```
Two points need a check before this change. First, dropping the `ForcedPass` handle must still run the pass. `refresh.rs:129` shows only oneshot senders, so I expect it does. Second, the host must accept that "claimed" can show before the listing updates.

**3. `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:9067` — the link bookmark is built before the check that can discard it.**
`pending_link_bookmark` does one ECDSA verify (`verified_names`) and makes two `SecretBytes` copies (`invite_secret` and `pointer_read_key`). It does this even when `personal` is true and nothing is written. The fix takes the key from the fragment first. It also runs `find` once instead of twice.
```diff
-        let (share, hold) = pending_link_bookmark(&amp;fragment, &amp;owner);
-        let key = share.key();
-        // A personal bookmark already reads the folder; the link adds nothing.
-        let personal = received.find(&amp;key).is_some() &amp;&amp; received.link_hold(&amp;key).is_none();
-        if !personal {
-            if received.find(&amp;key).is_none() {
+        let key = (owner.identity_pk().to_sec1(), fragment.scope_id);
+        let known = received.find(&amp;key).is_some();
+        // A personal bookmark already reads the folder; the link adds nothing.
+        if !known || received.link_hold(&amp;key).is_some() {
+            let (share, hold) = pending_link_bookmark(&amp;fragment, &amp;owner);
+            if !known {
                 received.reconcile(share);
             }
```

**4. `crates/engine/src/grants/received_status.rs:580` — a deadline change clones the whole `LinkHold`.**
To change one `Option&lt;UnixMillis&gt;`, the code clones the hold, which copies the 32-byte invite secret, and then inserts it again. This happens once per changed deadline, so the cost is small. It is still a copy of a secret that does not need to exist. The fix adds a setter to `ReceivedSharesList` in `accept.rs`.
```diff
+    pub(crate) fn set_link_deadline(&amp;mut self, key: &amp;BookmarkKey, deadline: Option&lt;UnixMillis&gt;) {
+        if let Some(hold) = self.links.get_mut(key) {
+            hold.deadline = deadline;
+        }
+    }
```
```diff
                 HoldChange::Deadline(key, deadline) =&gt; {
-                    if let Some(mut hold) = received.link_hold(&amp;key).cloned() {
-                        hold.deadline = deadline;
-                        received.hold_link(key, hold);
-                    }
+                    received.set_link_deadline(&amp;key, deadline);
                 }
```

**Low priority: `crates/engine/src/grants/invite.rs:837` with `facade.rs:9145` — the link list is computed N+1 times in one conversion pass.**
`convert_invite_claims` calls `committed_links` once before the loop. `convert_invite_claim` calls it again for every inbox item. Each call does the grant-set signature verify plus one `row_is_owner_attested` ECDSA verify per link. A conversion adds only personal rows, so the link rows do not change during the pass. The new waste is about L row verifies per item (L is usually 1). The set-signature verify on each item already existed, and it must stay because the set is re-signed after each grant. A possible fix passes the list in and keeps `authorise` on each item:
```diff
 pub fn convert_invite_claim(
     owner: &amp;OwnerAuthority&lt;'_&gt;,
     scope: &amp;CommittedScope&lt;'_&gt;,
+    links: &amp;[CommittedLink],
     scope_pointer_name: &amp;IpnsName,
@@
-    let links = committed_links(owner, scope)?;
+    owner.authorise(scope)?;
```
This changes a `pub` signature and trusts the caller to pass links from the same scope. At L = 1 it is optional.

**Checked and clean:**
- `EphemeralInvitee::from_secret` runs once for each held share in each pass: one scalar check and one KDF. Caching it across passes would keep the secret in memory longer, so I recommend no change.
- On the link path, `recipient_blinded_tag` runs 5 times for each held share in each pass: the personal tag once, the link tag 4 times. That is about 4 extra X25519 exchanges per held share on each due pass. It is too small to justify threading the tag through the code.
- The received-shares store is loaded once and saved at most once per pass. `due` is a map lookup, and after a heal nothing is resolved a second time.
- `InviteFragment::encode/decode` copies the secrets only into a tree that is zeroized after use. The sharing read calls `committed_links` once per scope.
- The copy of the link fields in the `rotation.rs` name wave is four field assignments.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review link-held read path" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Review of the link holder read path (`git diff origin/feat/1963-core-link-fields...HEAD`)**

The slice follows ADR 0024 D5 in most points. I found 4 should-fix defects and 5 nits. The most important defect: the engine deletes `linkSecret` before the personal blob opens.

## Findings

1. **should-fix**: `crates/engine/src/grants/received_status.rs:475-478`
   - **Defect:** The engine drops the link keys when the pass classifies the share as `Granted`. It does not wait for the personal blob to open. The `open` call can fail (513-516 sets `Unresolvable`) or can skip the blob (496 scope not renderable, 881 own tree, 937 seam error). The drop still persists (573-591).
   - **Scenario:** A co-writer re-seal publishes a personal blob that fails the AEAD open. The holder deletes `linkSecret`, and it cannot read through the link or through the personal blob. ADR 0024 D2 says "the persist that records the first personal **open**".
   - **Fix:** Push `HoldChange::Drop` only after `open` returns `Ok(Some(_))` on the personal path.

2. **should-fix**: `received_status.rs:372-373` and `:589-591`, against `facade.rs:9062-9079`
   - **Defect:** The refresh is now a load-modify-persist writer of the received-shares list, and it spans many network awaits. The claim command writes the same list at the same time, and nothing locks the list.
   - **Scenario:**
     - The tick loads the list.
     - The user joins link B. The command persists B and its `linkSecret`.
     - The tick then persists its stale copy. The tick persists on every first read, heal or deadline change, so this is a normal pass.
     - B is lost. The claim is posted, but nothing reads until conversion.
   - **Fix:** Load the list again just before the persist and apply the heal, deadline and drop changes by key. Alternatively, serialize all writers of the list through one engine async lock.

3. **should-fix**: `received_status.rs:633`
   - **Defect:** When the pointer consult returns `Unavailable` or `Ok(None)`, the pass keeps the last healed root name. It then resolves and renders that name, which is not this pass's `currentRootName` (ADR 0024 D5 item 3).
   - **Scenario:** A relay hides the pointer after a write wave. The blueprint says the old name is "dead to survivors" and a revoked write grantee can take it over. A replayed pre-wave owner record at a higher IPNS sequence then renders stale content. The personal path has the same weakness.
   - **Fix:** On the link path, do not render (classify `Unresolvable`) unless this pass vouched the name. At least, measure the record against the write-epoch floor that the consult raised.

4. **should-fix**: `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:4319-4325`
   - **Defect:** No test covers this change. It copies the link terms (kind, deadline, conversion permission, admission cap) onto the entry that a write wave re-mints. The test `a_root_a_write_wave_moved_still_opens_through_the_pointer` mints the link directly at the current root and runs no real wave.
   - **Scenario:** A regression turns every link into a personal row after one wave. Holders then see "revoked", and no test fails.
   - **Fix:** Add an `owner_actions` test: mint a link, join, run a write wave, then check that the holder is still `Granted` and that the entry is still `Link` with the same deadline.

5. **nit**: `received_status.rs:610-643`
   - **Defect:** `follow_held_pointers` is not inside `MAX_RESOLVES_PER_PASS`.
   - **Scenario:** Every due hold costs one pointer fan-out per pass, also when the resolve loop has no budget left.
   - **Fix:** Share the budget.

6. **nit**: `facade.rs:9026-9030`
   - **Defect:** When a fragment names this vault's own root, the join returns `UnsupportedTarget`. A forged fragment is attacker input, but the error reports host misuse, not a trust violation.
   - **Fix:** Map it to `TrustViolation`, the same class as `AcceptError::OwnVaultScope`.

7. **nit**: `facade.rs:9075` with `link_read.rs:60-63`
   - **Defect:** A second join replaces the hold and resets `deadline` to `None`.
   - **Scenario:** A user joins an expired link again after the owner's sweep cut it. The share then reads "revoked", not "expired".
   - **Fix:** Keep the stored deadline when the invite secret is the same.

8. **nit**: missing tests.
   - No test makes the first read fail and then shows a later tick read succeed.
   - No facade-level test joins a link that has already expired.
   - No test joins the same link twice on one device.

9. **nit**: `accept.rs:542-554`
   - **Defect:** The previous release refuses unknown keys, so it cannot read a list that holds `linkSecret`.
   - **Scenario:** A rollback to the previous build cannot load the whole received-shares list. ADR 0020 D3 requires only the forward direction, so put this in the release notes.
   - **Outside the diff:** `crates/core/src/payload/pointer.rs:182-218` does not compare the decoded `object.scope_id` with the expected id. Only the AAD binds it. Only the owner can exploit this.

## What the small changes do

- **`rotation.rs`:** carries the link terms through a write-wave re-mint (see finding 4).
- **`trigger.rs`:** removes stale doc text only (the write-link upgrade branch and `RecordedInvite::expires_at`).
- **`staging.rs`:** removes `INVITE_RECORDS_PREFIX` from the bookkeeping prefixes. The staging cleanup will now delete the retired invite records.
- **`revocation.rs`:** adds `ResolutionClass::Expired`, with the wire name `"expired"`.
- **`mount_convergence.rs`:** removes the invite-link arm of the write-share tests, because a link no longer runs a write-scope cut (ADR 0024 D4).

## Items 1-14

1. **CONFIRMED.**
   - The owner signature on the re-point object is checked before any record read or blob open. The chain is `received_status.rs:406` → `621` → `link_read.rs:100-107` → `pointer_fetch.rs:118-125`. `open_repoint` uses the fragment's `pointerReadKey` and the identity of the fragment code, and `facade.rs:9022` verifies that code's binding first. The record read comes later, at `received_status.rs:807`.
   - Implemented order: 1 (`facade.rs:9019`), 2 (`9022`), 4 (`9026`), claim post (`9045`), 9 (`9076`), 3 (`received_status.rs:621`), record verify (`807`), 5 (`1048`), 6 (`754-775`), 7 (`897`), 8 (`921`).
   - No check is missing. Checks 4 and 9 run earlier than the ADR order. D1 needs the early persist so that the tick can retry. The cost: a fragment whose pointer never resolves leaves a row that stays unresolvable.
2. **CONFIRMED.** The pass heals the name (`630`, `646`) and then resolves and verifies the record at that name (`806-807`). The link path reads no tombstone and no mailbox mirror. Caveat: finding 3.
3. **CONFIRMED.** The join checks the fragment scope id at `facade.rs:9026`. The record scope must equal the share scope (`received_status.rs:1029`, `869`). The own-tree and own-scope guards are at `881` and `523`.
4. **CONFIRMED.** The link tag comes from `recipient_blinded_tag(invitee.enc_secret(), owner enc subkey, share.scope_root_name)` at `756`, `1048` and `885`, with the name healed each pass. Test: `received_status.rs:3683` (no real wave; see finding 4).
5. **CONFIRMED** in code, with one test gap.
   - Every pass checks the link kind and the deadline (`754-775`). Expiry is `now &gt;= deadline`.
   - `now` comes from the injected scheduler at `facade.rs:7344`, and the slice calls no clock directly.
   - The join's check is its forced pass (`9081`).
   - Tests: `3733` (expired at `now == deadline`) and `3753` (revoked before the deadline). No test covers an expired link at join through the facade.
6. **CONFIRMED.**
   - The floors are keyed under the fragment owner's contact label (`678-683`), and the gate runs at `921`.
   - The join persists at `facade.rs:9076`, before its forced pass at `9081`.
   - The equal-floor short-circuit is at `received_status.rs:924-934`. No test covers a second join.
7. **CONFIRMED**, with the defect in finding 1.
   - The refresh prefers the personal tag (`718-720`). The drop is at `475-478` and persists at `573-591`, and the personal accept also drops at `accept.rs:1116`.
   - Test `received_status.rs:3710` reloads the persisted list and asserts `link_hold` is `None`. This means `linkSecret` is gone, and no secret bytes appear in the assertion.
8. **CONFIRMED.**
   - Removed: a grantee's bookmark classifies to `RevocationSignal` with `viaLink` false.
   - Expired: set at `761-762`, at `772-773` (link gone after the last verified deadline), and at `289-293` (unresolvable after that deadline).
   - Revoked: `RevocationSignal` with `viaLink` true, mapped in `receivedShares.ts`.
   - The expired rule overrides the blob result, so a cut at the deadline reads expired (test `3733`). An expired link reads as revoked only when no deadline was ever verified, or after a re-join (finding 7).
9. **Partly REFUTED.**
   - These go fail-closed and emit a trust event:
     - A bad re-point signature or a wrong scope returns `Rejected` (`pointer_fetch.rs:125`, `132`). The pass emits a trust violation and shows `Unresolvable` (`received_status.rs:634-640`, `450-459`).
     - A record scope mismatch is `Trust(SealOpenFailed)` (`1029-1033`).
     - A commitment that fails to verify is `Trust` (`1040-1044`).
     - A blob that fails to open is `Trust` (`897-903`).
   - A commitment that does not name the tag, or names it as another kind, gives `RevocationSignal` (`768`). This matches the blueprint rule that a fresh owner-signed record with no blob at your tag is the revocation signal, so it is not a defect.
   - The own-root refusal is `UnsupportedTarget` (finding 6).
   - Availability is kept separate: an absent pointer (`pointer_fetch.rs:115`), an unavailable pointer (`116`) and an unresolvable record (`807`) raise no trust event.
10. **CONFIRMED.** `STORED_LIST_V = 2` (`accept.rs:399`). The frozen v2 byte test at `1556-1582` is unchanged, and the new test at `1602` loads a bookmark without link keys.
11. **Code CONFIRMED, test REFUTED.** The claim command discards the read result (`facade.rs:9081-9083`). No test makes a read fail and then shows a later tick read. `received_status.rs:3781` shows only that the hold stays for the next pass.
12. **CONFIRMED.**
    - The secret types clear their bytes on drop.
    - Both the encode tree and the decode tree are cleared (`accept.rs:510`, `525`), and so is the decode array (`bytes.zeroize()`).
    - `HeldPointerKeys` returns a `Zeroizing` copy.
    - The test at `accept.rs:1614` compares two `LinkHold` values with `assert!`, not `assert_eq!`, so a failure prints no secret.
    - The non-test code in the slice has no `unwrap` or `expect` on untrusted input.
13. **CONFIRMED clean.** No added line has a `#NNNN` reference. I found no absence-justifying comment and no long workaround apologia.
14. **CONFIRMED.** The diff adds no new test file. All the new tests are in library unit modules or in existing `tests/*.rs` files. The job "Engine simulation tests" runs them with `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` (`.github/workflows/ci-rust.yml:101`), and the `Rust Result` gate in `ci.yml` requires that job to pass.
