- `crates/engine/src/facade/claim_conversion.rs`: a lock that holds the conversion pass off during a cut.
- Smaller edits: `grants/invite.rs` (the `CommittedScope` fields become `pub(super)`, the same change as #1987), `grants/mod.rs`, `owner_keys.rs`, `profile.rs`, `rotation/mod.rs`.
- Tests in `rotation/{trigger,cascade}/tests.rs`, `tests/owner_actions.rs` (10 new tests) and `tests/facade.rs`.
- The new flag is passed through `crates/wasm/src/lib.rs` and `packages/client/src/{facade.ts, worker/*}`.

**Design choices the issues left open:**
- `Command::RevokeInviteLink` gets `remove_grantees: bool`. In the client it is `revokeInviteLink(node, removeGrantees = false)`, and the web app does not change.
- `Revoke` and `Downgrade` now find the person on the owner-attested ledger row, not in the contact book. At a node that is not a scope root they now fail with the command's target refusal instead of `recipient-not-imported`. I updated the facade test for this.
- A person revoke runs the conversion pass first. It refuses with `link-has-a-pending-conversion` while a conversion through the admitting link is pending.
- The D3 clear is durable. Each cut records the cut epoch of the set that removes the recipient. A re-commit at or above that epoch raises a new `/cleared/` floor, so the grant floor does not change (this matches `CONTEXT.md`). The floor store cannot delete keys, so the clear is a raise.
- The sweep runs inside the tick, after the conversion pass. Its cadence is a new profile field, `link_sweep_cadence`: 600 s in production, 3 s in CI.
- The sweep resolves each scope root once, and resolves it again right before each cut. It checks the vault root too, and it cuts the deepest scope roots first.
- The revoke paths and the sweep hold the conversion flag for the whole cut. Without this, a pass could ack a claim through a link while that link is being cut, and the claim would be lost. I added this after self-review.

**Left out, and why:**
- **Choosing one link out of several:** link revoke still acts on the single link on a scope. When #1987 lets links coexist, the command needs a way to name the link.
- **End-to-end test for "device B stops withholding P":** to commit P again, device A needs the append path from #1987. The rule is tested at the cascade level instead.
- **Two-device concurrent test:** the test runs the two owner devices in sequence. The case where both sign the same sequence needs #1982, which is still open and not in this stack. After #1982 lands, that test should be tightened.
- **Expired write link with a pending write claim:** the tick cannot run a write-scope cut. Such a link waits until the share dialog converts the claim, then the next sweep cuts it. This errs on the safe side.
- **Owner phone test:** a freshly booted owner device needs one tick before it can revoke. I added the tick to that test. Without it the revoke fails with "descendant record unavailable".
- **Merge conflicts with #1987:** expect them in `cut_recipient` and `remint_grants`. #1987 also switches `Downgrade` to its own row lookup (`held_row`).</result>

1. **`received_status.rs:309`, expired links keep read access:** answered, not changed. ADR 0025 D2 gives the cut of an expired link to the owner sweep (batch E). Until then the holder's engine stops reading at the deadline and removes the read seed from memory.

A later failure of the parent, interior or descendant publishes no longer fails the mint. A later mint of the same folder finishes that work against the committed link entry, then refuses a second link with `invite-target-already-names-a-scope`.

3. **`received_status.rs:789`, a personal blob that does not open blocks the link:** fixed. The holder reads the personal tag only when that blob opens. Otherwise it reads through the link, on every pass, and keeps the link keys.
4. **`received_status.rs:459`, later links never refresh:** fixed. Capped passes now go least recently refreshed first, so every bookmark is reached in turn. The test fails without the change.

<result>I confirmed the hard violations below on HEAD. I found no tracker refs, no direct clock or RNG use, no zeroize problem, no encode/decode asymmetry and no TS enum. All new tests block a merge through a named gate.

## (a) Standards violations

**Hard (AGENTS.md "Code Style", rule: "State genuine non-obvious domain rationale once, at its home")**
- The ADR 0023 D4 rationale ("the cut removes the link row the conversion reads, so the cut and the pass exclude each other") is written again at six new sites:
  - `crates/engine/src/facade/claim_conversion.rs:121-123`
  - `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:9212-9215`
  - `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:7424-7425`
  - `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:8349-8351`
  - `crates/engine/src/facade/link_sweep.rs:7-10`
  - `crates/engine/src/grants/cut_set.rs:105-107`
- Its home is the `Running` doc. The other sites should give a bare cross-reference.

**Judgement calls**
- **Possible absence-justifying comments.** These lines describe a path the code no longer has, and will rot if a contact-book read comes back:
  - `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:8346`: "no contact book is read"
  - `crates/engine/src/grants/cut_set.rs:6`: "never by the device-local contact book"
- **Terminology (CONTEXT.md).** "checkbox" is a UI word. The engine field is `remove_grantees`. It appears at:
  - `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs:5182`, `:5210`, `:5213`, `:5283`
  - `crates/engine/src/grants/cut_set.rs:300`
- **Test message.** `crates/engine/src/profile.rs:185` says "the 30 s tick". The CI profile tick is 1 s (`profile.rs:119`), so the message is wrong for that profile.
- **Assertion on a fixture, not behaviour.** `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs:5231`, `assert_eq!(claimants.len(), 2);`, only uses a binding that is otherwise unused.

## (b) Smells

- **Duplicated Code: sweep cut vs `drive_cut`.**
  - The `OwnerCutNet { … }` literal at `crates/engine/src/facade/link_sweep.rs:246-271` repeats `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:8511-8536` field for field.
  - `rotate_on_cut` followed by `record_cut_epoch_floor(...)` repeats `facade.rs:8537-8551`.
  - The copy drops the write-report bookkeeping and hard-codes `vault_pointer_signer: None` (`link_sweep.rs:264`).
  - This is safe only because a link entry is "Always committed at `read`" (CONTEXT.md:66), so `revoke_grants` gives `write: false`.
  - Nothing in the code asserts that. If `report.write` is `Some`, the sweep drops it without a signal.
  - Suggestion: share the net-plus-floor step, or refuse release-active when `report.write.is_some()`.
- **Duplicated Code: `net()`.** `link_sweep.rs:64-90` is the same as `claim_conversion.rs:167-191`, except for `ancestry`.
- **Duplicated Code: loading the pending senders.** The code reads `load_conversions(...).pending().map(|claim| claim.sender).collect()` in two places: `facade.rs:9226-9235` and `link_sweep.rs:121-133`.
- **Duplicated Code: `cut_set.rs`.** The predicate "owner-attested &amp;&amp; committed as `Personal`" appears twice, at `cut_set.rs:29-35` and `:93-97`. Extract one helper.
- **Duplicated Code: tick cadence check.** `sweep_due = now.0 &lt; swept_at.0 || elapsed_at_least(...)` at `facade.rs:7427-7428` repeats `facade.rs:6641-6642`, but without the backward-clock rationale.
- **Data Clumps.** `LinkSweep` (`link_sweep.rs:20-41`, 18 fields) carries the same seam bundle as `ConversionPass` (`claim_conversion.rs:96-112`). The tick builds it again at `facade.rs:7438-7457`.
- **Efficiency.** The signer, keys and sweep factory are cloned on every tick before the `sweep_due` check (`facade.rs:7429-7431`).
- **Mysterious Name.** `PersonCut` and `committed_person`: CONTEXT.md uses the term **Grantee**, so `GranteeCut` would match the glossary.

<summary>Agent "Find vulns in stacked diff" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I found 2 should-fix and 3 nit findings, and no blockers. Scope was the stacked diff `9a2fef5b3...HEAD` only (21 files); I made no writes of any kind.

## Findings

**1. should-fix: the D3 clear can undo a cut that never published.** `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/worktrees/agent-a9ff3b5e78b5c6b87/crates/engine/src/rotation/cascade.rs:730` (record) and `:557` (clear). Confidence 7.
- **Defect:** `/revoked-at-cut/` is written in `record_revocation_floor`, before `publish_scope_root` at `:747`. It records the cut epoch N of a set that may never land. A later set this same device signs from the pre-cut base N-1 also carries cut epoch N, and N &gt;= N clears the cut.
- **Scenario:**
  - The owner revokes R' at scope S. The per-recipient floor and `cut_at = N` persist. The publish then fails: a crash, a `NotPublished` after the server took the PUT (lost ack), or a `LostRace`.
  - `record_cut_epoch_floor` never runs, so the device's cut-epoch floor stays N-1. The device re-adopts the N-1 set. A surviving write grantee can also republish it, which the gate accepts at floor N-1.
  - The owner then revokes X at S. `revoke_grants` signs cut epoch N and still commits R'. `effective_revoked_recipients` sees `N &gt;= cut_at(N)`, raises `/cleared/` and serves R' a blob under the fresh seed.
  - Before this diff, R' stayed withheld. That was the "a crash between the two leaves the cut remembered" guarantee at `cascade.rs:728`, and this diff removes it.
- **Fix:** write `/revoked-at-cut/` only after the publish confirms, next to `record_cut_epoch_floor` in `drive_cut` and in `LinkSweep::cut`, or right after `:747`. Keep the revocation floor itself before the publish. A missing key already never clears, so a failed or lost-ack cut stays restrictive. Add a test: record the floor, fail the publish, then run a second cut from the N-1 base. R' must stay withheld.

**2. should-fix: one claim can stop the sweep from ever cutting an expired write link.** `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/worktrees/agent-a9ff3b5e78b5c6b87/crates/engine/src/facade/claim_conversion.rs:354`, together with `crates/engine/src/grants/cut_set.rs` `expired_links` and `facade/link_sweep.rs:134`. Confidence 7.
- **Defect:** the sweep relies on the tick's conversion pass to clear pending claims. The tick uses `TickSites::cut_write_scope`, which always returns Seam. So a Write-permission claim at a scope still at `MINT_EPOCH` stays pending on every tick. `expired_links` then skips that link forever.
- **Scenario:** anyone holding a leaked Write link posts a claim just before the deadline. The tick acks it, and `convert_at` fails at the write-scope cut, so the entry stays pending. `acked_at` is before the deadline, so the claim stays convertible. The sweep excludes the link on every pass. The holder keeps reading through the link blob past the deadline until the owner opens the share dialog, runs "convert claims", or revokes by hand. ADR 0025 E1 bounds this window at "until the owner's sweep runs"; here the sweep runs and skips the link.
- **Fix:** before the sweep cuts a folder with due links, have it run a conversion whose `cut_write_scope` drives an `OwnerCutNet`. `LinkSweep` already holds `owner_pointer_seed` and builds one. Alternatively, emit an owner event for "expired link held by a stuck conversion".

**3. nit: committed_person cuts only the first row for an identity.** `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/worktrees/agent-a9ff3b5e78b5c6b87/crates/engine/src/grants/cut_set.rs:28`. Confidence 4.
- **Defect:** `.find` returns one row per identity. Two owner-attested rows for one identity under different encryption keys (different tags) leave the second row standing, and the revoke still reports success.
- **Scenario:** hostile person H claims a link with contact code K2 and is converted, so the row is keyed by K2. The owner also grants H directly from an imported contact K1. `create_grant` has no identity de-dup; only conversion de-dups by identity (`invite.rs:985`). Revoking H removes one row and H keeps the other.
- This is not a regression: the old contact-key path also cut one tag. But this diff owns the function.
- **Fix:** collect every matching row into the cut set. For Downgrade, refuse when more than one row matches.

**4. nit: a hostile index entry can hide any deeper scope from the sweep, not only nested ones.** `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/worktrees/agent-a9ff3b5e78b5c6b87/crates/engine/src/facade/link_sweep.rs:180`. Confidence 5.
- **Defect:** `visited` is marked before the resolve. The shared `RotationAncestry::record` also keeps the first parent it sees.
- **Scenario:** a co-writer of a shallow scope P adds Q's `scope_id` to P's `directChildScopeIndex`, with any name. Q can be anywhere deeper in the vault. The walk reaches the bogus entry first, the resolve fails, and Q is marked visited. The real path to Q is skipped, so Q's whole subtree is never swept.
- This goes beyond ADR 0025 E3, which covers only scopes nested under the grantee's own scope.
- There is no privilege escalation: `cut()` re-resolves under the walk-derived parent seed, and the ascent check fails closed.
- **Fix:** mark visited only after a successful gated resolve, and let a later parent take over from a parent whose child resolve failed. Otherwise, widen E3 in the ADR.

**5. nit: an early Seam hides a later trust violation from the abuse event.** `link_sweep.rs:141` and `:206`, and `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/worktrees/agent-a9ff3b5e78b5c6b87/crates/engine/src/facade.rs:7467`. Confidence 8.
- **Defect:** `report.failure.get_or_insert` keeps only the first error.
- **Scenario:** one scope is unreachable (Seam) and a later one is rejected by the gate (TrustViolation). No `AttributableAbuse` event is emitted for the rejected one.
- **Fix:** give a TrustViolation priority when recording a failure, or keep it separately.

## Focus areas checked and clean

1. **Recipient key comes only from owner-attested rows:**
   - `committed_person` and `link_cut_set` require `row_is_owner_attested` plus a Personal commitment entry (`cut_set.rs:28-35`, `:86-96`).
   - `via_link` is inside the signed binding (`crates/core/src/seal/write_body.rs:275`), so a co-writer cannot forge it.
   - The admitting link comes from `committed_links` under owner authority (`cut_set.rs:58-62`).
   - The command supplies only the identity, and its length is checked (`facade.rs` `cut_recipient`).
2. **Fail-closed:**
   - `revoke_grants` refuses an empty or uncommitted tag set and matches the old read/write arm (`trigger.rs:500-516`).
   - The pending-conversion refusals are Seam, not success.
   - Sweep cut failures are reported and retried.
   - I found no trust verdict turned into a success. The only downgrade is the event masking in finding 5.
3. **D3 clear:**
   - A missing `/revoked-at-cut/` key never clears, so older cuts stay restrictive (`cascade.rs:557`, `is_some_and`).
   - Floor writes are raise-to-max.
   - The keys cannot collide: the tails differ at byte 9 (`/revoked/` vs `/revoked-at-cut/`), and `no_two_epoch_namespace_key_shapes_collide` covers them.
   - `/cleared/` only feeds `granted.max(cleared)`, so it lowers no grant floor.
   - A later re-cut raises the revocation floor above the cleared value and `cut_at` to the new epoch, so the clear cannot bring the recipient back.
   - A replayed lower-epoch set is stopped by the gate, and would fail `&gt;=` anyway.
   - Two owner devices signing different sets at the same epoch is the #1959 residual.
4. **`Running::hold`:**
   - It never waits and is an RAII guard, released on return, error, `?`, cancellation and unwind (`claim_conversion.rs:124-137`).
   - The tick binds `_held` for the whole sweep (`facade.rs` tick).
   - The sweep and cut paths never call the pass again, so there is no re-entry and no deadlock.
   - Pending claims are read after the hold, with no await between the conversion and the hold. That closes the ack-then-cut race: claims acked later meet a set with no link row and are refused.
5. **Sweep resource use:**
   - Each index is capped at `MAX_DIRECT_CHILD_SCOPES` (`crates/core/src/seal/write_body.rs:620`).
   - Dedup by `scope_id` ends cycles.
   - Only gate-passing, owner-signed records expand the frontier, so an entry pointing outside the vault fails the resolve and stops there.
   - Total work is bounded by (real scope roots × index cap) per 600 s cadence.
6. **Determinism:** the new code has no `SystemTime`, `Instant` or `rand`. Time comes from `scheduler.now()` and entropy from the injected `RefCell&lt;Box&lt;dyn Entropy&gt;&gt;`.
7. **Logging and key handling:** the new code adds no logging. The abuse event carries only the TrustViolation message. The `pointer_seed()` accessor is `pub(crate)` and borrow-only.
8. **Codec:** `flag()` refuses a non-boolean `removeGrantees` before the call reaches WASM (`packages/client/src/worker/commandCodec.ts`).</result>

## New observations from the fold (none blocks)

1. nit `facade.rs:9209-9212`: while a tick pass holds `running`, `RevokeInviteLink` answers `link-has-a-pending-conversion`. The cause is a busy pass, not a pending conversion; `retire_refused` answers `CONVERSION_RUNNING` for the same state. Answer `CONVERSION_RUNNING` here too, so a host retries instead of hunting for a claim that is not pending.
2. nit `claim_conversion.rs:263-265`: `run` answers `Ok(())` when a pass already runs, so `ConvertInviteClaims` reports `Done` and converts nothing; the tick converts anyway. A `CONVERSION_RUNNING` seam error would be more honest.
3. observation, not new to the fold: two owner devices that each hold a different write claim at one read-only folder can both reach `cut_write_scope` in one window. The strictly-above-observed publish rule decides the loser; the E1 ack lock serialises one claim, not one folder. The PR body "Known windows" could name it.
4. nit `accept.rs:568-570`: every `InviteClaim::encode` error on a held claim maps to `Malformed::InvalidGranteeName`; the claim encoder has only that failure today, so the label holds.

## Crypto-privacy review (targeted: conversion.rs, claim_conversion.rs, InviteClaim codec)

Risk: LOW. Nothing to fix.

- The record is sealed at rest under the registered owner-local kind `PendingConversions` (0x07, HPKE-to-self through `BookkeepingSeal`), keyed by the owner tag; the KAT manifest gains accept and reject vectors. The quarantine keeps only the sealed blob; no plaintext reaches staging or the event.
- Trust boundary holds: `AckedClaim.sender` is the verified mailbox sender identity; conversion binds it to the owner-attested link row (`committed_links` under `OwnerAuthority`, `enforce_committed_ledger`), and a set the owner did not sign is a `TrustViolation`. The deadline verdict uses the injected `scheduler.now()` ack time.
- Encode/decode symmetry: `within("claim")` and `within("entries")` fire on both ends with release-active `Err`; `claimant_name` refuses on encode and decode; `FORMAT_V` and unknown keys reject on decode. Unit and release tests cover it.
- No secret material in assertions, errors or Debug: `InviteClaim` redacts `claim_id`, `contact_code`, `name`; `TooLong` display carries field name and lengths only; events carry no bytes.
- Determinism: no clock or RNG in the module; entropy enters through `self.entropy` and `SharedEntropy`.
- DoS: intake acks at most `MAX_CONVERSION_ENTRIES` per pass and drops an oversized payload after the ack; the admission cap bounds grants per link; refused entries cap at 64 with one event per eviction.
- Optional test ideas: a record sealed under another identity's `enc_secret` is quarantined, not opened; a det-CBOR payload with a duplicate key rejects at `decode_conversions`.

<result>All five fixes are in, and every gate passed on the final tree. Nothing is committed: this is working-tree changes only, as the brief asked.

**New and changed public Rust items**
- `SharingInviteLinks` is removed. The new `SharingInviteLink` has these fields:
  - `tag: Vec&lt;u8&gt;`
  - `permission: Permission`
  - `expires_at: UnixMillis`
  - `expired: bool`
  - `admission_cap: u64`
  - `pending_claims: u32`
- `ScopeSharing.invite_links` is now `Vec&lt;SharingInviteLink&gt;`. It lists every committed link in commitment order, expired ones included. The old `live` flag is gone, because a non-empty list says the same thing, so no flag was needed on `ScopeSharing`.
- `invite_link_refusal` now reports `invite-link-contact-budget-full` when any one link's budget is full. Before, only the sole link was checked.
- `Command::RevokeInviteLink { node, link_tag: Option&lt;Vec&lt;u8&gt;&gt; }`:
  - A tag that names no committed link is refused with `link-not-committed`. A tag that is not 32 bytes gets the same refusal.
  - With no tag and one link, the behaviour is unchanged.
  - With no tag and more than one link, it is refused with `link-ambiguous`.
- New check `InviteError::LinkAmbiguous` = `link-ambiguous`. It sits in `CHECKS` after `link-not-committed`, has the class "capability", and maps to `EngineError::MalformedInput`.
- New check `GrantEditError::RecipientKeyChanged` = `grant-recipient-key-changed`. It is last in `CHECKS`, has the class "capability", and maps to `MalformedInput`, the same as `SamePermission` and `NotGranted`.
- `HeldRow` gains `recipient_enc_pk: [u8; 32]`, and `CommittedLink` gains `admission_cap: u64`.
- `sole_link` is removed. `locate_invite_link` now also takes the optional tag.
- New event `Event::GranteeNamesCleared`, with no payload. In wasm its kind is `"granteeNamesCleared"`. No existing event fitted a corrupt local store.
- `StagingGranteeNameCache::clear()` is new.

**Pending claims and conversion**
- The claim count is now split per link, so each link's count is exact. Each counted claim now also records its signer, which is the link's ephemeral identity. The folder-row total is unchanged.
- `ConvertInviteClaims` needed no change, because conversion already matches each claim to its link by the signer.
- The permission-change path of a Grant posts no pointer, so the key-changed guard is on the same-permission retry only.

**One point for the security reviewer**
- The same-permission retry now raises the grant floor before the pointer post. That raise has no new publish behind it.
- I think this is safe: the row is owner-attested, its key must equal the contact's current key, and the owner's own command is the decision to grant.
- I reworded the rule in the `record_grant_floor` doc (`crates/engine/src/rotation/cascade.rs`) to allow this case.

**Tests** (in `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs`; all pass):
- `two_links_are_both_listed_and_a_revoke_by_tag_cuts_only_that_one` (it also checks the per-link claim count)
- `a_revoke_with_no_tag_over_two_links_is_refused_and_publishes_nothing` (it also covers an unknown tag)
- `a_grant_retried_after_a_failed_floor_raise_raises_the_floor`
- `a_grant_retry_to_a_contact_whose_encryption_key_changed_is_refused`
- `a_name_the_row_cannot_carry_is_ignored_on_a_retry_and_refused_on_a_mint`
- `a_name_cache_that_does_not_open_is_cleared_and_the_sharing_read_goes_on`
- The existing one-link, no-tag revoke tests still pass, with only the new `link_tag: None` field added.
- The unit test in `invite.rs` is renamed to `two_links_locate_only_the_one_a_tag_names`.

The existing floor-fault injector `fail_floor_raises_for` was enough, so no new one was added. The KAT reject corpus lists only the checks its vectors hit, so no vector files changed. `kat_checks.rs` now lists both new checks.

**Wasm** (only as much as needed to compile):
- `crates/wasm/src/lib.rs`: the wrapper is now `SharingInviteLink`, `inviteLinks` returns an array, `revokeInviteLink(node, linkTag?)` takes an optional tag, and the new event kind is added.
- `crates/wasm/src/host.rs`: the test is updated to match.
- Nothing under `packages/` or `apps/` was touched.

**Gates**
- `cargo fmt --all --check`: passed.
- `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine`: passed.
- `cargo test --workspace --exclude fuser --exclude cipherbox-desktop`: passed.
- Workspace clippy with `-D warnings`: passed.
- wasm32 clippy for core, engine and wasm with `-D warnings`: passed.
- Engine `kat_gen` run twice, and core `kat_gen` run twice: no drift.
- `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`: passed.

The edit scripts and gate logs are in `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/w1963/F/gA/`.</result>

- **Branch:** `feat/1975-engine-link-preview`
- **PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1991 (draft, `Closes #1975.`)
- **Head SHA:** `5c344aaab2c44de312ec3eb2b7bcdd1ffcfc0ebd`
- **CI:** all checks pass, including Engine simulation tests, Core KATs (native + WASM), Fmt + clippy, Workspace tests, Web, Web E2E Smoke and Tracker Refs. Desktop Build was skipped by the workflow. CodeRabbit skipped the draft.

**Files changed** (all under `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/worktrees/laneH-1975/`):
- Engine: `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`, `crates/engine/src/grants/link_read.rs`, `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`, `crates/engine/src/seams/floor_store.rs`, `crates/engine/src/seams/mod.rs`.
- Test kit: `crates/engine/src/testkit/world.rs`, `crates/engine/src/testkit/mod.rs`, and `crates/engine/src/testkit/fakes/{credential_store,floor_store,mailbox,mod,received_share_store,record_store,snapshot_cache,staging_store}.rs`.
- Tests: `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs`.
- wasm: `crates/wasm/src/lib.rs`, `crates/wasm/src/host.rs`, `crates/wasm/tests/boundary.rs`.
- Client: `packages/client/src/{facade,index,testkit}.ts`, `packages/client/src/worker/{protocol,engineWasm,commandCodec,engineHost}.ts`, and the tests `facade.test.ts`, `commandCodec.test.ts`, `engineHost.test.ts`, `serve.test.ts`.

**Design choices the issue left open:**
- **A read, not a command.** The preview is `Engine::preview_invite_link(&amp;self, fragment)`, placed next to `received_shares`. It does not go through the `Command` enum. This keeps it off the claim and grant commands that lanes E and F change. On the client it is the read kind `invitePreview` plus `EngineFacade.previewInviteLink`.
- **No-persist wrapper.** `NoPersistFloorStore` reads through to the real floor store. A raise returns the value it would reach and writes nothing, and `clear` is refused. The floor store is the only store the link read path writes to. `preview_read` wraps the floors itself, so a caller cannot skip the wrapper.
- **Shared read.** The join and the preview share one private function, `read_link_entry`. `join_read` maps its result onto the existing `JoinRead`. The join's behaviour does not change.
- **Already joined.** I added `joined: bool`, true when this account already holds a bookmark. `blueprint/engine.md` and ADR 0028 D5 ask for this state. The lane brief listed only live, expired, revoked and unresolvable.
- **Opening an adopted root.** When the account already joined, the gate refuses the root because its sequence is not newer than the floor. The preview handles this the same way the tick's equal-floor recovery does, so a joined link still shows its listing.
- **States.** A root that commits the link entry but has no grant blob at its tag previews as `revoked`. A failed commitment verify maps to `unresolvable`, the same as the join.
- **Result shape.** The names are `Option&lt;InviteNames&gt;`; in TypeScript they are `{ ownerName, folderName } | null`. The permission is `null` when the preview read no link entry. The listing is empty unless the state is `live`.
- **Test helper.** `FakeDevice::durable_state()` collects every fake store and the shared world, so the "no store changed" tests can compare before and after. The type has no `Debug`, so a failed comparison prints no key material.

**Checks that the tests are real:** I broke the code on purpose two times. When the preview ran over the plain floors, the "no store changed" test failed. When I removed the equal-floor handling, the "already joined" test failed.

**What I left out, and why:**
- The claim and the preview both repeat about 8 lines that decode the fragment and check it. I did not move them into a shared helper, because that edits `claim_invite_link` and lanes E and F edit that function. The fix after those lanes merge is a small refactor.
- No `apps/web` invite-page UI. The issue covers the engine only.
- I did not run the `/simplify`, `/security-review` or `/crypto-privacy-review` skills, because the brief gives review to a separate reviewer. The diff touches the trust boundary (the gate and the grant-blob open), so a crypto and privacy review is needed.

**Local runs, all passing:** `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine`, the workspace tests (excluding desktop, contract, fuser), `cargo fmt --all --check`, clippy with `-D warnings` (native and wasm32), the wasm boundary tests, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`, the client unit tests (754) and the web unit tests (776).</result>

<result># Review of PR 1991 — feat(engine): preview a link before join (head 5c344aaab, closes #1975)

Branch `review/1991` in my worktree, diff `origin/main...HEAD`, tree clean, no git or file writes made. All four gates ran inline (the brief forbids nested sub-agents, so each skill's fan-out was done by hand against its checklist). Local runs: the 8 preview tests and `a_no_persist_view_reads_through_and_writes_nothing` pass; `packages/client` 756/756 pass; `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` clean; CI green.

## Findings

### F1 — should-fix — `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs:5428` — the equal-floor path has no "wrote nothing" assertion

`a_preview_of_a_joined_link_reads_the_root_the_join_adopted` is the only test that drives `open_through_link` through the `SequenceNotNewer` arm (link_read.rs:390-401) and the pointer consult over floors that already hold a write-epoch value. It asserts `joined`, `state` and the listing, but not `durable_state()`. Failure scenario: a later change drops the `NoPersistFloorStore::over` wrap in `preview_read` (link_read.rs:289); the joined path then raises the sharer-scoped write-epoch floor through `PointerConsult::run` and no test fails. Fix (test only):

```rust
     let before = fx.recipient_device.durable_state();
     let preview = preview(&amp;holder, &amp;fragment).expect("the preview reads");
     assert!(preview.joined);
     assert_eq!(preview.state, LinkPreviewState::Live);
     assert_eq!(preview.listing.len(), 1);
+    assert!(fx.recipient_device.durable_state() == before, "the joined preview wrote nothing");
```

### F2 — should-fix — `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:9131-9156` vs `10706-10731` — share the link-opening prelude now

The decode / `EphemeralInvitee::from_secret` / `import_contact` / own-root refusal block (8 lines) and the `pending_link_bookmark` + `SharerScopedFloorStore::granted_by` + `JoinSeams` block (11 lines) are copied verbatim between `claim_invite_link` and `preview_invite_link`. Judgement on the deferral: share now. Issue #1975 says "PR batch F, alone", the helper is a pure private method, and the conflict it causes in a sibling lane that edits `claim_invite_link` is one hunk at the top of that function. Proposed shape (facade.rs, private):

```rust
struct LinkOpening { fragment: InviteFragment, invitee: EphemeralInvitee, owner: Contact }
fn open_link_fragment(&amp;self, fragment: &amp;str) -&gt; Result&lt;LinkOpening, EngineError&gt; { /* the 8 lines */ }
```

If the orchestrator keeps the deferral, record it as a fold into the lane that next touches `claim_invite_link`, not a new issue.

### F3 — nit — `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:666` — `InviteNames` collides with `cipherbox_core::payload::InviteNames`

Two public types named `InviteNames` now exist: the core signed-names struct that `InviteFragment::names()` returns (invite.rs:413) and the new facade result, both re-exported at crate roots. Rename the facade one to `PreviewNames`; the wasm/TS `names` field stays.

### F4 — nit — `crates/engine/src/grants/link_read.rs:220` — `unwrap_or(Permission::Read)` is unreachable

`GrantSetEntry::validate` (core `seal/grant.rs:917`) refuses a `Link` entry with no `conversionPermission`, so every decoded link entry carries one. The silent default would hide a future codec drift as "read". Prefer `let Some(p) = entry.conversion_permission else { return Ok(LinkEntryRead::Revoked) }`. Low weight.

### F5 — nit — `crates/engine/src/testkit/world.rs:127-135` — `DurableState` omits `name_registry`

`FakeDevice::name_registry` is the one device-held store the snapshot does not compare. The preview registers no name, so it cannot fail today; add it so the "every seam store" claim in the test name is complete.

### F6 — nit — `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:10717` and `10772` — the names signature verifies twice

`pending_link_bookmark` runs `verified_names` for the bookmark label, then the result builder runs it again. One ECDSA verify per preview is wasted; the F2 helper can return the verified pair once.

### F7 — nit, pre-existing (moved, not changed) — `crates/engine/src/grants/link_read.rs:205-213`

`verify_commitment_in_force` failure maps to `Unavailable`, as `join_read` did before. The refresh path (`received_status.rs:1130-1135`) maps the same failure to a `CommitmentVerify` gate rejection, which the standing rule reads as a trust violation. A holder of the write scope seed can publish a root record with a bad owner commitment and make the preview say "unresolvable" instead of raising a trust event; the effect equals withholding the record, so the impact is classification only. Outside the diff; note for the owner, do not block on it.

## Extra checks from the brief (all CONFIRMED clean)

- Writes: `preview_read` wraps `NoPersistFloorStore` itself (link_read.rs:289), so the pointer consult, `read_cut_epoch_floor`, and `adopt` → `PendingAdoption::commit` → `commit_floors` all write nothing. No `deposit_seed`, no `contact_store().record`, no `persist`, no `post_invite_claim`, no mailbox `ack`, no `file_forced_pass`; the test also asserts no spawned task. `DurableState` covers floors, staging ops + staged bytes (the contact store lives in staging), snapshot cache, credential, received shares, world records and every hub inbox (F5 is the one gap).
- Gate refusal → `PreviewRefusal::Gate` → `EngineError::from_gate(Rejected)` → `TrustViolation`; re-point refusal → `TrustViolation`; the forged-owner test proves both write nothing.
- Forged names: `verified_names` → `None`, state stays `Live`, listing still reads (test present).
- Listing: `PreviewEntry { name, kind }` only; the wasm boundary test asserts `size`, `id`, `ipnsName` have no getter. A `ReadBody::File` root yields an empty list.
- Equal-floor path: `adopt` fails at stage 4 after stages 1-3 (record verify, commitment verify, structure auth); the scope bind at line 352 restates stage 6; the seed-blob AAD and derived-read-key cross-checks are tautological for a blob opened from the same envelope; `sequence != floor || epoch &lt; epoch_floor` mirrors `received_status.rs:999-1000`. Sound, and it raises nothing under the wrapper.
- Fragment bound: `MAX_FRAGMENT_TEXT_LEN` (2732 chars) is checked before base64 decode, the blob is capped at 2048 bytes, then fixed-depth det-CBOR. The client refuses above 4096 chars in both the facade and the host. No loop or allocation scales with attacker input.
- No clock or RNG: only `self.seams.scheduler.now()`; no entropy is drawn.
- Membership probing (ADR 0024 E4): the blinded tag needs the invite secret from the fragment; `joined` reflects this account's own bookmark only; `permission` on `Expired` is what the holder can already read from the committed entry. No leak beyond E4.
- Comments: no tracker refs (gate passes), no absence-justifying prose, every new doc block is 5 lines or fewer.
- TS: `InvitePreviewState` and `Permission` are string literals; `previewState` fails closed on an unknown name; one name without the other is refused.
- PR hygiene: draft, no attribution in body or commits (grep = 0), bare `Closes #1975.`, parentheses in the title only for the scope.
- CI gates named: engine tests in **Engine simulation tests** (`cargo test -p cipherbox-engine`), wasm boundary in **Core KATs (native + WASM)**, client in **Web**.

  - New: `Event::GranteeNamesCleared`, `InviteError::LinkAmbiguous` (`link-ambiguous`) and `GrantEditError::RecipientKeyChanged` (`grant-recipient-key-changed`).
  - `locate_invite_link` takes a third argument, the tag, and `CommittedLink` gains `admission_cap`.
- **WASM:** `SharingInviteLink` replaces `SharingInviteLinks`, and `inviteLinks` is now an array. `Command.revokeInviteLink(node, linkTag?)` and the event kind `granteeNamesCleared` are new.
- **`packages/client`:** `SharingInviteLinkDescriptor` replaces `SharingInviteLinksDescriptor`. `inviteLinks` is now an array, and `revokeInviteLink(node, linkTag?)` sends `linkTag: null` when there is no tag. The `granteeNamesCleared` event descriptor is new.
- **`apps/web`:** There are labels for the two new checks. `inviteLinkState` shows the first link that has not expired and revokes it by its tag. `revokeInviteLink(linkTag?)` is in `useSharingActions`.

**Decision for you:** The fix for Greptile finding 2 raises the grant floor from a row that was read back from the resolved record. The caller rule on `record_grant_floor` says the recipient must never come from a resolved record. The fix changes that rule's doc so that a held row the owner grants again is allowed, because the grant command is the owner's own. Please review this change during the crypto and security pass.

<result># Review of PR 1987, fix commit a866d6f26..ff9d718a1

Head ff9d718a1, draft. `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine --release`: 2612 pass, 0 fail. `cargo fmt --all --check`: clean.

## Findings

### should-fix 1 — the sharing read reports a mint refusal the mint does not make

`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:11291-11298`. When any link at the scope has filled its per-link contact share (`link_budget_full`, `MAX_LINK_CONTACTS` = 128), the read sets the scope-wide `invite_link_refusal = invite-link-contact-budget-full`. The mint path (`share_scope` -&gt; `create_invite_link`) never checks this ground; the only producer is the contact store at claim conversion (`contact_store.rs:490,500`, mapped at `facade.rs:2194`). The field is documented as "the refusal an invite-link mint at this scope would report", so the read and the mint disagree. ADR 0026 D3 says a folder takes any number of live links, and `contact_store.rs:72-74` names the remedy: revoke the full link and mint a fresh one, which carries its own share.

Failure: link L1 at folder F has 128 link-sourced contacts and has expired. The read lists `[L1 expired]` and reports the refusal. `inviteLinkState` finds no unexpired link, so the web renders `share-no-mint` with "invite-link-contact-budget-full" and no mint control (`InviteLinkPanel.tsx:103-108`). The owner cannot take the documented remedy from the dialog, though the engine accepts the mint. With L1 live and full, the same refusal is hidden behind the `live` branch, so it is also not shown where it applies.

Fix: drop the scope-level override at 11291-11298 and add `contact_budget_full: bool` to `SharingInviteLink` (set from `link_budget_full(sources, &amp;link.tag)`), plus the same field on `SharingInviteLinkDescriptor`. The web slice (#1977) renders it on the link chip. Update the `the_sharing_read_...` tests and the doc comment at `facade.rs:3569-3575`.

### nit 2 — a name on a permission change is dropped without a test

`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:9080-9084`. The `Some(_)` arm (`apply_permission`) ignores `grantee_name`; the `owner_grantee_name` check now runs only in the `None` arm. The test `a_name_the_row_cannot_carry_is_ignored_on_a_retry_and_refused_on_a_mint` covers the same-permission retry, not the permission-change arm. Behaviour matches the PR body and ADR 0027 gives `RenameGrantee` for that case. No code change needed; add one assertion that a name on a permission change is not applied.

No other finding. `link_tag` of a wrong length maps to `link-not-committed` (fail-closed, `facade.rs:9490-9493`); `pending_link_claims` is bounded by the mailbox page and the set cap; `now` comes from the injected scheduler.

## A — TRUST: the retry floor raise (CONFIRMED safe)

Data trace at `facade.rs:9055-9080`:

1. The key the floor is raised for is `contact.enc_subkey()`, read from the owner's own contact book by the identity key the owner's command names (`recipient_contact` -&gt; `resolve_recipient`, `facade.rs:8221`). No byte of it comes from the network.
2. `held.recipient_enc_pk` comes from the resolved record, but only from a row that `row_is_owner_attested` accepts (`append.rs:117-127`): the owner's signature over the row bound to the scope's IPNS name. The record passed the adoption gate and `enforce_committed_ledger` before `held_grantee` runs. The row value is used only in an equality check that can refuse (`RecipientKeyChanged`); it never becomes the floor key.
3. Attacker outcomes. A network attacker or write-scope holder who alters a row's `recipient_enc_pk` breaks the owner signature, so `held_grantee` returns `None` and the mint path runs. A write-scope holder who republishes a pre-cut owner-signed set is refused at gate stage 2 on a device that recorded the cut (`blueprint/engine.md:190-191`, CONTEXT.md "Revocation floor"); on a device with no cut recorded, the raise changes nothing because `effective_revoked_recipients` withholds only when `cut &gt; granted` and that device holds no cut (`cascade.rs:519-530`). In every case the raise needs the owner's own `Grant` command naming that contact, so no floor rises for a key the owner never granted.
4. The caller-rule rewording at `cascade.rs:451-456` is correct: the recipient is still the command's contact, not a record field. The "after the publish" rule holds: the held row is on an adopted record, so a publish carried it. The floor law (CONTEXT.md:49) governs epoch floors on unseal; the grant floor is a device-local owner-intent record and stays outside it.

## B — one live link per scope (REFUTED as a rule; the refusal is wrong)

ADR 0026 D3: "A folder can have any number of live links. Each link has its own permission and its own lifetime." ADR 0025 D1 cuts one link row. The PR removed `sole_link` accordingly. Greptile's mechanism is imprecise: the check is the per-link contact share, not the admission cap. The conclusion stands: a scope-wide refusal for a per-link condition is wrong, and the mint never enforces it. See should-fix 1.

## C — the minimal web change (CONFIRMED safe to ship)

`inviteLink.ts:57-59` picks the first unexpired link; `InviteLinkPanel.tsx:62` passes `state.link.tag`; `useSharingActions.ts:116-120` forwards it; `facade.ts:415-419` sends `linkTag`; `commandCodec.ts:380-383` hands it to WASM; the engine cuts only the link whose owner-attested entry carries that tag (`invite.rs:800-815`, test `two_links_are_both_listed_and_a_revoke_by_tag_cuts_only_that_one`). No revoke goes to a link the owner did not see. With two live links the second is hidden until the first is cut, and `pending_claims` shows the first link's count only; #1977 replaces the dialog. Acceptable as an intermediate state.

## Gates

- /simplify: nothing to fix. No dead code (`sole_link` removed), no narrating or apologising comments, the `owner_grantee_name` check moved to one place.
- /security-review: one should-fix (finding 1, a misreport, no trust impact). Gate rejections stay TrustViolation; new refusals are MalformedInput on owner input; `link_tag` only selects among owner-signed link entries.
- /crypto-privacy-review: nothing to fix. The floor key holds a public key; the corrupt-cache path clears a sealed pre-fill only on `Unreadable` and reports once; tests assert public keys and epochs, no secret bytes.
- /code-review: each fix has a test in the "Engine simulation tests" gate (`cargo test -p cipherbox-engine`): `link-ambiguous` (`a_revoke_with_no_tag_over_two_links_is_refused_and_publishes_nothing`), `GranteeNamesCleared` (`a_name_cache_that_does_not_open_is_cleared_and_the_sharing_read_goes_on`), key changed (`a_grant_retry_to_a_contact_whose_encryption_key_changed_is_refused`), floor retry (`a_grant_retried_after_a_failed_floor_raise_raises_the_floor`), name-on-mint-only; `kat_checks.rs` covers the two new check names; `invite.rs` unit test covers tag selection. Client and web changes are covered in the Web gates. PR body: bare `Closes #1971.` / `Closes #1972.`, no attribution, no parentheses in the title. One nit (finding 2).

<result>PR 1988 is finished. The head is `96c446cac`, and CI is green on all 43 checks. Web E2E Smoke failed on the first run. I re-ran it once and it passed. I did not triage it.

**The four Greptile findings:**
1. **Failed delivery loses its retry:** fixed. A converted entry now stays in the record in a "pointer due" state. Each later pass posts the share pointer again, and the entry settles only when the post lands. There is one addition: a post that still fails `POINTER_RETRY_WINDOW` after the ack also settles the entry. The window is 40 posts × 24 h, which is 40 days. Without it, an account that the API does not hold would keep a record slot for ever.
2. **Ack can erase the claim:** fixed. The claim is written in an "acking" state before the DELETE runs. A retry converts an entry that is still in that state, whatever the DELETE answers. A fresh claim still needs `removed: true`.
3. **Failed re-point strands moved child:** fixed, but not with the parent sweep. The sweep cannot repair this case: it adds only a scope root that the index is missing, and it skips a root that the index names at a stale name. I tested this. The fix: when the next pass cannot resolve the folder at the name in the parent's index, it reads the owner-signed scope pointer. It resolves the moved root through the gate, re-points the parent index, and then converts. The cut now also reads the parent before the move.
   - Only a command pass (`ConvertInviteClaims` or `RevokeInviteLink`) does this repair. The tick does not.
   - The revoke and downgrade re-point on main has the same failure class and no repair. Both points are under "Known windows" in the PR body. I did not file an issue; you can decide if one is needed.
4. **Revoke ignores failed conversion:** fixed. A failed poll refuses the revoke with a retryable `Seam` error, `mailbox-unavailable`.

**The extra items:**
- **(a) Release-gate test:** I corrected the PR body row, and the test stays where it is. Moving it into `encode_refusals.rs` would make 4 crate-private items public.
- **(b) Failed-poll test:** `a_failed_poll_still_converts_the_claims_already_acked` is added.
- **(c) and (d) Pass already running:** `ConvertInviteClaims` and `RevokeInviteLink` now both answer `a-conversion-pass-is-running`.
- **(e) Known windows:** the entry for two owner devices at one read-only folder is in the PR body.

**Bug found on the PR head:** the test for (c) and (d) found a real bug. When `Running::take` failed, it still built a guard (because of eager `then_some`). Dropping that guard cleared the flag that the running pass held. It now uses `then(|| ...)`.

**Changed public items in `grants::conversion`:**
- Added: `EntryState`, `Hold`, `PointerDue`, `Verdict::PointerDue`, `POINTER_RETRY_WINDOW`, `ConversionEntry::converts()` and `refused()`.
- `ConversionEntry.refused` is replaced by `ConversionEntry.state`.
- `ConversionRecord::push` is replaced by `hold_for_ack` and `settle_ack`.
- The record now stores the `acking`, `pointerDue` and `grantFloor` fields. The decoder refuses any other field combination.

The test fake has `set_poll_failing` and `remember_recipient`. The facade types did not change, so I did not run the client tests.

<result># PR 1988 fix commit review (8820a75fb..96c446cac, head 96c446cac)

Evidence base: `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` at head: every suite green (1914 unit + integration suites, 0 failures). Line numbers are the head's.

## Findings, ranked

### F1 should-fix — `crates/engine/src/facade/claim_conversion.rs:806` — the pointer retry window starts at the ack, not at the conversion (Greptile T2, CONFIRMED)

`post_due` settles on `now &gt;= acked_at + POINTER_RETRY_WINDOW`. The first post runs inside `convert_at` (line 581) with no window check, so an entry that converted more than 40 days after its ack gets exactly two posts, then settles with no pointer delivered. The claimant holds a published row it can never find.
Timeline: day 0 the owner acks a write claim; the write-scope cut runs and the parent re-point fails (`a_failed_repoint_after_a_write_cut_is_repaired_by_the_next_pass` shows this state). The tick cannot place that folder (`TickSites::place`, line 921: "the scope root is not walked this pass"), so the entry stays `Pending`. On day 41 the owner opens the share dialog; the command pass repairs the index and converts; the first post fails on a transient mailbox error; the next pass settles the entry at line 806. The PR body itself names this window ("Only a command pass repairs a parent index...").
Fix: carry the conversion time in the entry and measure from it. Add `since: UnixMillis` to `PointerDue`, set from `self.scheduler.now()` in `convert_at` before the delivery loop; encode it as `pointerDueAt` next to `pointerDue`; `decode_state` requires `pointerDueAt` with `pointerDue` and refuses it without (same shape rule as `grantFloor`). Line 806 becomes `now.0 &gt;= pointer.since.0.saturating_add(POINTER_RETRY_WINDOW)`. Update the `POINTER_RETRY_WINDOW` doc ("after its ack" -&gt; "after its conversion"), `every_state_round_trips`, and the shape test.
Also a test gap: no test drives the settle branch at lines 806-808. Add one under "Engine simulation tests": forget the recipient, convert, advance the fake scheduler past the window, tick, assert the entry is gone and no key was posted.

### F2 should-fix — `crates/engine/src/facade/claim_conversion.rs:371-377` and `275-288` — a fresh `Acking` entry converts in the pass whose DELETE errored (Greptile T1, CONFIRMED, harm bounded)

Mechanism: `intake` persists the entry as `Acking` (line 372), then `self.ack` returns `Err` (line 377) and `?` leaves `intake` with the entry still `Acking` in memory. `run` puts every non-refused, non-`PointerDue` entry into `folders` (line 287), so `convert_at` runs on it in the same pass. Nothing between line 270 and line 287 excludes an `Acking` entry.
Two-device scenario: device A's DELETE never reaches the API. A converts, publishes the row for identity I and posts the pointer. Device B polls, holds `Fresh`, DELETE answers `removed: true`, converts. If B resolved A's published root, `convert_invite_claim` finds the row for I (`invite.rs:1050-1054`) and answers `Unchanged`: no row is added, only a second pointer post. If B resolved the root before A's publish landed, B appends its own row for I and both devices publish at the same observed sequence; the #1982 rule picks one and the other publish is lost. The claimant can read with either row. This is the pre-existing "two owner devices at one folder in one window" race the PR body lists; the failed DELETE only adds one more entry into it.
Real harm: NOT a duplicated ledger row. A duplicate is impossible: a device that sees the row takes the no-op path, and a device that does not see it replaces the set. The residual is a redundant pointer post, a second `GranteeJoined` notice on a stale resolve, and one more path into the lost-update race.
The second half of T1 (a `Resumed` hold kept when the later DELETE answers `removed: false`) is correct as written. The fake models a stale poll (`stale_poll`, `answer_next_poll_as_of_now`), so `Resumed` + `removed: false` cannot tell "my own earlier DELETE landed and its answer was lost" from "another device removed it". Dropping the entry would lose the claim in the first case; keeping it costs a no-op conversion in the second. Keep it.
Minimal fail-safe fix: do not convert a fresh `Acking` entry in the pass whose DELETE errored; convert it on the next pass, when the record is loaded from disk. In `run`, record `let held = record.entries().len();` before `intake`, and in the loop at line 275 skip `entry.state == EntryState::Acking &amp;&amp; at &gt;= held`. The next pass either sees the item again (`Resumed`, a DELETE with an answer) or converts it as the only holder. This restores "the DELETE answered before the convert" on every path except a lost DELETE response, which is undecidable and a no-op on the ledger. Test: `set_ack_failing(true)`, convert, assert nothing is granted and one acked entry waits; heal, convert, assert one grant and one posted key.
The reply to the Greptile thread can cite `invite.rs:1050` (`Unchanged`) and the `EntryState::Acking` doc, which already states the no-op.

### F3 nit — `claim_conversion.rs:577-580` — dead `else { continue }`

Lines 570-575 set every `verdicts[delivery.at]` to `PointerDue` before the loop, so the `let Some(Verdict::PointerDue(pointer)) = ... else { continue }` at line 578 always matches. Keep the pre-loop set (it makes the rows durable when `folder_name` fails at line 576) and read the pointer from a `Vec&lt;(Delivery, PointerDue)&gt;` built in the first loop.

### F4 nit — `crates/engine/src/grants/conversion.rs:216-220` — a full record drops a refusal in memory

`hold_for_ack` runs `retain` on the refused copy before `has_room`; on `None` the refusal is gone from memory and `run` persists that when any verdict is set. The item stays in the mailbox and the next pass re-holds it, so only the refused count flickers by one. Check `has_room` before `retain`.

### F5 nit — `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:9198-9203` docstring vs `claim_conversion.rs:368-370`

The docstring says an inbox that holds a claim refuses the revoke. A full record (`hold_for_ack` -&gt; `None`, `break`) leaves the item unacked in the inbox and the revoke proceeds, because `pending()` does not know it. Pre-existing break, 256 entries needed; state the limit in the docstring or refuse the revoke when `intake` broke on room.

## T1 / T2 verdicts

- T1: mechanism real (same-pass conversion after an errored DELETE; cross-device no-op or lost-update race). Harm: no duplicated row; a redundant pointer post and one more entry into the known concurrent-publish window. Fix: F2 (defer a fresh `Acking` entry to the next pass) plus a test. Do not change the `Resumed` rule.
- T2: mechanism real and reachable through the stale-parent-index window the PR body names. Fix: F1 (measure from the conversion time, new `pointerDueAt` field, fail-closed shape rule) plus a test for the settle branch.

## Gate verdicts

- /simplify: the 40-day window is needed. `PointerDue` entries count against `MAX_CONVERSION_ENTRIES` (256, `has_room` line 200) and cost one `place` plus one post per tick; a claimant whose API account is gone would pin a slot and a request per tick forever. Keep the window, fix its origin (F1). No duplicated state machine: `Verdict` is the per-pass delta and `EntryState` the durable state, each used once. One dead branch (F3). Comments are short and state rationale at its home; no tracker refs; no absence-justifying prose.
- /security-review: the record decode is fail-closed (`decode_state` refuses two states, `acking: false`, a stray `grantFloor`, an unknown permission; `an_entry_with_two_states_is_refused` asserts behaviour). Encode cannot write another shape because the state is an enum, so rule 8 symmetry holds without a new release test. `moved_root` maps `PointerConsultError::Rejected` to `TrustViolation` and `Unavailable` to `Seam` (lines 693-701), and the moved root then passes the adoption gate through `resolve_anchored` under the `Refused` arm (`rotation.rs:3048` answers `Unavailable` for a superseded root, so the branch is reachable). The revoke refuses on a failed poll with a retryable `Seam` (`mailbox-unavailable`), fail-closed. Time comes from `self.scheduler.now()` only (lines 366, 792). One gap: F5. `Running` is single-threaded `Cell` state; the `then_some` eager-guard bug is fixed and `a_running_pass_refuses_a_conversion_and_a_link_revoke` fails on the old code (the first `take` would clear the flag and the second command would run). No new DoS: entries are bounded at hold time.
- /crypto-privacy-review: the scope-pointer read in (3) reuses `PointerConsult`: `open_repoint` authenticates under the owner identity, binds to `scope_id`, refuses a write-epoch regression, then advances the floor on sight. No new key derivation, no new primitive. No secret bytes in any new assertion (tests assert posted-key counts, granted identities, write epochs, scope ids, IPNS names). `claim.clone()` in `intake` copies a claim payload (public claimant material), no seed. Nothing to zeroize was added.
- /code-review: each fix has a test in "Engine simulation tests" (`ci-rust.yml:78`, step `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine`): (1) `a_pointer_that_does_not_land_is_posted_again_until_it_does`; (2) `a_crash_between_the_ack_and_the_record_write_keeps_the_claim`, `a_fresh_hold_whose_delete_removed_nothing_goes`; (3) `a_failed_repoint_after_a_write_cut_is_repaired_by_the_next_pass`; (4) `a_mailbox_outage_refuses_the_link_revoke`, `a_failed_poll_still_converts_the_claims_already_acked`; (c/d) `a_running_pass_refuses_a_conversion_and_a_link_revoke`. Missing: the window-expiry branch (F1) and the errored-DELETE path (F2). The PR body's release row is correct: the same job runs `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine --release --test encode_refusals` (`ci-rust.yml:106`). PR body: bare `Closes #1969.` and `Closes #1970.`, no attribution line, title parentheses are the scope only, the table names every new test.

Verdict: two should-fix items (F1, F2), both cheap and each closing one Greptile P1 with a behaviour test; three nits. No blocker.

<result>Draft PR https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1993 is open. The branch is `feat/1977-web-share-dialog` and the head is `ddb243413`. All CI checks pass on that head. Desktop Build was skipped. The PR stays a draft. No bot review was requested.

**What changed**
- **People table:** the owner row is first. Each grantee row shows the name, which the owner can edit in place. The fingerprint shows on hover. The row also shows "direct" or "via link", a view/edit control, and a revoke that asks for confirmation and shows the fingerprint.
- **Create-link row:** it takes a permission, a lifetime (7 days by default) and the owner name. For a write link, the dialog shows the write-link warning. The dialog shows a new link once, with a copy control.
- **Link chips:** each link shows as a chip. The chip shows the expiry, the admission cap, the claims that wait and a "full" marker. A chip revokes its link by tag, after a confirmation that names the link and the people who joined through it. The confirmation has the "also remove the N people" checkbox.
- The contact-code path is now under "advanced".
- The "convert claims" button is gone. The dialog now converts waiting claims when it opens (ADR 0023 D4). This keeps the invite e2e specs working on main.
- **Owner name:** the dialog keeps it in `localStorage` and passes it to the mint. It is forgotten when the session ends, so a later account on the same browser does not get it.
- `shareRefusals.ts` now shows command refusals in words for each check id the dialog can hit.
- The e2e page object and specs, and the cross-client specs, now use the new dialog.

**Differences from the brief**
- **Rust change:** the ledger rows on main have no `viaLink` field. I added `via_link` to the engine `SharingGrant`, and it crosses to the client. Only a row that the owner signed carries it. This makes the PR touch Rust, which the brief did not expect.
- **Merge overlap:** PRs 1988 and 1989 change some of the same files. Small merge conflicts are likely.
- **"X joined" notice:** not built. The `granteeJoined` event is not on main; it arrives with PR 1988.
- **"since" column:** left out. The sharing read has no grant time.
- **Admission cap input:** left out. The mint command takes no cap, so the engine default of 25 applies. Each chip shows the cap.

**The `removeGrantees` fold:** it is one call in `apps/web/src/hooks/useSharingActions.ts`. There, `revokeInviteLink(linkTag, _options)` calls `facade.revokeInviteLink(target, linkTag)`. After PR 1989 merges, rename `_options` to `options` and pass it to the facade call. On main, conversion does not write a via-link tag yet, so the checkbox never shows.

**Puppeteer (local stack):** I verified these parts:
- The dialog opens with the owner row first.
- A write link minted with the owner name "Mia". The warning showed and the link showed once. The chip read "edit · expires Oct 2, 2026 · admits 25". The name was stored.
- The chip revoke showed a confirmation that names the link, and the confirm removed the chip with no error.

A second account is necessary to verify grantee rename and revoke in the browser. The manual steps are in the PR body.

**Local Playwright:** the sharing, invite, cross-client and write-grant specs pass. These cover conversion on open and the downgrade through the new control. One `@full` spec fails: "a claim on its own grants nothing". It checks only the claimant's shared-folders page, which this PR does not change, so I think it also fails on main. I did not check that. The Docker stack and the local API are still running.</result>

<result># Review of PR 1993 (feat/1977-web-share-dialog, head ddb243413)

Evidence: `pnpm typecheck` passes; `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web test` passes (68 files, 789 tests); every PR check is green at ddb243413. No git write, no browser, no nested agent.

## Findings

### should-fix

1. `apps/web/src/hooks/useSharingActions.ts:168-175` — `revokeInviteLink(linkTag, _options)` drops `removeGrantees` in silence. CONFIRMED.
   Scenario: PR 1988 (conversion writes `via_link`) merges before PR 1989 (client option). The checkbox shows, the owner ticks "also remove the 1 person", confirms, the link is cut, the person keeps access, no error shows. Today the checkbox never shows on main (grep finds no production write of `via_link`), so the window opens at the 1988 merge.
   Fix (fail closed, one line; the fold replaces it):
   ```ts
   run('revokeInviteLink', async (facade) =&gt; {
     if (options.removeGrantees) throw new Error('removing the people who joined is not in this build');
     await facade.revokeInviteLink(target, linkTag);
   ```
   Add one vitest: the checked confirm reports that refusal and keeps the link.

2. `apps/web/src/components/sharing/LinkSection.tsx:97-105` — the mint stays enabled while `fresh` shows, so a second mint replaces the shown-once link. CONFIRMED.
   Scenario: the owner mints a read link, does not copy it, changes to write, mints again. The first link is live, holds a grant-set slot and an admission cap, and no one can ever hold it. The `minting` ref guards only the double click.
   Fix: `disabled={busy || fresh !== null}` on `share-mint-link`, or hold `fresh` as a list and render one `CopyableValue` per mint.

### nit

3. `LinkSection.tsx:49-50` — `storeOwnerName(name)` runs before the mint. A name the engine refuses (`invite-name-too-long`) persists and pre-fills every later open. Move the store into `.then` when `fragment !== null`.
4. `apps/web/src/sharing/ownerName.ts:5-8`, `EngineProvider.tsx:73` — `forgetOwnerName` runs only in the effect cleanup. A tab close skips it, so the next account on the browser sees the previous owner's label. A label, not a key. Either accept and drop the "later account does not inherit it" claim, or key the entry by a session value.
5. `ShareDialog.tsx:81` — "people with access · 1" renders beside "people unavailable" when `scope === null`. Hide the count when `scope` is null.
6. `useSharingActions.ts:170` — the comment states a condition ("takes no `removeGrantees` yet"); drop "yet" so it stays true until the fold changes it.
7. `PeopleTable.tsx:119-160` and `LinkSection.tsx:211-278` — two confirmations with one shape (title, notes, keep, danger button, `role="alertdialog"`). A small `Confirm` primitive would hold both. Not required for merge.
8. Props `busy` and `actions` both travel; `busy` is `actions.busy !== null` (`ShareDialog.tsx:44`). Derive it in the children, or keep as is.
9. Rename has vitest coverage only; no Playwright step edits a name. Acceptable with the "Human verification" list in the PR body.

## Answers to the implementer's gaps

1. Admission cap. Engine: `Command::CreateInviteLink` (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:1255`) gets `admission_cap: Option&lt;u64&gt;`; `create_invite_link` (`facade.rs:8802`) maps `None` to `DEFAULT_ADMISSION_CAP` and refuses `0` or a value above a new `pub const MAX_ADMISSION_CAP: u64` (beside `DEFAULT_ADMISSION_CAP` at `crates/engine/src/grants/invite.rs:283`; bound it under the grant-set capacity that fires `grant-set-full`) with `MalformedInput { check: "invite-admission-cap-out-of-range" }`, then `LinkTerms { admission_cap }`. Wasm: `create_invite_link(node, permission, expires_at, owner_name, admission_cap: Option&lt;u64&gt;)` (`crates/wasm/src/lib.rs:2044`). Client: `protocol.ts:585` descriptor gets `admissionCap: number | null`; `commandCodec.ts:367` passes it through a bounded-integer check; `facade.ts:392` gets `admissionCap?: number`. Web: `LinkSection` adds `&lt;input type="number" min={1} max={MAX}&gt;` with default 25; the hook gets a fourth argument; `shareRefusals.ts` gets a label for the new check. Tests: an engine unit test for the refusal (release-active `Err`), a codec test, a vitest for the input.
2. Conversion on open. Harmless now: `convert_invite_claims` (`facade.rs:9674-9684`) polls the mailbox first and returns `Ok` on an empty inbox; the cost is one mailbox poll per open of a folder with a link. Removal after 1988: delete the second `run('convertInviteClaims', ...)` block in `useSharingActions.open` (`useSharingActions.ts:104-113`) and return `reached`; drop `'convertInviteClaims'` from `SharingCommand` and the vitest cases that assert the call. Note that ADR 0023 D4 itself names "when the owner opens the share dialog" as a trigger, so the web call goes only if 1988 puts that trigger inside the engine (for example in the sharing read).
3. No grant time exists. `GrantLedgerEntry` (`crates/core/src/seal/write_body.rs:76-`) carries tag, recipient keys, permission, `owner_sig`, `via_link`, `grantee_name`; the commitment entry carries `deadline` only on a link entry; ADR 0023 D2 retired the row `expiresAt`. A signed `grantedAt` is a wire-format change with an ADR. The column is correctly dropped.
4. "X joined": correct to fold after 1988. `granteeJoined` is absent from `packages/client/src/worker/protocol.ts` on main.
5. `via_link` on the read. Sourced only from an owner-attested row: `project_grant_ledger` (`facade.rs:4369`) filters by `row_is_owner_attested`, and `via_link` is inside the signed preimage (`insert_optional_signed`, `write_body.rs:274-277`, used by `encode_recipient_binding`). The test `a_via_link_tag_reaches_the_host_only_on_an_owner_attested_row` lives in `facade.rs` `mod tests`, which runs in "Rust / Engine simulation tests" (`ci-rust.yml:78`) and "Workspace tests". It proves an unsigned row drops the tag; a tampered `via_link` on a signed row is covered in core (`write_body.rs:1260`).
6. `localStorage` holds one key, `cipherbox.share.ownerName` (`ownerName.ts`); the other users (`sealedStore.ts`, `coreKit.ts`, `wagmi.ts`) are pre-existing. `EngineProvider` clears it in the effect cleanup (sign-out and rebuild). Gap: nit 4.
7. The failing spec is stale on main, not changed by this PR. Since PR 1986 a claim records a link hold (`facade.rs:9645 received.hold_link`) and `received_shares` lists every hold as a row with `via_link: true` (`facade.rs:11108-11119`), so the claimant's `/shared` page shows one row and `shared-empty` never appears. In that spec the owner's dialog closes before the claim (`tests/web-e2e/sharing.ts:mint`), so this PR runs no conversion there. Conversion on open is lawful under ADR 0023 D4. The full Web E2E workflow last ran 2026-09-19, before 1986 merged, so no main run shows the break. Fix in this PR: assert `shared-list` has one row that reads as a link hold with no grant, in place of `empty`.

The fragment shows only through `CopyableValue`, lives in the dialog's React state until unmount, never in the store, a URL query, or a log (no `console.` in the sharing tree). Names, fingerprints and check ids render as React text or attributes; no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. One fail-open: finding 1.
- /crypto-privacy-review: clean. The web builds no fragment; the owner name goes to the engine, which signs it into the fragment; the fingerprint comes from `facade.identityFingerprint`. No crypto in TypeScript.
- /code-review: every acceptance line maps to a test in "Web host typecheck + tests" (create link, revoke link with and without the checkbox, change permission, rename, no "convert claims" control) and "Web E2E" (mint, revoke, downgrade, conversion on open). No `.spec.ts` under vitest. Page objects updated. PR body: no attribution, bare `Closes #1977.`, title has no parentheses beyond the scope. Gap: item 7 in the e2e suite.

<result># PR 1988 rebase-integration review (head 1add867aa on main dacb46d80)

Evidence: `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` exit 0 (every binary green; last binary 253 passed). CI gate: `ci-rust.yml:101` runs this crate, `:106` runs `encode_refusals` in release.

## Findings

**nit 1** — `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:9757-9761` (`revoke_invite_link`). With `link_tag: None` the link is located only inside the `cut_and_rotate` closure, after the poll and the conversion pass. Scenario: two links at a folder, claims waiting; the owner sends revoke with no tag; the engine acks and converts the claims, publishes the root, then answers `link-ambiguous`. The tag case already refuses before the pass through `link_identity`. Fix: pin for `None` too — `link_identity(session, node, link_tag.as_ref())` taking `Option&lt;&amp;[u8;32]&gt;` and calling `locate_invite_link(.., tag)`; the closure then keeps only the `find` arm. The test at `owner_actions.rs:4655` uses an empty inbox, so it does not see this.

**nit 2** — `facade.rs:9772` vs `grants/invite.rs:940` (`link_of_sender`). The revoke picks the first link with the pinned identity (`.find`); the conversion refuses two links under one identity. Same set, two rules. Not reachable with real entropy (each mint draws a fresh ephemeral). Fix: reuse `link_of_sender(&amp;links, &amp;identity)` in the closure.

**nit 3** — `facade.rs:9660` `publish_converted_set` and `facade/claim_conversion.rs` `ConversionPass::publish` are the same reseal-and-publish body with two error mappings (`MalformedInput{check}` + Seam/TrustViolation vs `from_rotate(Reseal|Publish)`). One free function removes the copy. Behaviour is the same (non-retryable publish → TrustViolation).

No blocker, no should-fix.

## Points (1)-(8)

1. Correct. `link_identity` runs first through the gated owner read; `locate_invite_link` → `committed_links` accepts only rows passing `row_is_owner_attested` under `owner.authorise(scope)` (`invite.rs:861-887`). The cut closure re-resolves the node after the pass (parent index re-pointed by `cut_write_scope`) and finds the link by identity, which the wave does not move (tags do: `kdf::blinded_tag(shared, scope_root_ipns_name)`, `ledger.rs:71`). A wrong-length tag or a tag no link carries refuses `link-not-committed` before the poll (`facade.rs:9751`, `:9758`; test `owner_actions.rs:4674` asserts no publish).
2. Used, not dead: `edit_scope_set` (`facade.rs:9645`, main's append/permission-change path) calls it. Body byte-identical to main's. See nit 3.
3. Correct. `ClaimCounts::of` keys `(scope_root, sender)`; `pending(node)` sums the per-root range, `link_pending`/`link_refused` read one link; `PointerDue` entries count in neither. Sender is the link's ephemeral identity (intake: `item.sender_identity.to_sec1()`), matching `CommittedLink::ephemeral_identity_pk`. Wasm `SharingInviteLink` exposes `contactBudgetFull` and `refusedClaims` (`wasm/src/lib.rs:1127-1136`, `host.rs` test `:931/:951`); client `readInviteLink` and `SharingInviteLinkDescriptor` carry both.
4. Identical. Pre-rebase `cut_for_write_conversion` (commit `9b62de5bec`, `trigger.rs:595-612`) and main's `cut_for_write_scope` (`trigger.rs:575-591`) have the same body: `authorize_cut` (owner signer) + `enforce_committed_ledger`, set unchanged, write plane only, no revoked recipients. No `/revoked-at-cut/` record exists in either tree under any spelling; both paths raise the cut-epoch floor (`record_cut_epoch_floor`) and the write-epoch floor (`advance_write_epoch_on_sight`) after the wave (`cut_write_scope` mirrors `drive_cut` `facade.rs:8747-8776`). The vault-root branch of `drive_cut` is not needed: a link at the vault root is refused (`invite-target-is-the-vault-root`, `facade.rs:3782`).
5. Confirmed. PR 1987 (`dacb46d80`) renamed `cut_for_write_grant` → `cut_for_write_scope`, removed the "set committing no write row is refused" rule and deleted `a_write_grant_cut_refuses_a_set_committing_no_write_row`. The branch adds `a_write_conversion_cut_drives_a_read_only_set_through_the_write_plane` (`trigger/tests.rs:791`), which asserts what main now specifies.
6. Present. `CLAIM_NOT_HELD` (`claim_conversion.rs:34`) fires in `intake` when the fresh-hold persist fails before the delete; the entry is un-held and the item stays on the mailbox. `revoke_invite_link` returns it (`facade.rs:9773-9778`). Test `a_record_write_that_fails_at_intake_refuses_the_link_revoke` (`owner_actions.rs:5538`) asserts the refusal, the item waiting, the link intact, then a clean revoke.
7. Both numbers are correct. `ALL` = 7 kinds (0x01-0x06, 0x07 PendingConversions, 0x08 GranteeNames). The accept file has 8 vectors because `empty-body` is a vector, not a kind (main: 6 kinds, 7 vectors). `kat_manifest.rs:1867` and the manifest both say 8; the in-crate order test maps PendingConversions→5, GranteeNames→6.
8. Retry is harmless and unreachable. `convert_invite_claim` never returns `LinkAmbiguous` (it uses `link_of_sender`, which answers `LinkNotCommitted` for a duplicate); the variant comes only from `locate_invite_link(.., None)` on the revoke path. Listing it under "a check conversion never runs" keeps the match exhaustive with no drop-by-default; terminal would be equally safe.

## Security / crypto pass (diff regions only)

- Gate rejection → TrustViolation: `moved_root` maps `PointerConsultError::Rejected` to TrustViolation; `moved_by_write_cut` with no wave → TrustViolation; non-retryable publish → TrustViolation. OK.
- Fail-closed decode symmetry: `InviteClaim::encode`/`::decode` both refuse a name `GranteeName::new` refuses, release-active (`?`, no `debug_assert`); test `a_claim_name_no_row_can_carry_is_refused_at_both_ends` hits both ends. OK.
- Time: `acked_at` from `scheduler.now()` at intake, and the deadline verdict is judged against `acked_at` on every retry; `post_due` window and `link_deadline` also read the scheduler. OK.
- Secret bytes in assertions: `link_read.rs` tests compare `hex_lower(&amp;KEY)`, the idempotency key the API sees, not a secret. Owner-local KAT vectors carry `ownerSecret`/`ephemeralScalar` by design. `AckedClaim`/`InviteClaim` Debug redact sender, payload, name. OK.
- Trust boundary of the pinned identity: read from an owner-attested link row through the gated owner read (point 1). OK.

**Decision for you:** the brief says the append path lets one identity hold two rows, but I found no command that does it. A direct share and a claim conversion both find an identity that already has a row and add no second row. So the S5 test builds the two rows in the seeded vault, not through commands. The refusal guards a state that only an outside edit to the set or a race between two owner devices can produce. If you want an end-to-end form, it needs a new way to produce that state.
