<result>I have all the evidence I need; no further tool calls are required. Findings follow.

# Review of PR 1994 (feat/1978-web-invite-preview, head 316ce5ca7)

Evidence (run once in the reviewer worktree at 316ce5ca7): `pnpm install` OK; `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/client build` OK; `pnpm typecheck` OK (apps/web, tests/web-e2e, tests/cross-client); `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web test` 69 files / 801 tests pass, and both new files under `src/routes/invite/` are picked up by the vitest include `src/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}`; `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine preview` 10 passed (incl. the changed joined-link test); `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` clean. No git write, no browser.

## Findings

1. **should-fix (process, not code)** — `apps/web/src/routes/InvitePage.tsx` and `InvitePage.test.tsx` (deleted here). PR 1988 (open) edits both files at their OLD path (`claimInviteLink(fragment, '')` plus two mock expectations). Failure: whichever PR lands second hits a delete/modify conflict on rebase; if 1988 lands second its hunk re-creates the old page next to the new one. Fix: after 1988 merges, rebase, drop 1988's hunk on the old path, apply the fold in (3).

2. **nit** — PR body, "Tests". The engine test in `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs` is attributed to "Rust / Workspace tests (Linux)". That job runs `cargo test --workspace ... --exclude cipherbox-engine` (`.github/workflows/ci-rust.yml:58-63`). The gate that runs it is "Engine simulation tests" (`cargo test -p cipherbox-engine`, ci-rust.yml:100-101). Fix: rename the gate in the PR body.

3. **nit** — PR body, "Scope notes". "The identity token carries only `method`" is not accurate: `IdentityCredential.email` (`packages/login/src/identity.ts:25`, "for display only") reaches `WebCoreKitSession.email()` (`apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.ts:343`). The empty default is still correct: ADR 0027 D1 and alternative (f) reject the email as the default name. Fix: reword the note to cite ADR 0027 D1.

4. **nit** — `apps/web/src/routes/invite/InviteCard.tsx:41` `key={`${index}:${entry.name}`}`: the index alone is the key. Cosmetic; no failure.

Nothing else confirmed. No tracker refs, no absence-justifying comments, no long apologia (the InvitePage header states the bearer-capability rationale once, 8 lines). No `.spec.ts` under vitest. No `debug_assert!`.

## Answers to points (1)-(5)

**(1) `scope`.** It is `fragment.scope_id` copied by `pending_link_bookmark` (`crates/engine/src/grants/link_read.rs:58`), so it comes from the fragment, NOT from data the gate verifies. It is, however, exactly the id the join bookmarks (`received.reconcile(share)` keys on the same `share`) and the id `received_shares()` and `SharedPage` route through (`folderPath(share.scope)`), so post-join navigation is consistent with the shared list. Leak: none beyond the fragment the bearer already holds; it triggers no extra resolve, so ADR 0024 E4 is untouched; it is a routing handle, not a fifth displayed item, so ADR 0028 D2 holds. Tests: `a_preview_of_a_joined_link_reads_the_root_the_join_adopted` asserts `preview.scope == fx.folder` and that a received-shares row carries it (gate: Engine simulation tests); `invite_preview_getters_cross_with_boundary_shapes` asserts the getter crosses as 16 bytes (gate: Core KATs, the `cargo test -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` step). Residual outside this diff: nothing in the preview binds `fragment.scope_id` to the root it opens; a forged id in a fragment the attacker holds only misroutes the attacker's own tab.

**(2) Display name.** `apps/web/src/auth` holds no display name. The only candidate is the email (finding 3), which ADR 0027 D1 forbids as a default. Empty is right.

**(3) Fold after 1988.** `InviteCard` calls `onJoin(name)`; `InvitePage.join(scope, name)` dispatches `facade.claimInviteLink(fragment, name)`; the mock expectation becomes `[[FRAGMENT, 'Grace']]` after a change event. The engine's `NameTooLong` surfaces through the existing `refused` alert; no web-side bound is needed.

## Findings

**BLOCKER — a committed write grantee can make its own grant unrevokable.** `crates/engine/src/grants/cut_set.rs:35-50` (`committed_grantee`) and `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:8287-8289`: the revoke names the grantee only through an owner-attested ledger row. A write grantee authors the write body, and `enforce_committed_ledger` (`crates/engine/src/grants/ledger.rs:244-266`) compares `(tag, permission)` only, so a row with a zeroed `owner_sig` or a rewritten `recipientIdentityPk` still adopts. Scenario: W (write grantee) re-seals the body with its own row un-attested. The owner's sharing read still names W from the commitment plus contact book (`facade.rs:4569-4597`; base test `the_sharing_read_names_a_rewritten_row_from_the_owners_own_commitment`, whose doc says "so a revoke can reach the tag"). `Command::Revoke { W }` now answers `rot-revoke-not-granted` (`grantee_cut_set` -&gt; `None`), `RevokeInviteLink { remove_grantees: true }` skips W (`link_cut_set` uses `committed_personal`), and no tag-addressed revoke exists. Every re-seal keeps wrapping W's blob off the commitment. On the base the revoke reached the tag through the contact book, so this diff reopens the hole the base's sharing read was built to close. Fix: when no attested row names the identity, resolve the tag the way the sharing read does — the committed personal entry whose `recipient_enc_pk(pointer_read_key)` equals the contact's enc key on this device (inverse of `labels.committed_contact`) — or add a tag-addressed revoke fed from `projected.unattested`. Test: seed `bystander_row_with_corrupt_sig` and the `poisoned` row of the fixture at `owner_actions.rs:3877`, `import_recipient`, `Command::Revoke { honest }` is `Ok` and the tag leaves the commitment.

**should-fix — the person revoke that cuts an admitting link skips the inbox and record guards the link revoke applies.** `facade.rs:8259-8265` ignores every non-trust error of `convert_invite_claims` (poll failure, `the-conversion-record-is-full`, `claim-not-held`), while `revoke_invite_link` (`facade.rs:9958-9981`) refuses on them because "a claim left on the inbox could never convert after the cut". Scenario: mailbox down; owner revokes P, admitted by L; L is cut in the same set; Q's un-acked claim on L can never convert and sits as a refused entry until the owner dismisses it. Fix: keep the `convert_invite_claims` error and, inside the select closure when `cut.admitting_links` is non-empty, refuse with `mailbox-unavailable` or the pass's Seam message exactly as the link path does. Test: `a_mailbox_outage_refuses_the_revoke_of_a_link_admitted_grantee`, plus the direct-grantee revoke still succeeds under the outage.

nit — `link_sweep.rs:221-225`: the release-active write-plane refusal has no test. Seed a link entry committed at write (the S5 test shows the seeding shape) and assert the sweep publishes nothing and reports the Seam.
nit — S6 has no test: an index entry that names a child under the wrong parent is still cut under its real parent.
nit — the sweep has no per-tick cap (`link_sweep.rs:78-91`): one vault walk plus one cascade per due root, deepest first, so a nested due root's subtree is re-keyed by its own cut and again by each due ancestor; `Running` is held for the whole run and every link revoke or link-admitted person revoke answers `a-conversion-pass-is-running` meanwhile. Owner-authored workload, so not attacker-driven; state it in the PR body or cap the cuts per sweep.
nit — `facade.rs:8907-8910`: `CutKind::Downgrade` unpacks a one-element `BTreeSet` and invents a `NotGranted` arm; a `CutKind::Downgrade(tag)` payload removes it.
nit — a `retire_cut_links` failure after a landed cut (`link_sweep.rs:85-87`, `facade.rs:8313-8322`) leaves the refused entries for good: the next sweep never lists the cut link, and a retried person revoke finds no admitting link; in `revoke_grant` the `?` also returns before `forget_link_grant`. Retire entries whose sender no committed link names on the next `hold_record` instead.

## B — integration

`revoke_grants` (`trigger.rs:500-517`): one `drop_tags`, one cut-epoch step, write plane only when a cut tag is a write row; an empty or unknown set is `NotGranted`. `grantee_cut_set` reads owner-attested personal rows plus each committed link a via-link names; `viaLink` sits inside the owner signature (`write_body.rs:276`), so a writer cannot forge one. `cut_and_rotate` returns the tag set and `cut_at` takes it. `RevokeInviteLink` keeps the base's early pin (`link_identity`, `facade.rs:10027`) and `link_of_sender`, so a write conversion between pin and cut still names the link. `hold_record` takes `Running` synchronously before its first await, then loads; the sweep and both revoke paths run under it, and a busy lock answers `a-conversion-pass-is-running` on both (`facade.rs:8297-8299`, `:9985-9987`). `retire_cut_links` persists through the pass and repaints counts. The D3 clear (`cascade.rs:540-563`) loops over the committed entries only, so it fires only for a recipient the owner-signed set commits; it raises `/cleared/` to the cut's read epoch and lowers nothing; `/revoked-at-cut/` rises only after the publish. The sweep is a `ConversionPass` method paced by `profile.link_sweep_cadence` (600 s / 3 s; `the_link_sweep_runs_slower_than_the_tick`), runs after the conversion pass, and `link_swept` advances only when the sweep ran.

## C — ADR 0025

D1: the link cut leaves no blob at the link tag; `remove_grantees` adds only owner-attested rows whose signed via-link names that link. D2: walk from the vault root, deadline against the injected `now`, converts first, never cuts a link with a pending entry, one cut per scope root (a due root resolves twice: at the walk and again before the sign, which is the two-device rule). D3: the row names the person; the clear rule is "cut epoch not below the recorded cut". D4: one cut set, one cut-epoch step, one rotation, asserted in every revoke test. A revoked person loses the blob at the cut and keeps what it saw (E2); its contact-book slot is released. A revoke never removes a grantee the owner did not name: the person cut removes that identity's rows and the admitting links only (the other claimant keeps access in `a_person_revoke_cuts_the_admitting_link_in_the_same_rotation`); the link cut removes grantees only under the flag. The blocker above is the one gap: an un-attested row is a grantee the owner sees and cannot reach.

## D — gates

- /simplify: no dead code after the rebases (clippy clean, removed imports gone); the grantee cut and the link cut share `committed_personal`; one nit (`CutKind::Downgrade` payload). Comments are short, no tracker refs, no apologia.
- /security-review: two findings (the blocker and the should-fix). Gate rejections stay `TrustViolation` and reach `AttributableAbuse` from the sweep report; the D3 clear only raises floors; `now` comes from the scheduler; no attacker-driven unbounded work (one nit on the owner-driven sweep size).
- /crypto-privacy-review: `/revoked-at-cut/` binds (scope, recipient enc key) to the cut epoch this device signed; `/cleared/` binds the same pair to the read epoch of the cut it clears; the cut authority is the owner signature over the commitment behind the adoption gate; the walk's parent seed is `Zeroizing`; tests assert tags and blob presence only, no secret bytes. Nothing beyond the blocker.
- /code-review: every acceptance line of #1973 and #1974 has a named test in Engine simulation tests; the cadence has a profile test at both profiles; the PR body has bare `Closes` lines, no attribution, no parentheses in the title, and a "Local gates" section.

Merge recommendation: do not merge until the blocker lands with its test; fold the should-fix into the same push.</result>

<result>The fold is on PR 1989 as one commit on top of the feature commit, pushed once. Head is `ce6e7ef2ffa66b6ee5800f6ad85c8e7db4feb7e8`. The PR is still a draft and no review was requested. Every gate you listed passed locally, and so did the web unit tests. There is no CI result, because CI does not run on a stacked base.

**Items done**
- **B1:** A person revoke now finds a write grantee who broke the owner signature on its own row. It uses the same rule as the sharing read: the committed `recipientEncPk` of the one contact that holds it. A link revoke with `remove_grantees` also takes such a row.
- **S1:** When a link admitted the grantee, a person revoke now refuses on a mailbox outage, a full conversion record or a claim that cannot be held, as the link revoke does. A direct grantee's revoke still succeeds during an outage.
- **N2:** A new test proves that a sweep still cuts the link under the correct parent when another index names the scope root under a wrong parent.
- **N3:** One sweep cuts at most 8 scope roots (`MAX_SWEEP_CUTS`). The next tick sweeps again without waiting for the cadence.
- **N4:** `CutKind` now carries its rows: `Revoke(&amp;tags)` or `Downgrade(&amp;tag)`.
- **N5:** Each hold of the conversion record (link revoke, link-admitted person revoke, sweep walk) retires refused entries whose link the scope no longer commits. A failed retire write no longer skips `forget_link_grant`.
- **Tests against the old code:** The B1, S1, N3 and N5 tests fail on the old code, as expected. The direct-grantee outage test and the N2 test pass on both. I also checked that the N2 test fails when `visited` is set before the resolve.

**Items that changed shape**
- **B1, union instead of fallback:** The contact route runs for every row without an attested label, not only when no attested row names the identity. This matches the sharing read row for row, so after a revoke that person no longer shows on the folder.
- **B1, links:** A person revoke adds a link to the cut only from an attested row's via-link. The via-link on an unattested row is written by the grantee. If it counted, a revokee could delay its own revoke by naming a link that has a pending claim.
- **B1, link revoke:** For `remove_grantees`, an unattested row counts by its via-link alone. The cut can only grow. The residual risk is that a writer can mark a bystander's row so that it is cut when the owner removes that link's grantees.
- **N1:** You cannot seed a link entry at write permission. Core refuses a link at write on encode and on decode, and core's release gate already tests this. I kept the refusal as a function, `sweep_cut`, and gave it a unit test in `link_sweep` that uses a personal write row. It is not in `encode_refusals`, because that file cannot reach the fake world. The refusal returns `Err`, so a release build keeps it. The PR body states this.
- **N5, new behaviour:** A retire that fails after a landed cut no longer fails the command, for both revokes. The next hold of the record repairs it, so the command reports the landed cut.
- **Person revoke reads the contact book first:** If the book is unreadable, the revoke now fails before the cut.

The PR body now describes these changes and lists the new tests under "Engine simulation tests". It keeps "Local gates" and the two bare `Closes` lines, and it has no attribution.

<result># PR 1989 fold 2 review (head ce6e7ef2f over 10241f4f3)

Evidence: `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` on ce6e7ef2f: every suite green (1951 lib + 253 owner_actions + others, 0 failed). Log: `~/.claude/scratch/w1963/G/reviewer2-cargo-test.log`. Findings file: `~/.claude/scratch/w1963/G/reviewer2-findings.md`.

## Findings

1. **should-fix** `crates/engine/src/facade/link_sweep.rs:99-102` and `facade.rs:7914-7916`. `report.more` is `due.len() &gt; MAX_SWEEP_CUTS` before any cut runs, and the tick skips `link_swept.set(now)` while `more` is true. Scenario: nine or more scope roots carry an expired link and the eight deepest cuts fail every time (publish leg refuses while resolves succeed). `more` stays true, so the sweep runs on every poll tick (30 s in production, not 600 s), walks the whole vault under `Running` each time, and the ninth root is never reached: `take(8)` on a fixed walk order always picks the same eight failing roots. Fix: count landed cuts toward the cap and set `more` only when the cap was reached with roots left (iterate all of `due.iter().rev()`, `break` with `report.more = true` once `landed == MAX_SWEEP_CUTS`). That closes the hot loop and the starvation in one shape. One test: eight failing cuts plus one landing root; assert the ninth root is cut and the cadence advances.

2. **nit** `facade.rs:8797-8806` and PR body ("`Command::Revoke` reads the contact book only for the row with no attested label"). The read is unconditional. A contact-store `Seam` now refuses the revoke of a fully attested grantee before the cut. Fail-closed and retryable (`ContactStoreError::Seam` maps to `EngineError::Seam`, `facade.rs:2419`), so the behaviour is acceptable; the body sentence is wrong. Correct it, or read the book lazily inside the select closure only when an unattested personal row exists.

3. **nit** `facade.rs:4521-4542` (`sole_holder`). Two contacts on one encryption subkey make the unattested row unreachable and the command answers `rot-revoke-not-granted` (matches the sharing read's zero label). A contact M who imports a code that binds writer W's subkey makes W's broken row unrevocable by person revoke on that device until the owner drops M. Add one "Accepted" line to the PR body.

4. **nit** N1 placement. `a_sweep_cut_that_names_a_write_row_is_refused` lives in `link_sweep.rs:303` (debug unit test), not in `tests/encode_refusals.rs` (`--release` gate). The refusal is a plain `if ... return Err` (`link_sweep.rs:55-66`), so the release build runs the same path; the brief's fallback (owner_actions + PR body note) is matched in effect, and the PR body note is present.

## Verification table

## Answers

**(a)** The contact route runs for every personal row that is unattested or carries the `UNATTESTED_IDENTITY_PK` placeholder; an attested row with a real label answers by identity only (`cut_set.rs:83-89`). The match is anchored on the owner-signed commitment entry (`recipientEncPk` unmasked with the scope `pointerReadKey`) plus the local book; the book is the only non-owner input. Two contacts on one subkey: `sole_holder` names nobody, the row stays, the command answers `rot-revoke-not-granted` (finding 3). Unreadable book: refuses before any scope read, nothing is published, `Seam` for a host outage and `TrustViolation` for a book this build cannot open. Fail-closed and correct; the PR body sentence is inaccurate (finding 2).

**(b)** `viaLink` is inside the owner-signed binding preimage (`crates/core/src/seal/write_body.rs:314-333`). A revokee that edits its via-link breaks its own attestation, the row becomes unattested, `admitted` is empty, and the revoke skips the conversion gate. `convert_before_link_cut` runs unconditionally, but its error applies only when an attested row names an admitting link (`facade.rs:8830-8831`). A revokee cannot delay its own revoke.

**(c)** Confirmed: W setting `via_link = L` on B's row breaks B's binding, and `link_cut_set` (`cut_set.rs:166`) counts the row by via-link alone, so `remove_grantees` cuts B. Residual: W already re-seals the grant section and `enforce_committed_ledger` compares `(tag, permission)` only, so W can replace B's blob today; that denial is visible to B as an unopenable blob and the owner's next re-mint repairs it, while the fold's cut is owner-signed and permanent until a re-grant. The bystander cut needs a hostile writer in the scope and the owner pressing `remove_grantees`. The alternative (attested via-links only) lets a hostile writer keep a write grant silently after the owner removed "everyone through L"; the fallback person revoke fails when W's subkey is ambiguous (finding 3). Silent retention of a write grant is worse than a visible, recoverable over-cut. Acceptable with one "Accepted" bullet in the PR body: a write grantee can mark another row with a link; `remove_grantees` then cuts that row; a re-grant restores it.

**(d)** Confirmed. `settle` failure is swallowed at `facade.rs:8866` and `:10059`; `forget_link_grant` still runs (`:8877-8886`). Refused entries never block a revoke: the gate reads `pending_links()` (pending only), and `evict` caps refused entries at 64 of 256 (`conversion.rs:46,50,277`), so a stale entry cannot fill the record. Every later hold repairs: the link revoke (`facade.rs:10035`), a link-admitted person revoke (`:8849`), the sweep walk at each reached root (`link_sweep.rs:195`), each persisted by `settle`. No window leaves an entry that blocks a later revoke.

## Merge recommendation

Fix finding 1 (small) and the two PR body lines (findings 2 and 3), then mark ready for CodeRabbit; no blocker remains.</result>

<result>The revoke fix is pushed to PR 1988 and CI is green. The new head is `c16bc9589`, amended into the single commit with the same message. The attribution grep prints nothing, and I pushed once with `--force-with-lease`.

I did not apply the suggestion literally. I did not make every non-trust error refuse the revoke. Instead, `ConversionPass::run` now returns `PassOutcome { intake, conversion }`, so the revoke no longer compares message strings. The tick and `ConvertInviteClaims` use `into_result()`, which returns the intake error first, as before. How the revoke treats each error class:
- **Intake failures refuse the revoke** with the intake error. This covers the pass lock, the record load, the pass keys, a failed ack, a full record, and a record that does not persist around the ack. Each of them can leave a claim on the inbox.
- **Conversion failures do not refuse it directly** (resolve, entropy, contact book, publish, lost race). They leave the entry pending, and the cut already refuses a link with a pending entry (`link-has-a-pending-conversion`).
- **A failed share-pointer post does not refuse it.** The post does not read the link. If it refused, a claimant that the API does not know could block the link revoke for the whole `POINTER_RETRY_WINDOW`. The existing test `a_claimant_the_api_does_not_know_does_not_block_the_revoke` keeps this behaviour.
- **A trust violation is reported, and the revoke continues.**

This goes against part of your brief, which said publish and entropy errors refuse the revoke: those cases still refuse, but through the pending-entry check and with the `link-has-a-pending-conversion` message, not the original error. The real hole was an ack that fails on a claim of another link. The intake stops there, so the revoked link's claim stays on the inbox with no record entry, and the old code cut the link.

- **Test:** `an_ack_that_fails_refuses_the_revoke_of_a_link_whose_claim_it_left_on_the_inbox`, in `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs` (Engine simulation tests). It fails against the old match. The revoke refuses with a Seam error that starts with "mailbox ack". The inbox keeps both claims, both links stay committed, and nothing publishes. After the heal, the revoke converts both claims and cuts only the named link.
- **Person revoke:** on this branch `revoke_grant` cuts no link, so there was nothing to change. When `convert_before_link_cut` in PR 1989 is rebased onto this, it must check `pass.intake` in the same way.
- **Local gates:** engine tests, fmt, clippy, the wasm32 check and tracker-refs all pass.

The only fix I see is to cut every personal row that fails the owner signature check. - **Branch:** `fix/web-e2e-link-first-specs`
- **PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1996 (draft, `Part of #1963.`, no attribution)

| Test | Cause | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `tests/cross-client.spec.ts:20` "a grant reaches the second client on one nocache refresh" (Smoke and Full) | A race. Since PR 1988, every owner tick converts claims on its own. When the spec clicks "convert claims" while the tick is converting, the engine refuses the click. The CI screenshot shows the refusal in the dialog: `seam error: a-conversion-pass-is-running`. The tick then makes the grant (the owner log shows the `granteeJoined` event), but the dialog only reads grants when it opens. So the row count stays at 0. | A new page-object helper, `SharePage.openUntilGranted`, closes and opens the dialog until the grant row shows. It has a bounded timeout. |
| `tests/cross-client/src/share.ts` `grantOneFolder` (Cross-Client macOS, scenario `share-grant-cut`) | The same race. | `openUntilGranted` |
| `tests/invite.spec.ts:49` "a claim on its own grants nothing" (Full) | A stale spec. Since PR 1986, a claim records a link hold, so `/shared` shows one row, not an empty list. | The spec now expects one row with the folder name and no empty state. I renamed it to "a claim lists the folder once…". |
| `tests/invite.spec.ts:28` "converts to a grant" (it passed, but was open to the race) | The tick can convert before the dialog opens, so the "no grants" state is not always there. | `openUntilGranted` |
| `tests/web-e2e/staging/invite.spec.ts` | The same race, in the staging deploy run. | `openUntilGranted` with a 360 s timeout |

I also removed the `convertClaimsButton` getter, because no spec clicks the button now. Only tests and page objects changed: five files under `tests/web-e2e/` and `tests/cross-client/src/`.

## Should-fix

1. `docs/SHARING.md:250` — "The web invite page ... sends ... an empty owner name" is false. The owner name enters at the mint from the share dialog: `packages/client/src/facade.ts:406-418` defaults `ownerName = ''`, and `apps/web/src/hooks/useSharingActions.ts:105-113` passes none. The engine refuses the own root at the fragment open (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:9921`, `invite-names-the-own-vault-root`), before any resolve. Fix: move it to step 2, or drop "in order".

<result>Both issues are fixed in one draft PR, and CI is green on it.

- **PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1999 (draft, no attribution)
- **Branch:** `fix/1992-1995-engine-followups`, head `4117ae507dcea67da35ff5e4f1b4e8412bc477ac`, one commit
- **CI:** 27 checks pass and 4 are skipped. No check fails. All local gates passed. No KAT vector changed, so I committed no vectors.

**#1992:** The refresh leg (`grants/received_status.rs`) now sends a `GateError::Rejected` from `assemble_candidate` to `report_refusal`. The inbox walk (`grants/inbox.rs`) now reports it as `AcceptError::Gate`. `Seam` stays a skip on both paths. On the refresh leg a rejected record marks the share `Unresolvable`, the same as a replay below the sequence floor. It is never a revocation. There is one test per path, and I confirmed that both fail on the old mapping.

**#1995 design:** The book now keys the link origin by `CommittedLink::ephemeral_identity_pk`. I did not rebind the origins at the cut. The contact book is local to each owner device, and a cut runs on one device. A rebind would leave the book on every other owner device at the old tag. A failed book write after the publish would also leave it stale for good. The identity key needs no write at the cut.
- The book writes `linkIdentity` and still reads the `linkTag` entries that the current staging release wrote.
- An entry with both keys, or with neither, is refused as `BookCodecError::LinkSourceNotOne`.
- A conversion through a link rebinds each `linkTag` entry that names that link to its identity.
- The two #1995 engine tests fail when I make the book store the tag again.

**Deviation from the brief:** A writer cannot break a row's signature through the engine. To break it after a real wave, the test must author a whole scope root under a random write seed. So I split the #1995 test in two:
- `a_links_contact_share_survives_the_write_scope_cut_of_a_write_claim` uses a real write-claim cut. After the cut, `contactBudgetFull` still holds and the link refuses the next claim.
- `a_link_revoke_with_remove_grantees_takes_a_stripped_row_after_a_wave_moved_the_link_tag` uses a seeded vault root. The book holds the link at its tag from before a wave, and the revoke with the flag still takes the stripped row.

**Public items changed:**
- New `pub enum LinkSource { Identity, Tag }` with `names(&amp;CommittedLink)`, re-exported from `grants`.
- `link_budget_full(&amp;[Option&lt;LinkSource&gt;], &amp;CommittedLink)`.
- `ContactStore::record_from_link` and `record_unchanged_from_link` now take `&amp;CommittedLink`.
- `ContactStore::contacts_with_sources` now returns `Option&lt;LinkSource&gt;`.
- `LinkSources::contacts` now holds `Option&lt;LinkSource&gt;`.
- `ConvertedClaim::link_tag` becomes `ConvertedClaim::link: CommittedLink`.
- New `BookCodecError::LinkSourceNotOne`.

**Known windows (also in the PR body):**
- A legacy `linkTag` entry whose link already moved before this release stays unmatched. The owner can still revoke that person directly.
- A write wave re-mints a row whose signature a writer broke *before* the wave as an owner-signed placeholder with no via-link reference. A link revoke with `remove_grantees` does not reach that row after the wave, whatever the book holds. This fix does not change that.</result>

<result># Review of PR 1999 (head 4117ae507) — fix/1992-1995-engine-followups

Findings file: `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/w1963/findings.md`

## Findings

### 1. should-fix — `crates/engine/src/grants/cut_set.rs:213-217` (`link_cut_set`): #1995 AC3 is not met after a real write wave

Defect: `remove_grantees` cannot reach a stripped row once a wave re-minted it.
Mechanism: the wave re-mint (`crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:4296-4340`) turns a row the owner does not attest into an owner-signed placeholder with `recipient_identity_pk == UNATTESTED_IDENTITY_PK` and `via_link = None`. `link_cut_set` matches a stripped row only while `!row_is_owner_attested(...)`. After the wave the row IS attested, so neither clause matches. The book change cannot close this gap; the PR lists it as "known window 2", but issue #1995 AC3 reads "reaches a broken row after a write wave".
Failure scenario: writer W joins through link L and strips its via-link reference. The owner converts one write claim (a write-scope cut). The owner revokes L with `remove_grantees`. W keeps its write grant as the placeholder row. `revoked_rows` (`cut_set.rs:87`) already treats `attested &amp;&amp; identity == UNATTESTED_IDENTITY_PK` as an enc-keyed match for a person revoke; `link_cut_set` does not.
Fix (mirrors `revoked_rows`; `UNATTESTED_IDENTITY_PK` is already imported at `cut_set.rs:18`):

```rust
                    row.via_link == Some(link.tag)
                        || ((!row_is_owner_attested(&amp;owner_identity, row, name)
                            || row.recipient_identity_pk == UNATTESTED_IDENTITY_PK)
                            &amp;&amp; joined.contains(&amp;entry.recipient_enc_pk(book.pointer_read_key)))
```

Safety: a placeholder exists only where a writer broke a row; the enc match is the rule the pre-wave clause already applies; a direct grantee is excluded by `joined_through` (origin `None`). Test: in `revoke_a_link_over_a_stripped_row` run a write-claim conversion after `seed_vault` and before the revoke, then assert the placeholder row is cut. That also repairs finding 3.

### 2. nit — `crates/engine/src/grants/contact_store.rs:522-541` (`record_unchanged_from_link`): the legacy rebind runs on one path only

Only `record_from_link` rebinds `LinkSource::Tag` entries. An `Unchanged` conversion through the same link returns before any rebind. Scenario: a `linkTag` book, only re-claims arrive at that link, then a wave — the link's share resets. Hoist the rebind loop into one helper both paths call, and `put` when an entry moved. Related: the rebind inside `record_from_link` is lost when the call returns `Err` (no `put`); idempotent and bounded, so harmless.

### 3. nit — `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs:4085` test name overclaims

`..._takes_a_stripped_row_after_a_wave_moved_the_link_tag` runs no wave; it records the book under a tag from a second write seed. It does prove the book keys by identity and fails on the old code. Rename it or make it a real wave (finding 1's test).

## Gate verdicts

- /simplify: nothing to apply. `LinkSource { Identity, Tag }` is the minimal shape: a `linkTag` entry cannot be converted at decode (no identity known) and dropping it to `None` would vouch the contact. No dead code, no tracker refs in added lines, no narrating or apologia comments, `cargo fmt --all --check` clean.
- /security-review: #1992 holds. `assemble_candidate` (`net/adopter.rs:223`) returns `Seam` for fetch failures and `Rejected` for a CID mismatch, a malformed grant section and `ScopeRootNotResealable`. Refresh leg: `Rejected` -&gt; `report_refusal` -&gt; `emit_trust_violation` (`AttributableAbuse`) and `Classified::unresolvable()`, never `RevocationSignal`. Inbox: `Rejected` -&gt; `report(AcceptError::Gate)`, attributable because `rejection().is_some()`; the item stays un-acked like every other attributable arm. `Seam` is the only skip on both paths. #1995: the identity is owner-attested at every point of use — conversion, revoke and sharing read all take the link from `committed_links`, which requires `owner.authorise(scope)` and `row_is_owner_attested`. The commitment is owner-signed, so a writer cannot add a link entry; a claimant's item must be signed by the link's ephemeral identity and `link_of_sender` refuses a duplicate identity. The book is sealed HPKE-to-self, so no attacker authors a `Tag`, both-keys or no-key entry; decode fails closed on both/neither (`LinkSourceNotOne`, tested) and encode emits exactly one key (release-active). The rebind is one pass over at most `MAX_CONTACTS` entries and idempotent. Finding 1 is the one open gap.
- /crypto-privacy-review: clean. The identity key is public material. The book's AAD is `owner_local_aad(header)` (version, kind, owner tag) and the body is opaque, so the new field changes neither the AAD nor the sealed shape; HPKE info unchanged; no KAT. A `linkTag` book the current staging release wrote still opens and decodes (tested). No secret bytes in assertions. The opened body stays `Zeroizing`.
- /code-review: #1992 AC1-AC3 covered; both engine tests fail on the old code (it emits no abuse event: `.ok()?` and `continue`). #1995 AC1 (unit + engine: the full share refuses the next claim after a real cut) and AC2 (`contact_budget_full` after a real cut) covered. AC3 is not met after a real wave (finding 1). All tests run under `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` = "Engine simulation tests". PR body: bare `Closes #1992.` / `Closes #1995.`, no attribution, parentheses only around the scope, known windows stated — but known window 2 is an acceptance line.

## Design judgement: identity key, no rebind at the cut — agree

The contact book is per owner device. A cut runs on one device and holds the old-to-new tag map only there; a rebind at the cut leaves every other device's book at the old tag with no map to repair it, and a failed book write after the publish is permanent. The identity key needs no write, is what `revoke_invite_link` already pins across the pre-cut conversion (`link_identity`), and is owner-attested wherever a `CommittedLink` is read. The residual `Tag` window is bounded to books the current staging release wrote.

## Evidence

`cargo test -p cipherbox-engine`: 1958 unit tests and every integration suite pass. CI: 27 checks pass, the rest skipped. Tracker-ref grep on added lines: none. No git write and no repository file edit were made.

## Merge recommendation

Apply finding 1 (three lines plus one test, in scope of #1995 AC3), then merge. Findings 2-3 are optional. No blocker: a person revoke already reaches the placeholder, so the gap is a missed acceptance line, not a trust hole.</result>

<summary>Agent "Fix OPFS handle collision #2000" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I opened draft PR #2001 for issue #2000. CI is green, but only on the second attempt: the first run failed one Web E2E Smoke spec.

- **Branch:** `fix/2000-staging-store-serialize-handles`
- **PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/2001 (draft, body has no attribution, ends with `Closes #2000.`)
- **Head SHA:** `a12a4f3cba64553e3b85406350ea9d5b8d8558f7` (one commit, `fix(client): serialize access to one staged file behind one handle`, no attribution trailer)

**CI:** All required checks now pass, including "Web / Shared packages typecheck + tests". On the first attempt, `tests/cross-client.spec.ts:20` ("a grant reaches the second client on one nocache refresh") timed out waiting for the grant row after "convert claims". That spec is on the claim-conversion path this fix touches. Web E2E Smoke is on the known flaky list, so I re-ran the failed jobs once and they passed. I did not find the cause of the first failure, and I did not check whether this spec also fails on main.

**Local gates, all pass:** `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`, the client suite (783 tests), and the web suite (778 tests, run after a client dist rebuild).

**Design:**
- `OpfsStagingStore` has a private map `fileQueues` from file name to the point where the last queued task on that name settles.
- `serialized(fileName, task)` starts the task after the earlier task on that name settles. It stores a settle point that cannot reject, so a failed task does not block the next one.
- When the chain for a name goes idle, the store deletes its map entry, so the map does not grow.
- These go through the queue: reads, whole writes (temp write plus commit move), removes, each removal in `clear`, and the size check in `stagedBytesTotal`. A move or remove onto a file with an open handle also fails, so writes and removes needed the queue too.
- `stagedKeys` only lists names and opens no handle, so it does not use the queue.
- Tasks on different names still run at the same time. Writes keep the exclusive handle. I moved the read and write bodies into private `readStaged` and `writeStaged` methods, so the diff has no re-indentation.
- The queue is per store instance. There is one store per worker session.

**Tests** (`packages/client/src/seams/stagingStore.test.ts`): the fake handle now refuses a second open while one is open, and the fake directory refuses a move or remove onto an open file. There are five new tests:
- Two concurrent reads of one file return the same bytes.
- A read that starts during a write resolves after it, with the new bytes.
- A remove waits for a read that holds the file open.
- A held read of one file does not block a read of another file.
- The next access to a file runs after an earlier access fails.

Four of the five fail on the old code. The different-file test passes on both; it guards against serializing across all files.

<result>**PR 2001 review — head a12a4f3cb, `fix/2000-staging-store-serialize-handles`**

Local run: `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/client test` = 32 files, 783 tests, all pass. CI attempt 2 all green. Findings file: `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/w1963/findings-2001.md`.

## Findings (no blocker, no should-fix)

1. nit — `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/worktrees/agent-a42870e7f122cf9b2/packages/client/src/seams/stagingStore.ts:284` — `clear()` queues the removal of an in-flight temp under the temp name, while the writer that holds that temp's sync handle sits in the record name's chain. Scenario: `clear()` runs while `putStagedBytes` is between `createSyncAccessHandle` and `close`; real OPFS `removeEntry` on the temp rejects `NoModificationAllowedError` and `clear` throws. Pre-existing on main; the next `openStagedDir` sweeps the temp. No change needed.
2. nit — `stagingStore.ts:85` — the chain is per `OpfsStagingStore` instance, not per directory. Only `test/browser/conformance.worker.ts:164` makes two instances on one name, sequentially. Production makes one instance per worker (`src/worker/browserSeams.ts:57`). No change needed.
3. nit — `stagingStore.ts:255` — `removeStagedBytes` awaits `stagedDir()` outside the chain; read and write await it inside. The memo makes both orders equivalent. Optional consistency only.
4. nit — `stagingStore.test.ts:123` — the fake `move` refuses only an open destination; real OPFS also refuses an open source. The store closes the temp handle before the move, so the gap hides nothing.

## Verified claims

- Rejected chain: `settled` swallows the rejection; the next task runs (test 5). Idle delete: the delete callback registers before any later `.then(task)` on the same `settled`, and the identity check keeps a newer entry. No leak, no stale chain.
- Ordering: `fileQueues.set` runs synchronously inside `serialized`, so a read enqueued after a write in one turn observes the write (test 2).
- Re-entrancy: no queued task calls `serialized`. `clear` and `stagedBytesTotal` enqueue from outside; `writeStaged` touches the temp (unique, unqueued) and moves onto `fileName` inside `fileName`'s chain. No self-deadlock.
- Hang: each task awaits only OPFS calls. No engine callback, no network inside a task. A hang there blocked its own caller before this PR; the only new coupling is same-name.
- No cross-context lock: `blueprint/web-client.md:86-100` — one Web Lock holder per origin spawns the one engine worker; followers spawn no worker; sync access handles exist only in a worker. One in-process chain is sufficient.
- Moved bodies: `writeStaged` and `readStaged` are the old bodies unchanged; only the `fileName`/`staged` prologue stays in the public methods.
- Crypto/privacy: the `staged` copy already existed; the closure holds it until the task runs, then drops it. Nothing logged. No new retention.
- Tests fail on old code, confirmed by reading: (1) the second `createSyncAccessHandle` throws a DOMException; (2) the read has fewer awaits than the write and returns the old bytes before the move; (3) `removeEntry` on the held file rejects; (5) the second read rejects a DOMException, not `StagingIoError`. Test 4 guards global serialization: one chain would hold the `otherKey` read behind the gated read until the vitest timeout.
- Fake fidelity: OPFS rejects a second `createSyncAccessHandle`, and a `removeEntry` or `move` onto a file with an open handle, with `NoModificationAllowedError`; the fake matches. The gated open is a test lever only.
- Suite runs in `Web / Shared packages typecheck + tests` (green). PR body: bare `Closes #2000.`, no attribution. Title: parentheses around the scope only. Comments short, rationale only. Tracker Refs gate green.

## CI attempt 1 flake (`tests/web-e2e/tests/cross-client.spec.ts:20`, job 108263373987)

Failure: `share-grant-row` count 0 for 5 s after the convert click, no error shown. On the old code a handle collision surfaces as a thrown DOMException, not a silent 0. The click's pass polls the inbox, converts what it finds, then re-reads sharing (`apps/web/src/hooks/useSharingActions.ts:127-128`). When the owner tick has already acked the claim (ack-first, PR 1988) but its pointer has not landed, the click converts nothing and the re-read shows 0 rows until the tick's publish finishes; a 5 s expect misses it. That is the race `openUntilGranted` (reopen until rows show, 90 s) fixes on PR 1994; it is not on main. The queue in this PR adds only sub-millisecond, local, same-name waits with no network inside a task; it cannot tighten that 5 s wait.

- /crypto-privacy-review: not applicable (no crypto). No retention or logging issue.
- /code-review: spec — the three acceptance items hold; standards — AGENTS.md rules hold.

<result>Report (worktree at main 8baea3c45; no git writes; copy at `~/.claude/scratch/w1963/greptile-three-concerns.md`).

## Concern 1 — sweep vs a claim pending on another owner device: REAL

- `crates/engine/src/facade/link_sweep.rs:112-116` reads `held.pending_links()` from THIS device's record only; `crates/engine/src/grants/cut_set.rs:213-223` skips only those senders.
- Intake is ack-first (`crates/engine/src/facade/claim_conversion.rs:525-563`): after the ack, device B holds the only copy.
- Conversion judges the deadline against `acked_at` (`crates/engine/src/grants/invite.rs:1013-1016`), so a claim acked before the deadline is still convertible after it. With the link cut, `link_of_sender` (invite.rs:937-949) gives `LinkNotCommitted`, `disposition()` (invite.rs:218-223) maps it to `Settle`, and `run()` (claim_conversion.rs:468-475) drops the entry: no grant, no refusal count, no event.

Scenario: B acks claim C for link L at T &lt; deadline, then ticks later or goes offline. A sweeps at T &gt;= deadline, sees no pending entry, cuts L. B's next pass settles C silently. The claimant sees "expired", gets no grant, and cannot re-claim (L is cut and past its deadline).

ADR 0025 D2 and the PR 1989 body both say "never cuts a link with a pending entry" with no device qualifier. The two-device test `two_owner_devices_sweeping_one_window_publish_one_cut` (`crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs:6425`) holds no claim on the second device. Neither names the window.

Minimal fix: add `link_sweep_grace` to `SyncTimingProfile` (`crates/engine/src/profile.rs:85-129`, e.g. 2 x cadence) and in `sweep_links` use `now = scheduler.now() - grace` for `walk`/`cut_expired`. An online B converts within one tick. Cost: ADR 0025 E1 read-past-deadline grows by the grace. A device offline past the grace still loses the claim; that residue equals a manual link revoke on A with a claim held on B, which the design already accepts. Option (b) (B re-adds a row after the cut) is not minimal: once L is cut, B holds no link terms (permission, cap, ephemeral enc key) unless the link is recorded at intake. Add one simulation test: B acks before the deadline, A sweeps after it, B converts within the grace.

## Concern 2 — row lost after a link re-tag: NOT REAL as a person-revoke miss; the `remove_grantees` part is #1995 (PR 1999)

- `cut_set.rs:74-95` `revoked_rows`: an attested row matches by identity, so a tag move never affects it; an unattested or placeholder row matches by the book's sole enc subkey.
- `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:4296-4306`: a wave re-mints an unattested row with `UNATTESTED_IDENTITY_PK` but keeps `recipient_enc`, so the placeholder still matches by enc key through the converting device's book (`facade.rs:8806 sole_enc_subkey`).
- The only tag-keyed route is `LinkOrigin.link_tag` used by the link revoke with `remove_grantees` (`contact_store.rs:512-560`). That is exactly #1995, fixed in PR 1999. PR 1999 "Known windows" already names the placeholder residue, and the direct person revoke reaches it.

An owner device whose book never held the contact cannot name a stripped row, but that was true before the wave too (rendered unresolved, `facade.rs:4608-4618`). No new loss.

## Concern 3 — a conversion or import replaces a key an older row still needs: REAL (two paths)

- The tag is `blinded_tag(shared, name)` over the X25519 shared secret (`ledger.rs:167`), so a rotated subkey K2 mints a NEW tag. `convert_invite_claim` (`invite.rs:1046-1060`) finds a held grant by tag, else by identity on ATTESTED rows only. A stripped row is unattested, so a re-claim by the same identity under K2 is `Granted`, not `Unchanged`.
- `record_from_link` (`contact_store.rs:512-565`) removes the held entry and pushes the new contact with NO enc-subkey compare; only `record_unchanged_from_link` (`contact_store.rs:490-509`) refuses `RecipientKeyChanged`. The hand import `record` (`contact_store.rs:467-488`) replaces the entry unconditionally.

Scenario: write grantee W (identity I, key K1) strips its own row R1. W re-claims through an open link with code (I, K2): Granted, new row R2, book I -&gt; K2. Owner revokes I: `revoked_rows` takes R2 by identity; R1 is unattested and K1 != K2, so R1 survives and the rotation re-seals its blob for K1. W keeps reading. Same result when the owner hand-imports W's rotated code before the revoke. No per-tag revoke command exists (`revoke_grant` is by identity, `facade.rs:8790`).

Minimal fix: (1) `record_from_link` returns `RecipientKeyChanged` when the held entry's subkey differs (mirror of lines 506-508); (2) hand `record` of a known identity keeps the old subkey on the entry as a former key and `sole_enc_subkey`/`RevokedPerson.contact_enc_pk` carry every subkey ever bound, so `revoked_rows` reaches the old unattested row; or refuse the import and tell the owner to revoke first. Add a cut_set test: a stripped row under K1 plus an attested row under K2 fall to one person revoke.

## Reply texts to post

Concern 1: The window is real. The sweep on one owner device reads only its own conversion record, and intake is ack-first, so a claim that another owner device acked before the deadline is invisible to the sweep. That device then finds the link cut, and the conversion settles the claim with no grant and no refusal. Neither ADR 0025 D2 nor the PR body names this. Fix planned on main: the sweep cuts a link only after a grace period past its deadline, longer than the tick cadence, so an online device converts first. This PR's diff does not touch the sweep.

Concern 2: A person revoke matches an attested row by identity, so a link re-tag after a write wave cannot lose it. A stripped or placeholder row matches by the contact's encryption key, which a wave keeps. The only tag-keyed route is the link revoke with `remove_grantees` through the contact-book origin; that is #1995, fixed in PR 1999 by keying the origin by the link's identity key. This PR does not change that code.

Concern 3: Confirmed on two paths. `record_from_link` and the hand import replace a known identity's encryption key with no check; only `record_unchanged_from_link` refuses. A stripped row is unattested, so a re-claim under a rotated key is granted and the book flips, and the person revoke then misses the old row. Fix planned on main: refuse `claim-recipient-key-changed` on the granting path too, and keep the former subkeys on the book entry so the revoke reaches every row it bound. Not in the diff of this PR.

No cargo probe was run; the verdicts rest on the cited source lines.</result>

- **(a) and (b):** The link revoke now reads every subkey the book binds to each identity, current and former. A subkey bound to more than one identity matches no row. The book is read once through a new `contacts_with_bindings` method, which replaces `contacts_with_former_subkeys`. The person revoke uses the same data.
- **(c), done:** `record` and `record_from_link` now refuse a new contact whose subkey another identity binds, current or former, with `RecipientKeyChanged`. Without this, a write grantee with a second identity could make the key ambiguous, and its own stripped row would then escape every revoke. With it, no write path can create the overlap. The book codec does not refuse overlaps, so an existing book with one still loads.
- **Changed test:** the existing test `the_sharing_read_leaves_a_row_two_contacts_claim_unattested` imported an impostor code and expected success. That import is now a trust violation, so I renamed the test to `a_contact_code_that_binds_the_subkey_of_another_contact_is_refused` and updated it. A new unit test keeps the read-side ambiguity rule covered.
- **Tests:** the new cut_set test and the new owner_actions test (`RevokeInviteLink { remove_grantees: true }`) both failed on the old code. The owner_actions test failed because the revoke cut B's row. All listed gates pass, and both `kat_gen` runs show no drift.
- **CodeRabbit thread:** I posted the reply, one disposition comment, and the PR body with the test rows and the four `Closes` lines. The thread was already resolved when I checked.
- **Greptile thread (invalid):** it says a book written before this change, where two identities share a subkey, lets a link revoke leave a grantee's stripped row. That behaviour already exists on `origin/main`: the same count==1 rule and the test "a subkey two contacts bind names nobody". The released build never wrote former subkeys, and (c) stops new overlaps. The only way to close this for existing books is to change the ambiguity rule on all three paths, so the revoke either refuses or also cuts the other identity's row. That is your call if you want it.

<result>CodeRabbit's finding is **real**. I fixed it, rebased the branch on the new main, and pushed once. The thread has my reply and is resolved.

**Why it is real (PR 1993, comment 4109721701)**

The engine can cut people that the dialog does not show:
- `project_grant_ledger` in `crates/engine/src/facade.rs` (about line 4654) sends `viaLink` and the name only on a row that the owner attests. On any other row, `viaLink` is `null`.
- `link_cut_set` in `crates/engine/src/grants/cut_set.rs` (about lines 186-215) takes a row in two cases:
  - Its `via_link` equals the link tag, even when the writer broke the owner signature. The projection hides that tag.
  - The row has no attested label, and a contact that the link sourced holds its key. The `LinkSource` can be `Identity` or the legacy `Tag`.
- After a link is re-keyed, `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:4296-4339` moves the `via_link` of attested rows to the new tag, so those rows agree. An unattested row gets the placeholder label and no `via_link`. Its projected `viaLink` is `null`, but the cut still takes it through the contact book.
- The legacy `LinkSource::Tag` window never makes the cut bigger than what the dialog shows. After a write wave, the old tag stops matching and the cut gets smaller.

Before the fix, the dialog hid the choice when no visible row named the link and always sent `false`. Those people kept access.

**The fix (commit ef79045c7, `fix(web): always offer the remove choice on a link revoke`)**
- `apps/web/src/components/sharing/LinkSection.tsx`: the revoke prompt always shows the remove checkbox and sends the owner's choice to the engine unchanged. It still lists the people whose row names the link. It shows a count ("1 person") only when that list is exact.
- `apps/web/src/sharing/inviteLink.ts`: new `unclearGrants` returns the rows with no name and no link. A row with a name is attested, so the engine cuts it only when its visible `viaLink` matches. Only a row with no name and no link can hide a joiner. The prompt shows `// N grant(s) with no name and no link may have joined through it` only when such a row exists. Where the list is exact, it shows no hedge.
- `apps/web/src/stores/sharing.store.ts` and `packages/client/src/worker/protocol.ts`: the `viaLink` doc now says that `null` also means a row the owner does not attest.
- Tests in `ShareDialog.test.tsx` and `inviteLink.test.ts`:
  - Updated three existing tests.
  - Added two dialog tests: the unclear note appears and `true` is sent, and `true` is sent when no row names the link.
  - Added one `unclearGrants` unit test.
- The e2e page object needed no change, because `revokeFirstLink` does not use the checkbox.

**Rebase onto 53191d913 (PR 1994)**
- `tests/web-e2e/page-objects/share.page.ts`: I kept this branch's version. Main's `liveLinkExpiry` and `revokeLinkButton` belong to the old live-link panel. This branch replaced that panel with chips and no spec uses them now. The branch's `openUntilGranted` default is 180 s with a 30 s inner wait, so main's calls that pass a timeout still work.
- `tests/cross-client/src/share.ts`: I kept the branch's `toHaveValue('read')`, because the permission is now a select. Main's `grantee.openFiles()` merged in without a conflict.
- `tests/web-e2e/staging/invite.spec.ts`: I kept main's join flow and used the branch's `toHaveValue('read')`.
- `tests/web-e2e/tests/invite.spec.ts`:
  - I kept main's preview and join tests and its "a join lists the folder once" test. I dropped the branch's old "go to your files" steps, because that link is gone on main.
  - I kept the branch's `data-via-link` check in the tolerant form `/^(true|false)$/`, because the owner can convert the claim before the claimant reads the list.
  - The header comment now names both the preview and join and the conversion when the share dialog opens.
- The rebase applied the other files without a conflict.

**Push:** `git push --force-with-lease` at 01:42:13 UTC, one push. The new head is ef79045c777d7a03f8df7d4ec48f177d4b00ec1d.

1. **Web share dialog: keep.** The panel runs a conversion pass at once (`InviteLinkPanel.tsx:53`). The client mints with an empty owner name (`packages/client/src/facade.ts:412`). The dialog has a downgrade control only (`ShareDialog.tsx:134`).
2. **Invite page: delete.** The page runs the preview (`routes/invite/InvitePage.tsx:87`). It sends the name that the card collects (`InvitePage.tsx:126`, `InviteCard.tsx:16`).
3. **Admission cap: keep.** `Command::CreateInviteLink` has no cap field (`facade.rs:1419`). The mint sets `DEFAULT_ADMISSION_CAP` (`facade.rs:9281`, `grants/invite.rs:332`).
4. **Stale parent index: keep.** Only the command sites re-point after an `Unavailable` resolve (`claim_conversion.rs:584`). The tick places only a scope root that its walk proved (`claim_conversion.rs:1053`).
5. **Downgrade, two waves: keep.** `settled_scope` runs the owed wave (`facade.rs:9833`). Then `apply_permission` runs the cut (`facade.rs:9721`).
6. **`remove_grantees` contact route: delete.** `link_cut_set` now takes a row with a placeholder label by its `recipientEncPk` (`cut_set.rs:204`). The contact book keys the link by its identity key (`contact_store.rs:658`).
7. **One subkey under two contacts: reword.** The ambiguity rule stays (`facade.rs:4540`). No write path makes such a pair now (`contact_store.rs:488`, `:610`, `:665`). The row now says that only a book from an earlier build can hold a pair.
8. **Cut floor with no cut epoch: reword.** Main has no legacy read path. The state now occurs when the floor raise or the cut-epoch record fails after the publish lands (`cascade.rs:555`, `:754`).

After PR 1999, some statements outside the list were also incorrect. I changed them so that they agree with the code:

- The person revoke also matches a former subkey.
- The link revoke also finds a row through a contact that the link admitted.
- The sweep waits `link_sweep_grace` after the deadline.
- `claim-recipient-key-changed` now states its second cause.

No other sentence says that the web host does not have a control.

**Defects found.** Each is a sub-issue of #1963, with labels `v2-build` and `bug`, milestone `post-cutover`:
- **#2022:** after device B's write-link conversion moves the folder's scope root, device A shows "people unavailable" in the share dialog. This stays after a new sign-in on A. A fresh device reads correctly. Because of this, A cannot revoke the write link, so step 5 is `test.fail`.

**Design choices:**
- **Two devices:** each device is a browser context of its own. The login secret stays in the test process and reaches each context through a Playwright binding (`context.exposeFunction`), so no `evaluate` argument carries it. A device goes offline by closing its tabs.
- **Tick conversion (step 3):** the test proves that B's tick converted the claim, because B's engine emits `granteeJoined` before any share dialog opens on B.
- **Test layout:** the spec has 5 tests. The smoke test covers steps 1 to 3. The `@full` tests cover step 4, step 5, step 6 and step 7, and each one first runs steps 1 to 3.
- **Re-key in step 6:** B mints a link and revokes it, and then B uploads a file. The reader must list that file.
- **Expiry in step 7:** no expiry hook exists in the client or the sign-in hook. The test moves the page `Date.now` back only for the mint. The engine reads its own clock in its worker, which does not move. This adds no production code path. The CI profile sweeps in about 30 s.

**Staging:** I could not run the staging spec, but it passes the typecheck. It is two tests: steps 1, 2, 3, 6 and 4 in one, and step 7 in the other. Step 5 is left out until #2022 is fixed. The staging profile sweeps only 10 minutes after device B signs in. I estimate the Staging E2E run grows by 25 to 30 min. It took 33 min in its last run, and its step budget is 80 min.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I confirmed #2024 with an engine test. The cause is in the engine: the invite page does what the engine tells it. A fix is prototyped and passes, but it conflicts with the literal wording of ADR 0028 D5, so you need to make a ruling.

**1. Root cause (engine)**
The engine treats "a bookmark exists" as "this account holds the folder". A person revoke leaves the holder's personal bookmark on disk. Only the in-memory verdict changes to `revocation-signal`. The conversion already removed the link hold. When the holder opens the new link, two places block the join:
- **The preview:** `preview_invite_link` sets `joined = received.find(&amp;share.key()).is_some()` (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:11562-11567`). The web turns `joined` into "open folder" with no "join" (`apps/web/src/routes/invite/invitePreview.ts:26`).
- **The claim:** `claim_invite_link` has `personal = bookmarked &amp;&amp; link_hold.is_none()`. It returns `Ok(())`, posts no claim and writes no hold (`facade.rs:10229-10238`). So even if the page showed "join", the press would do nothing and report success.

The repro in a temp worktree ran mint, join, convert, person revoke, mint again. The holder verdict was `RevocationSignal`. The preview gave `state=Live joined=true`. The join gave `Done`. The owner inbox had 0 items and there was no hold.

**2. Why the tests missed it**
- `a_device_serves_a_grantee_again_once_another_device_commits_it_again` (`crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs:8433`) proves the owner side converts a cut person again through a new link. But it posts the claim with the owner harness. It never runs the holder's claim or preview.
- No holder join test starts from a personal bookmark that a cut emptied. `a_preview_of_a_joined_link_reads_the_root_the_join_adopted` checks only "bookmark exists means joined".
- Step 6 of the web e2e tests went around the defect with a direct contact-code grant.

**3. The fix (engine only, no web change)**
- **`crates/engine/src/grants/link_read.rs`:** the link read gets the session enc secret. After the commitment verifies, it computes `personal`: this account's own tag names a committed Personal entry, and a signed blob exists at that tag. This is the same test that `facts_from` uses for `blob_present`. `personal` goes on the three Live variants. The check does not use the in-memory verdict, because the invite page is a fresh session after a page load and that verdict can be empty.
- **Preview (`facade.rs`):** on a live link, `joined = bookmarked &amp;&amp; (personal || the held link is this link with a claim)`. The other states do not change.
- **Claim (`facade.rs`):** a live read with `personal == false` is a cut bookmark. It does not stop early. It reconciles the bookmark from the fragment, then posts the claim and writes the hold. An unavailable read keeps today's early return.
- **Invariants:** `personal` is read only after the commitment verifies, so a refused set is still a TrustViolation. No encode path changes, and there is no new clock or RNG and no `debug_assert`. The preview still stores nothing.

The prototype was about 86 lines added and 14 removed, in the temp worktree only. With it, the claim posts, the owner converts, and the holder gets back to `Granted` through its personal grant (not the link), with the hold removed. All 189 `owner_actions`, 7 `invite_claims` and the `link_read` lib tests pass. I committed nothing and removed the worktree.

**Decision for you:** ADR 0028 D5 says a link this account already joined means "a bookmark exists". The fix narrows that. ADR 0025 E4 ("A cut person who holds another live link rejoins with no owner step") and ADR 0025 D3 support the fix. `blueprint/web-client.md:291` and `blueprint/engine.md:1169` say only "already joined", so they stay correct. Decide if D5 needs an ADR amendment, or if E4 already covers this.

**E2E change:** in step 6 of `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts` and `tests/web-e2e/staging/link-first.spec.ts` (branch test/1979-link-first-two-device-e2e), replace the contact-code grant with this sequence: device A mints a new read link, the reader joins, A converts, and the reader shows `granted via-link=false`.

**4. Regression tests** (in `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs`, next to line 8433)
- `a_person_the_owner_cut_joins_again_through_a_new_link`: the full sequence above, ending at `Granted`, not through the link, with the hold removed.
- `a_preview_of_a_granted_personal_bookmark_through_a_new_link_is_joined`: a person who is still granted still sees joined, and the join posts nothing.

**5. Risk and batching**
- The change touches only the holder's link read path. The owner conversion does not change.
- A holder whose earlier link was revoked before conversion will now also see "join" on a new link. The claim path already allowed that; only the preview hid it.
- `crates/engine/src/facade.rs` is shared with #2022 and #2023, but in different regions, so a text conflict is unlikely.
- `grants/link_read.rs` is used only by this fix. If #2023 edits `facts_from` in `grants/received_status.rs`, reuse its predicate instead of a copy.
- Batch #2024 with #2023, because both are on the holder side. #2022 is on the owner side.

# Diagnosis #2022: owner device A reads no sharing state after device B's write-scope cut

## 1. Root cause (layer: engine)

I reproduced the defect in the engine simulation. The web and client layers behave correctly.

- **B's cut raises only B's floor.** The write-scope cut on device B runs a write wave. The wave moves the folder root and seals its owner-write-blob at write epoch 2. B raises only its own write-epoch floor (`crates/engine/src/facade/claim_conversion.rs:835`).
- **A keeps floor 1.** Device A kept write-epoch floor 1 for the folder scope from its own link mint. Floors are durable, so a new sign-in keeps them.
- **The seed does not open at floor 1.** The owner recovers the write scope seed only at the standing floor (`crates/engine/src/net/adopter.rs:640-653`, `open_write_scope_seed_at`). The blob at epoch 2 does not open at floor 1, so `write_scope_seed` is `None`. `write_plane_of_gated` then gives `ResolveFailure::Unavailable` (`crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:1602`).
- **The sharing read turns this into `null`.** `scope_sharing` (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:11774`, `resolve_anchored(...).ok()?`) turns that failure into `state: None`.
- **All owner actions fail the same way.** Every owner action on the folder uses the same resolve (`OwnerRotationNet::gated_write_plane`, `rotation.rs:1805-1822`). In the simulation, a link mint on A after the cut also fails with `Seam("descendant record unavailable")`.
- **Nothing raises A's floor to 2.** Only a scope-pointer consult (`net/pointer_fetch.rs:134`) raises the floor of a device that did not run the wave. There are three consult paths, and none runs on A:
  - **Tick walk.** `ScopeWalk::recover_write_plane_from_pointer` (`rotation.rs:1401-1406`, from #1646) returns early "while a floor stands". A fresh device has no floor, so it consults and seeds floor 2. That is why the third device works.
  - **Polled tick consult.** It consults only `FocusWindow::open_shared_scopes` (`sync/tick.rs:166`). No production code fills that field, so no tick ever consults the owner's own interior scopes.
  - **On-access consult.** The owner access paths build their nets with `PointerConsultArm::Refused`. `ConsultReason::OnAccess` (`sync/pointer.rs:54`) is defined but has no caller.
- **Content reads still work** because the cut does not change the read plane.

## 2. Why the tests missed it

- `a_write_claim_converts_on_the_other_owner_devices_tick_with_one_cut` (`crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs:5480`) asserts the sharing read only on the converting device (the phone). The phone raised its own floor. The test never reads on the minting device.
- `a_claim_converts_on_the_owners_other_device` uses a read link, so no write wave runs.
- The #1646 tests cover a device with no floor. No test covers a device whose floor is below the pointer's write epoch after another owner device ran a write wave.

## 3. The fix

**Location.** The change goes in `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs`, in `OwnerRotationNet::gated_write_plane`. All owner resolves go through this one function: the sharing read, mint, grant, revoke, conversion and repoint.

**Change.**
1. `write_plane_of_gated` gives `Unavailable` at `RootAnchor::Descendant`, and the section holds an owner-write-blob. In that case, run an on-access `PointerConsult::run` for the scope. It raises the write floor on sight.
2. Open the blob again at the new floor, set `root.write_scope_seed`, and read the write plane again.
3. If the pointer is absent or unavailable, give `Unavailable`. If the consult rejects the pointer, give `Rejected`.
4. `rotation.rs:2997` is a second `write_plane_of_gated` site. Check whether the sweep path needs the same change.

**Invariants to keep:**
- **A rejected pointer is a TrustViolation.** A rejected pointer (bad signature or write-epoch regression) must map to `ResolveFailure::Rejected` and then to `EngineError::TrustViolation` through `from_resolve_failure`, never to `Unavailable`. `scope_sharing` drops errors with `.ok()?`, so it must also emit a trust-violation event for a rejection.
- **Floors stay monotonic.** Only `PointerConsult` raises a floor, and only to the epoch the owner signed.
- **The stale-copy case does not change.** When the pointer epoch equals the floor, the blob still does not open and the answer stays `Unavailable`.
- **Other invariants.** No encode path changes, so encode/decode symmetry is not affected. The fix adds no clock, no RNG and no `debug_assert`.
- **`PointerConsultArm` doc.** The doc says a `Refused` pass consults nothing. Update it to name the on-access consult as a separate trigger, or pass a `ConsultReason::OnAccess` arm.

**Blueprint and glossary text the fix obeys:**
- `blueprint/engine.md` "Pointer planes": "the pointer resolve joins the focus-window tick for open shared scopes, runs on access for cached ones, and is the first act on cold start."
- `CONTEXT.md` "Scope pointer": "consulted on the sync tick for open shared scopes, on access for cached ones, and first on cold start."
- `CONTEXT.md` "Floor law": floors "cold-seed from the re-point object's owner-vouched epochs".

**Optional (same PR or a follow-up issue).** `ScopeWalk::recover_write_plane_from_pointer` can also consult when a floor stands and the blob does not open, paced by `pointer_consult_interval`. The tick would then repair A with no owner action. The e2e needs only the on-access fix.

**Prototype result.** The prototype makes the sharing read and the mint on A pass. The full `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` suite passes with it (0 failures).

## 4. Regression test

Put it in `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs`, next to line 5480, using `GrantScenario`:
1. Device A (`fx.engine`) mints a write link.
2. `post_claims` posts one claim.
3. A phone (`boot_owner` on a second `FakeDevice`) converts it on its tick.
4. Assert that `fx.engine.sharing(fx.folder).state` is `Some` and holds the write row.
5. Assert that `try_mint_link_at(Read)` on A gives `Ok`.
6. Assert that A's write-epoch floor (`write_epoch_floor_key`) moves from 1 to 2.

Before the fix, steps 4 and 5 fail on main: `state` is `None`, and the mint gives `Seam("descendant record unavailable")`.

Add a rejection case too: plant a pointer below A's floor, and assert that a command on A gives `TrustViolation`.

Then remove `test.fail` from `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts:247`, and add step 5 to `tests/web-e2e/staging/link-first.spec.ts`.

## 5. Risk and hot files

- **Hot files for this fix.** `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs` (`gated_write_plane` and a new helper) and `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs`. `facade.rs` changes only if the trust-violation event is added in `scope_sharing`.
- **#2023** (a grantee session keeps a read view after conversion) is probably in `grants/received_status.rs` and the facade grantee refresh. It may share `facade.rs` and `owner_actions.rs` with this fix, but not `gated_write_plane`.
- **#2024** (the invite page stays at `joined`) is mostly in `apps/web`, with a possible engine preview part in `facade.rs`. I expect no overlap with this fix.
- **New cost.** An owner resolve of a root that does not open now costs one pointer GET per access. A root whose blob is sealed below the floor for good pays that GET on each owner access. Pace it if the sweep resolves such a root on every tick.
- **Interaction with a running wave.** During a wave, a consult can raise the floor after the re-point lands and before the facade raises it itself. The tick's anchor consult already does the same, and the raise only goes up, so this adds no new hazard.</result>

<result>I found 13 simplification findings. I read every changed file, checked each item against the worktree, and dropped three of the suggested checks after confirming them.

**Findings**

1. `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts:120-178` and `tests/web-e2e/staging/link-first.spec.ts:93-94`: `let scope = ''; let link!: URL; let readerPage!: Page; let bTab!: Tab;` pass values out of `test.step` closures through mutable variables. Playwright 1.56 returns the body's value from `test.step`, so `const link = await test.step('1. ...', async () =&gt; { ...; return link; })` does the same job. The file also mixes a `''` sentinel with `!` assertions for the same pattern. Cost: four mutable variables declared away from where they are set, and the `!` stops the compiler from catching a missed assignment.

2. `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts:141-153` repeats `join()` from lines 99-111, and `tests/web-e2e/staging/link-first.spec.ts:116-126` repeats `join()` from lines 73-83. Each copy differs only by the headline and entries checks between `expectState('joinable')` and `name.fill`. The simpler form splits the helper in two: one part opens the link, signs in and waits for "joinable", then returns the `InvitePage`; the other fills the name and joins. Step 2 then calls both and puts its preview checks in between. Cost: the claim sequence exists four times in two files, and a change to the claim route has to land in all four.

3. `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts:99-111`: the new `join()` rebuilds `claimHere` from `tests/web-e2e/sharing.ts:45`. That function already takes a `ClaimSignIn` so a caller can supply its own sign-in (here, `signInHeld`). The simpler form adds an optional `name` to `claimHere` and passes `start: () =&gt; vault.signInHeld(holder.login.accountId)`. Cost: a second claim helper next to the shared one, with weaker checks (no `hash === ''` check, no account check).

4. `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts:219-224`: `writer.online()` already returns a `Tab` that holds `files` and `vault`. The test keeps only `page` and then builds a new `FilesPage` and `VaultPage` from it. The simpler form is `const tab = await writer.online();` followed by `tab.files` and `tab.vault`. Cost: two copies of the same page objects, and the `Tab` type loses its purpose at this call site.

5. `tests/web-e2e/devices.ts:57`: the doc says `page()` returns "A page of this device that holds no session yet". That is false for the writer. `join(writer)` creates the page and signs it in, and the later `writer.online()` gets that same signed-in page back through `pages()[0]`. The simpler form is a comment that states what the code does: "the device's open page, or a new one". Cost: the comment misleads anyone who reasons about session state from it.

6. `tests/web-e2e/page-objects/share.page.ts:294-300`: the `try/finally` in `mintExpiringIn` restores `Date.now` only when `mintLink` throws. That can only happen after the test has already failed, and every caller closes the tab (`a.offline()` / `offline(page)`) soon after. A plain sequence of patch, mint, restore does the same job. Cost: an extra block and a second `window as unknown as { restoreNow }` cast for a path that changes nothing.
   - The same method ties the literal `'7 days'` to `7 * DAY_MS` (line 292). If one changes without the other, the deadline is wrong and nothing reports it.

7. `tests/web-e2e/page-objects/share.page.ts:258`: `lifetime` is still positional in front of the new `terms` object. Three call sites (`tests/link-first.spec.ts:135`, `:191`, `staging/link-first.spec.ts:110`) must pass `undefined` to reach `terms`. The simpler form moves `lifetime` into `LinkTerms`, so the call is `mintLink({ ownerName })` or `mintLink({ lifetime: '30 days' })`. Cost: a placeholder argument at each call site, and two ways to say what one mint carries.

8. `tests/web-e2e/page-objects/share.page.ts:75-80`: `linkChipsFor` filters the chips by a nested `has:` on `share-link-summary` with `hasText`. The chip contains the summary text (`LinkSection.tsx:227`), so `this.linkChips.filter({ hasText: \`${label} · \` })` does the same job. Also, the only caller passes `'write'` (`tests/link-first.spec.ts:258`), so the `'read'` → `'view'` branch never runs. Cost: an extra locator level and an untested branch.

9. `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts:215`, `:255`, `:338`: each `test.step` title repeats its test's title, prefixed with a number. Each step also wraps the whole rest of the test body. The simpler form drops the wrapper, or keeps only a short numbered title. Cost: two copies of the same title to keep in sync, plus one level of nesting.
   - In the staging spec, the steps run in the order 1, 2, 3, 6, 4 (`staging/link-first.spec.ts:148`, `:199`). The numbers do not match the order in which the steps run.
   - The staging test title at line 236 carries a step number ("7. ...").

10. `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts:66-69`: `standing()` has one caller, the poll in `awaitStanding`. It also throws away the `{landed, refusal}` result that `refreshed()` returns. The simpler form inlines `standing()` into the poll body. Cost: one more name to follow for one line of work.
    - Both specs also define a free function `awaitStanding`, while `SharedPage` already has an `awaitStanding` method (`shared.page.ts:65`) and a private `awaitRow` poll loop with a nudge option. The new helper polls the same row a third way. The simpler form is a scope-aware method on `SharedPage` that reuses `awaitRow`, with a regex matcher. Cost: two helpers with the same name that do different things, and three poll loops over one row.

11. `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts:81`: `listsUntil(page, name, count)` has two callers (lines 240 and 328), and both pass `count = 1`. The simpler form drops the parameter and asserts `1`. Cost: a parameter that exists for a case nobody uses.

12. `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts:166`: "Nothing on B opened the share dialog yet, so the conversion is the tick's." This explains a path that is absent, which AGENTS.md "Comments" names as a smell. Cost: the claim becomes false as soon as someone adds a dialog open before line 167.

13. `tests/web-e2e/staging/link-first.spec.ts:215-216`: the file builds `new SharedPage(writer)` twice on consecutive lines. One local variable does the same job. Cost: minor, but it is one more pattern that differs from the rest of the file.
