- **`Flow` / `readLinkOverTwoDevices` returning `a`, `b`, `bTab`:** some test uses each field. The write-link test uses `a`, `bTab` and `scope`. The revoke test adds `readerPage`. The re-key test uses all five. The sweep test uses `a`, `b` and `bTab`. `b` cannot be derived from `bTab`, because `Tab` has no reference to its device. This is not a finding.

<result>I found the engine root cause (#2023) and reproduced it in an engine test. # #2023 diagnosis: a write link holder keeps a read view after conversion

## 1. Root cause (engine)

A session that joined through the link cannot write after the conversion. A new session on the same device writes at once.

- **The join:** the write link join adds the shared folder to the render tree at the old scope root name, R0. `merge_grafted` (`crates/engine/src/grants/received_status.rs:233`, the `None` arm near line 253) sets `meta.ipns_name` only when it creates the node.
- **The conversion:** it does a write-scope cut, which moves the scope root to R1 (`crates/engine/src/facade/claim_conversion.rs:618-647`, `cut_write_scope` at 810).
- **The bookmark moves to R1** by the pointer heal (`follow_held_pointers` / `HoldChange::Heal`) and by the `accept_share` reconcile (`grants/accept.rs:1231`). The personal blob opens at R1, and the write seed is deposited against R1. The `/shared` row shows `granted`, `write`, `via-link=false`.
- **The render node keeps R0.** The `Some(meta)` arm (`received_status.rs:248-251`) only renames. It never updates `ipns_name`.
- **The drain uses R0.** `grafted_write_passes` (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:5035-5068`) takes its name from `base.node(root).ipns_name`, and the drain scope uses `root_name: &amp;pass.name` (`facade.rs:7751-7760`). The R1 write seed does not derive R0. The drain treats this as the name-skew case, which waits with no event (`sync/drain.rs:7515`). So no write from that session ever lands.
- **A new session works** because it adds the folder from the bookmark, which is at R1.

**Evidence.** I added a test to `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs` in the temp worktree. It runs: a read link and a reader, device B converts on its tick, a write link, the holder reads through the link, and B converts again.
- **Same session, before the fix:** the row changed to Write. A queued create stayed in the queue for every pass, with no dead letter and no hold.
- **New session:** it drained the create at once.
- **Same session, with the fix below:** it drained the create. All 1968 engine lib tests and all 190 `owner_actions` tests passed.
- The test and the fix are in `~/.claude/scratch/w1963/diag/2023/diag-repro-and-fix.patch`.

**Open point:** in my test, `SnapshotView.permission` changed to `Write` one tick after the row did. The e2e reports `read`, and I did not reproduce that. One likely cause:
- `grafted_write_roots` is set at `facade.rs:7602`, before the received-share pass at `facade.rs:7972` deposits the seed. So the permission changes one tick later, and no `SnapshotUpdated` event comes with that change.
- A host view painted between those ticks stays `read`. It changes only after another event or a pull, and the web pulls only when `manualRefresh` succeeds (`apps/web/src/engine/snapshotStore.ts`).
- This lag also fits the `write-grant.spec.ts` flake with 2 workers. A direct grant has no stale name, but it has this one-tick lag.

## 2. Why the existing tests missed it

- `received_status.rs` `a_root_a_write_wave_moved_still_opens_through_the_pointer` checks the healed bookmark and the listing. It does not check the render node's `ipns_name`.
- `owner_actions.rs` `a_write_link_mints_at_read_and_its_conversion_runs_one_write_cut` and `a_write_claim_converts_on_the_other_owner_devices_tick_with_one_cut` check only the owner side.
- The `WriteGrantee` harness (`facade.rs` tests, near line 19700) accepts a write grant directly, so its folder node already has the final name. No test adds the folder before the root moves and then writes.
- The name-skew wait sends no event, so nothing reports the failure.

## 3. The fix

1. **`merge_grafted` in `grants/received_status.rs`:** when the node exists and its `ipns_name` differs from `share.scope_root_name`, set it to the bookmark name. Include that change in the `SnapshotUpdated` condition. This is the patch I tested, about 10 lines.
2. **`facade.rs` near line 7602:** when the new `grafted_write_roots` set differs from the old one, send `Event::SnapshotUpdated`. Then the host repaints on the tick that proves or drops a write pass.

The fix must keep these invariants:
- The name comes only from the verified bookmark, healed by the owner-signed pointer (ADR 0024 D5 step 3) or by the gated `accept_share`. It never comes from a record's own word.
- `seed_names` / `deposit_write_seed` (`facade.rs:4801-4825`) stays the proof that the seed names the root.
- A gate rejection stays a TrustViolation.
- The fix adds no `debug_assert!`, no clock and no RNG. It changes no wire format or encode path, so encode/decode symmetry does not apply.

Normative text:
- **ADR 0024 D4:** "At conversion the owner engine mints the personal row at the conversion permission, with the write material and a write-scope cut when the folder has none yet … The grantee then writes under its own writer pseudonym."
- **ADR 0024 D2:** "The persist that records the first personal open also deletes `linkSecret`. The person is then a grantee."
- **ADR 0023**, as the issue states it: each URL holder becomes a writer after conversion, with no owner step.

## 4. Regression tests

- **Engine test in `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs`.** It uses `GrantScenario`, `mint_link_at(Permission::Write)`, a holder session on `fx.recipient_device`, and a phone device at the owner identity that converts on its tick. The patch has the diagnostic version. The steps:
  1. The holder claims and ticks until the link read shows the folder.
  2. The phone ticks and converts.
  3. The holder ticks twice.
  4. Assert that `snapshot(shared).permission == Write`.
  5. Send `Command::Create` under the shared root and tick.
  6. Assert that the queue is empty and that the owner lists the child at the moved root.
- **Unit assertion in `a_root_a_write_wave_moved_still_opens_through_the_pointer`:** the render node of `SCOPE` has `ipns_name == scope_root_name()`.
- **For fix 2:** assert that `SnapshotUpdated` is sent on the tick where the root enters `grafted_write_roots`.
- **E2E:** step 4 of `tests/web-e2e/tests/link-first.spec.ts` and of `staging/link-first.spec.ts` then drops the second sign-in.

## 5. Risk and shared files

- **Fix 1** changes the render name of the shared folder's root. The focus leg reads that name through `scope_root_record_name` (`facade.rs:4139`) to walk back a lagging record. R1 is the correct name for that walk.
- **Fix 2** sends one more event each time the set changes. This costs little, because the store merges pulls that overlap.
- **Shared hot files:**
  - `crates/engine/src/facade.rs` is touched by all three defects. #2022 is in `scope_sharing`, with owner state left stale after another device's write-scope cut. That is the same class of defect: a stale device-local root name.
  - `grants/received_status.rs` is touched by #2023, and possibly by #2024 (the state of a cut person's bookmark drives the invite page to `joined`).
  - The invite route in `apps/web` and `grants/invite*.rs` belong to #2024.
- **Batching:** put #2022 and #2023 in one PR.</result>

<result>The two resolve sites are too high for fix 1: the tick walk keeps the bug. Fix 2 is at the wrong layer, and the drain name should come from the seed. Fix 3 reads the fact at the right place, but the rule that decides "joined" is now written twice.

**Fix 1: owner write seed after another device's write-scope cut**

1. `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:1405-1411` (`recover_write_plane_from_pointer`). **Deeper change available.** The walk still returns `Unavailable` when a floor stands. Its comment says the record that did not open is only a stale copy. Fix 1 shows that this is false. The tick walk and the drain for a descendant stay dark until a sweep or an on-access resolve raises the floor.
   - Proposal: when the standing floor does not open the blob, consult the pointer and open again at the floor the consult leaves. Put this "open, else consult and open" step in one free function. `ScopeWalk` and `OwnerRotationNet` both have its inputs: transport, floors, scope_keys, identity, payload_version and enc_secret.
   - Cost now: the same step exists in two copies, and the walk heals only by side effect.

2. `rotation.rs:1822` and `:3038`: the diff adds the same `write_scope_seed.is_none()` check twice. **Deeper change available.** The owner turns a gated root into a write plane in `write_plane_of_gated`, which has three callers: `:1829`, `:3049` and `:3464` (`promoted_root`). The diff covers two of them.
   - Proposal: one `OwnerRotationNet` method that finds the seed (with the consult) and opens the write plane. It replaces all three calls.
   - Cost now: `promoted_root` does not get the fix, and each new caller must remember the check.

3. `crates/engine/src/net/adopter.rs:640-653` (`recover_write_scope_seed`). **Current depth is right: do not put the fix here.** The adopter is the adoption gate, and grantees use it too. It has no transport and no pointer keys. A network consult there would add I/O and a pointer-plane dependency to every gate pass.

4. `rotation.rs:1557-1563` against `:1422`: the new helper maps a rejected pointer to `Rejected`, the walk maps it to `Unavailable`. **Deeper change available.** The shared helper from item 1 should return `PointerConsultError` unchanged. Each caller then maps it. The walk can keep `Unavailable` for the drain's valve and still report the trust violation.
   - Cost now: one owner-signed rejection gives two verdicts. It now emits a trust violation on the sharing read (`facade.rs:11781-11799`) but not on the tick.

5. `rotation.rs:156-162` (`PointerConsultArm`). **Deeper change available.** The diff adds an exception: the on-access consult runs even under `Refused`. So the arm no longer means "this pass consults nothing".
   - The new helper also ignores `RootAnchor`. Under `VaultRoot` it consults a scope pointer, but the walk says the vault root reads none (`:1325-1327`, `:1357`).
   - Cost now: an extra fan-out GET whenever the vault root's seed does not open, and a rule that is no longer true.

**Fix 2: holder drain name after the owner's cut**

6. `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:5054` (`grafted_write_passes`). **Deeper change available.** The drain takes its name from the render node. The seed is already checked against the bookmark name when it is stored (`received_status.rs:1061-1067`, `deposit_write_seed`).
   - Proposal: set `name = derive_write_name(&amp;write_scope_seed, scope_id)`. The name and the seed then have one source and cannot disagree.
   - Cost now: the drain is only correct if `merge_grafted` has updated the render tree first. That is a tick-order dependency on a display structure.

7. `crates/engine/src/grants/received_status.rs:245-262` (`merge_grafted` moves `ipns_name`). **Current depth is right, but only for display.** Keep it so the rendered tree stays consistent. After item 6 the drain no longer depends on it. Change the comment ("the grafted drain pass publishes under this node's name") so it does not claim that.

8. `facade.rs:7602-7609` (`SnapshotUpdated` when the write-root set changes). **Current depth is right.** The code that changes the set sends the event, and it sends it only when the set is different.

**Fix 3: cut bookmark seen as "joined"**

9. `crates/engine/src/grants/link_read.rs:265-270` and `received_status.rs:1167-1176` (`holds_committed_blob`). **Current depth is right.** `read_link_entry` holds the verified owner-signed set, so it is the correct place to read the fact. Moving the check into a shared function lets `facts_from` and the link read use one rule.
   - Reusing `facts_from`/`classify` directly would need a read-epoch floor that the link read does not load, and it needs `share.scope_root_name` to already be the healed name. The bool is the `blob_present` fact alone, which is enough here.

10. `facade.rs:10236-10242` and `facade.rs:11567-11589`: the facade has two different rules for "joined". **Deeper change available.** The claim, when the read returns `Unavailable`, uses the old rule: `bookmarked &amp;&amp; no hold || claimed`. The preview, on every arm other than Live, uses `joined = bookmarked`.
    - Proposal: one function on `ReceivedSharesList`, next to `claimed_through` (`accept.rs:348`). Signature: `standing(key, invite_secret, live: Option&lt;bool&gt;) -&gt; {Personal, ClaimedHere, Cut, Absent}`. The claim and the preview both call it.
    - Cost now: two versions of the rule have already drifted apart on the not-Live arms.

11. `link_read.rs:219` and `:327`: `personal` is carried through `LinkEntryRead`, `JoinRead` and `PreviewRead`. **Deeper change available, small.** `JoinRead::Live` and `PreviewRead::Live` could hold one shared "live entry" struct. Then a new fact from the verified set is added in one place, not three.

I made no file edits.</result>

**(b) Baseline smells (all judgement calls).**

1. Duplicated Code — `rotation.rs:1842-1870` (`write_seed_on_access`) repeats the shape of `recover_write_plane_from_pointer` at `rotation.rs:1400-1432`: read `owner_write_blob`, build `PointerConsult`, `consult.run`, read `write_epoch_floor`, `open_write_scope_seed_at`. The two differ only in the floor precondition and the error type. Extract one "consult, then open at the vouched floor" helper. The `PointerConsult { scope_keys: self.keys.scope_keys, owner_identity: self.keys.identity, payload_version: self.payload_version }` literal now appears at `rotation.rs:1862` and `rotation.rs:3103` in the same impl; a `fn pointer_consult(&amp;self)` removes both.
2. Duplicated Code — the `if root.write_scope_seed.is_none() { root.write_scope_seed = Some(self.write_seed_on_access(..).await?) }` block appears at `rotation.rs:1824-1826` and `rotation.rs:3038-3044`. Fold it into the root-fetch step.
3. Duplicated Code — the `LinkReader { share: &amp;share, hold: &amp;hold, owner: &amp;owner, invitee: &amp;invitee, my_enc_secret: session.enc_subkey() }` literal is built twice from the same `LinkOpening` fields (`facade.rs:10205-10211`, `facade.rs:11575-11581`). Give `LinkOpening` a `reader(&amp;self, secret)` method. The `LinkReader` bundle itself removes the previous five-parameter Data Clump; that part is an improvement.
4. Primitive Obsession — `live_personal: Option&lt;bool&gt;` (`facade.rs:10209-10227`) encodes three states: read did not answer, granted in own name, cut. The reads at `facade.rs:10238` (`unwrap_or_else`) and `facade.rs:10279` (`== Some(false)`) decode it by hand. A three-variant enum names the states.
5. Mysterious Name — `personal: bool` on `JoinRead::Live`, `PreviewRead::Live`, `LinkEntryRead::Live` (`link_read.rs:152, 236, 327`). The docs say "`personal` is `LinkEntryRead::Live`'s", so a reader needs two hops to learn the meaning. `granted_in_own_name` reads at the use site. CONTEXT.md does use "personal row/blob", so this is borderline.

No Feature Envy, Shotgun Surgery, Speculative Generality, Message Chain, or Middle Man of note.</result>

<result># Security review: link-first stale-roots diff

No finding meets the bar (new, exploitable, &gt;0.8 confidence). Traces and verdicts follow.

## Traces

**T1. `write_seed_on_access` trust boundary** (`crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:1842-1870`, `crates/engine/src/net/pointer_fetch.rs:104-140`, `crates/engine/src/sync/pointer.rs:248-262`, `crates/engine/src/net/adopter.rs:681-699`).
The consult opens the pointer block under the derived pointer read key and verifies it under `self.keys.identity` (core `open_pointer_payload`). A block that does not open or verify is `Rejected`. `write_epoch_regression` refuses a vouched epoch below the floor as `Rejected`. `advance_write_epoch_on_sight` raises monotonic-max only to the owner-signed epoch. The seed opens under HPKE AAD bound to that floor, so an attacker cannot select the open epoch. A blob that does not open at the raised floor is `Unavailable`, which matches the pre-existing `recover_write_scope_seed` convention (`adopter.rs:626-641`: keyless, never a verdict). Verdict: fail-closed. No fail-open.

**T2. `Rejected` swallowed into `Unavailable`/`None`?** `write_seed_on_access` maps `PointerConsultError::Rejected` to `ResolveFailure::Rejected`; `resolve_scope` maps it through `SweepResolveFailure::from`; `scope_sharing` (`facade.rs:11784-11797`) now emits a trust-violation event on `Rejected` and stays `None` on other failures. Verdict: no new swallow. The change removes one (`.ok()?`).

**T3. Consult during a running wave** (`gate/floor.rs:578-590`, `rotation.rs:2087-2090`, `rotation.rs:4574-4600`). The publish holds `WriteEpochLease` across sign and put. `advance_write_epoch_on_sight` defers when the scope is leased and returns the unchanged floor; the on-access open then fails and reports `Unavailable`. A raise that lands between enumeration and publish already fails the publish with `Rejected` (`node.write_epoch &lt;= plane.write_epoch`) and is pre-existing. Verdict: the new consult cannot strand the write plane.

**T4. `personal` in `link_read.rs:265-270`**. Computed after `verify_commitment_in_force` (`link_read.rs:224-235`). `holds_committed_blob` requires the tag in the owner-signed commitment and a blob at that tag. An attacker cannot forge `personal = true` for an uncommitted tag. A stale-but-genuine set can make `personal` wrong in both directions, but the impact is one redundant claim post plus a transient `permission: Read` heal (`pending_link_bookmark`, `link_read.rs:60-84`) that the next tick corrects, because a holder reads its personal tag first (`received_status.rs:786-788`). Verdict: below the bar (low impact, needs a routing position, no confidentiality or integrity loss).

**T5. `claim_invite_link` reconcile on `live_personal == Some(false)`** (`facade.rs:10279-10281`). `BookmarkKey` is `(sharer_identity_pk, scope_id)` (`accept.rs:137`), and `sharer_identity_pk` comes from the fragment's owner, which also verifies the commitment. A fragment under a different owner cannot touch another owner's bookmark. Under the same owner the fragment must open a committed link tag, so the writer of the fragment holds a real invite secret from that owner. The healed root name is the pointer-vouched root, not a fragment value. Verdict: no cross-owner overwrite. Same-owner overwrite needs the owner's own link and is transient.

**T6. `merge_grafted` `ipns_name` source** (`received_status.rs:249-256`). `share.scope_root_name` is written only by the accept flow from a gate-adopted record, by `claim_invite_link` from the consulted pointer root, and by the tick's heal. An own-tree id collision is refused before merge (`received_status.rs:972-974`). Two sharers that pick one scope id can flip the render node's name against each other, but the drain refuses a name the write seed does not derive (`sync/drain.rs:3179`) and `granted_scope_roots` excludes ambiguous ids (`accept.rs:428-435`). Verdict: DoS-class and pre-existing on the rename path; excluded.

**T7. Trust-violation message** (`facade.rs:11789-11793`). `hex_lower(&amp;scope_root.0)` is a node id, not key or seed material. Verdict: no secret leak.

**T8. Hygiene**. No new clock or RNG call (`scheduler.now()` is the injected seam, pre-existing). No `debug_assert!`; every `assert!` in the diff is in `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs`. No new log of secret bytes. Verdict: clean.

## Summary

The diff adds no high-confidence, exploitable vulnerability. Every new path that reads the network authenticates under the owner identity, checks regression against the durable floor, and reports a gate failure as `Rejected` rather than as availability. The only behavior an attacker in a routing position can influence is a transient bookmark shape and one redundant claim, which the next tick heals.</result>

<result>## SECURITY REVIEW COMPLETE

**Executive summary.** The three fixes keep the trust axis intact on their main paths: the on-access consult keeps `Rejected` as a trust verdict, floors move only to an owner-signed `writeEpoch` under the lease, `personal` is read after stage 2, and the render name comes only from the verified bookmark. One Medium finding: a pre-existing probe (`refuse_an_unindexed_scope`) treats every `ResolveFailure::Rejected` as "no scope here", and the new helper adds a pointer refusal as a second source of that verdict, so a replayed pointer can now let a mint proceed over a live scope root. Two Low findings on the `personal` predicate and on the walk helper's silent downgrade. Risk level: **Medium** (one fix needed before merge).

Files analyzed: 6 (5 source, 1 test). Crypto operations touched: re-point unseal + owner ECDSA verify, owner-write-blob HPKE open at a floor epoch, pairwise X25519 blinded tag, grant-set commitment verify. Issues: 1 Medium, 2 Low, 1 Info.

### Axis verdicts

**A. Rejected stays a trust verdict.** `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:1868-1874` maps `PointerConsultError::Rejected` to `ResolveFailure::Rejected`, `Ok(None)`/`Unavailable` to `Unavailable`. `gated_write_plane` (`:1821`) propagates with `?`; the sweep maps through `SweepResolveFailure::from` (`crates/engine/src/rotation/sweep.rs:153-160`, `Rejected -&gt; Rejected`). `facade.rs:11784-11797` now emits an abuse event on `Rejected` and returns `None` only after that. The link path is unchanged: `LinkReadRefusal::Repoint` and `Gate` still propagate as `Err` (`link_read.rs:205-231`), and `preview_invite_link` maps them with `?` (`facade.rs:11583`), so a forged set is never a "not joined" preview. `claim_invite_link` maps `JoinRead::Unavailable` to `None` and falls back to the bookmark shape (`facade.rs:10240-10241`) — availability, not a trust downgrade. Difference from the walk helper: `rotation.rs:1417-1422` maps `Err(_)` (including `Rejected`) to `WritePlaneDark::Unavailable` with no event. The new helper is the correct one under rule 6; the walk's downgrade is a pre-existing gap (Finding 3). **One new path is wrong**: `facade.rs:9450-9458` (Finding 1).

**B. Floors rise only to an owner-signed epoch.** `pointer_fetch.rs:118-140`: `fanout_get_classified` verifies the IPNS record under the owner-keyed pointer name; `open_repoint` -&gt; `crates/core/src/payload/pointer.rs:182-218` unseals under `pointerReadKey` with `(v, scopeId)` AAD, re-encodes the canonical object and verifies `ownerSig` under the caller's identity (`self.keys.identity` on the owner path). `write_epoch_regression` (`gate/floor.rs:520-531`) refuses `writeEpoch &lt; floor` before any write; `advance_write_epoch_on_sight` (`:578-588`) is monotonic-max and takes the `WriteEpochLease`; a leased scope returns the standing floor untouched (`:583-585`), no raise, no error. Same-device wave: the lease is taken in the publish (`rotation.rs:2089`, `:4578`) after enumeration, so a consult inside the window is dropped and the helper's open at the old floor fails to `Unavailable`; a consult before the window raises the floor and the guard at `rotation.rs:4590+` refuses a floor above the enumeration snapshot. The wave aborts; it never signs below the floor. No strand. The helper re-reads the floor after the consult (`:1876-1879`) and opens at that value, so seed and epoch are consistent.

**C. `personal` after stage 2.** `link_read.rs:227-238` runs `verify_commitment_in_force` (owner signature over the set bound to the root name, plus cut-epoch bar) before `holds_committed_blob` (`:248-253`). The predicate is the one `facts_from` calls (`received_status.rs:1154`), one definition at `:1167-1177`. An attacker-served set cannot change `personal` without failing the verify, with one caveat: the predicate checks tag presence only; it does not open the blob (`self_locate_signed`, `ledger.rs:103-108`, is a find by tag). A committed write grantee re-wraps blobs on re-seal, so it can corrupt or drop a victim's blob without touching the commitment (Finding 2). Consequences: `personal=false` for a granted person -&gt; one extra claim in the owner inbox and `received.reconcile(share)` (`facade.rs:10279-10281`) replaces the entry with the fragment's shape; `same_bookmark` (`accept.rs:182-188`) shows the fields at stake: `scope_root_name` (consult-vouched), `display_name`, `permission`, `pointer_read_key` (owner-minted, proven by the re-point open). No key downgrade. `personal=true` for a cut person needs a genuine owner set at or above the cut floor; a pre-cut replay is refused by `refuse_stale_cut_epoch` on a device that recorded the cut.

**D. Render name source.** `received_status.rs:253-256` reads `share.scope_root_name` from `open.share`, the `&amp;ReceivedShare` borrowed from the stored list (`:944-952`, `:1080-1085`). That field is written by `heal_root_name` (`accept.rs:380-384`) from `follow_held_pointers` (`received_status.rs:704-716`), whose consult verifies under `contact.identity_pk()` keyed by `share.sharer_identity_pk` (`:446-449`); by `HoldChange::Heal` (`:485`); and by `reconcile` on the claim path after `JoinRead::Live` set the root from the consult (`facade.rs:10213-10215`). Never from `candidate.envelope` or any record field. Correct.

**E. Key material.** `my_enc_secret: &amp;'a X25519Secret` (`link_read.rs:270`) is a borrow of `session.enc_subkey()`; the callee derives its own `SecretBytes` shared secret and drops it (`ledger.rs:65-73`); no caller buffer is zeroed. The helper returns `Zeroizing&lt;[u8; SECRET_LEN]&gt;` moved into `GatedScopeRoot.write_scope_seed`, the terminal owner (`rotation.rs:474-486`). Abuse text: `hex_lower(&amp;scope_root.0)` (a node id) plus a static string, wrapped in `RedactedText` (`facade.rs:4340`). Tests: `floors(&amp;SECRET)` opens a fixture store; assertions are on epochs, public keys, permissions, counts. No secret bytes in assertions.

**F. Determinism and symmetry.** Only `self.seams.scheduler.now()` (2 sites, injected seam) and `SharedEntropy(&amp;self.entropy)` (pre-existing). Every `assert!` in the diff is in `tests/owner_actions.rs`. No `debug_assert!`. `crates/core` is untouched (0 hunks); no encode path changes; symmetry unaffected.

### Findings

**[MEDIUM] A pointer refusal now reads as "no scope here" on the mint probe.**
Location: `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:9450-9458` with the new source at `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:1868-1874`.

```rust
Ok(_) =&gt; Err(EngineError::UnsupportedTarget { check }),
Err(ResolveFailure::Rejected) =&gt; Ok(()),
```

`refuse_an_unindexed_scope` runs `resolve_anchored` at the derived name and treats `Rejected` as the gate saying no live root stands there. Before the diff, a gate-passing root whose owner-write-blob did not open at the floor was `Unavailable` (retry). Now the helper consults the pointer, and a replayed re-point below this device's write floor is `Rejected`, which the probe maps to `Ok(())`: the mint proceeds on a node where a live scope root stands, and no abuse event fires on this path. Preconditions are narrow (no read-epoch floor on the node, a write-epoch floor above the replayed epoch, an owb sealed higher), and the trigger is a server replay of a genuine older pointer record. Impact: the owner's device may publish a second root over its own live scope root and lose the standing grant set.
Recommendation: the probe needs only the gate verdict. Read through `resealable_root`/`gated_root` and stop before the write plane, or add a distinct `ResolveFailure` variant (or a `PointerConsultArm::Refused` check inside `write_seed_on_access`) so a consult refusal cannot satisfy this arm. Also emit the abuse event inside `write_seed_on_access` so every caller reports a refused re-point, not only `scope_sharing`.

**[LOW] `personal` does not open the blob.**
Location: `crates/engine/src/grants/received_status.rs:1167-1177`, used at `link_read.rs:248`.
A committed write grantee can corrupt a victim's blob during a re-seal without touching the owner-signed set. The victim then previews as `joined` and `claim_invite_link` posts nothing (`facade.rs:10243-10245`), while the pass cannot open the personal blob. Dropping the blob is the better-handled case (`personal=false`, the join re-claims). Impact: one grantee stalled until the owner re-seals; no key exposure.
Recommendation: on the join/preview path use the open-based predicate `personal_blob_opens` (`received_status.rs:1098-1124`) or treat an unopenable blob as `personal=false`. Keep `facts_from` as is.

**[LOW] The walk helper downgrades a refused re-point to `Unavailable` without an event.** Pre-existing, outside the diff.
Location: `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:1417-1422`. `Err(_)` covers `Rejected`. Reachability is low (a rolled-back epoch cannot fire with no floor; only a bad owner signature remains), but rule 6 asks for an attributable event. Recommendation: split the arm and call `emit_trust_violation`.

**[INFO] Duplicate claim on a false-negative `personal`.** `facade.rs:10247-10267` posts a second claim for an already-granted identity. Confirm the owner-side convert is idempotent for an identity that already holds a committed tag (Test 3 below).

Axes B, D, E, F: no findings.

### Test suggestions

1. `an_on_access_pointer_consult_with_a_bad_owner_signature_is_a_trust_violation` — serve a re-point at the moved root, sealed under the right `pointerReadKey`, `ownerSig` by another secp256k1 key. Assert `sharing().state == None`, one abuse event, `try_mint_link_at -&gt; Err(TrustViolation)`, floor unchanged.
2. `a_stale_root_copy_below_the_vouched_write_epoch_stays_unreachable` — floor at N, pointer at N+1, endpoint serves the root record with the owb sealed at N. Assert `sharing().state == None`, zero abuse events, floor raised to N+1; then serve the fresh record and assert the read succeeds.
3. `a_committed_tag_without_a_blob_previews_as_not_joined_and_a_second_convert_is_idempotent` — commit the account's tag, omit its blob. Assert `preview.joined == false`, the join posts one claim, `convert` on the already-granted identity leaves one commitment entry.
4. `a_forged_set_on_the_preview_path_is_a_trust_violation_not_a_preview` — replace `commitment_sig` at the vouched root with a signature by another key. Assert `preview_invite_link -&gt; Err(TrustViolation)`, the received-share store is byte-identical, no floor raised (`NoPersistFloorStore`).
5. `an_unindexed_scope_probe_does_not_read_a_refused_pointer_as_no_scope` — a promoted root at the derived name with the owb sealed above this device's write floor, a replayed pointer below the floor, no read-epoch floor. Assert the mint fails with `TrustViolation`, one abuse event, no new root record published.
6. `a_consult_during_a_leased_publish_window_does_not_raise_the_floor` — hold `acquire_write_epoch_lease` for the scope, run the sharing read against a pointer one epoch above the floor. Assert the floor is unchanged and `state == None` with zero abuse events; drop the lease, re-run, assert the floor rises and the read succeeds.
